He all but imperceptibly shook his head in negation. “And what did you have in the paper bag you carried when you came here and from which you tore that scrap of paper upon which you wrote the misleading note?”

“This gun.”

“You carried the gun in a paper bag?”

“I was smart, was I not? Who would think that in a cheap paper bag there was a gun?”

“Not even a disciple of the fourth dimension.” Hansen was aiming now at her wrist. It was absurd—he faintly shook his head again. No—no! “How did it happen that Mr. Endicott had his overcoat on but you had his hat?”

===Chinese===
“告诉我，”他说，“你在那箱子里面究竟是怎么喘得上气的。”

“它后面是破的。”问得这样漫不经心，倒把她惊得脱口作答。

“那你自己的后背，也差不多要断了吧。”汉森这个蠢货，莫非到底要用枪托把玻璃砸碎？汉森正目不转睛地盯着他，求他的主意。他几乎觉察不出地轻轻摇了摇头，示意不可。“那你来这儿时手里拎的那只纸袋里头，装的到底是什么？你就是从那只纸袋上，撕下了那一小片纸、在上面写下了那封引开我们注意的字条的。”

“这把枪。”

“你把枪装在纸袋里拎着？”

“我挺聪明，是不是？谁会想到一只蹩脚的纸袋里头会有一把枪？”

“便是第四维度的门徒也想不到。”汉森这会儿正瞄准她的手腕。这太荒唐——他又微微摇了摇头。不——不行！“怎么会弄成恩迪科特先生穿着大衣、帽子却在您手里的呢？”

===Original===
“I wear it for a better disguise. I have the dust on my face—there is the hat—it fits well over my cloche. The effect is astonishing.”

“I see, and so when Endicott came back into the room to get it he couldn’t find it and thought he must have left it in the cupboard?”

“Yes—yes—you are a smart man, too.”

“And you entered the house with a duplicate key which you had had made from one of Endicott’s?”

“Dear heaven, yes—how else?”

===Chinese===
“我戴上它，好乔装得更像样些。我脸上抹了灰——再加上这顶帽子——它正好能罩在我的钟形帽外面。这效果惊人得很。”

“我明白了，所以恩迪科特回到房里来取帽子的时候找不着它，便以为自己一定是把它落在衣橱里了？”

“对——对——你也是个精明人。”

“而您进这宅子时，用的是一把照着恩迪科特的钥匙配出来的备份钥匙？”

“老天，对——不然还能怎样？”

===Original===
It did not please her that her climax should come at a commonplace moment, when inconsequential questions were being asked and equally inconsequential answers being given. It was not bravura: the man was genuinely unafraid. And she wanted him to be afraid. One shouldn’t just dribble from the world: there should be a blaze, a scene.

Then Hansen rapped, quite gently, upon the panes.

Inspiration? Genius? Perhaps. Lieutenant Valcour’s Gallic blood swept back to the nation of its source and he could have kissed that dear, that brilliant Hansen upon both of his ruddy, his intelligent, his Nordic cheeks.

She whirled as if something had flicked her. Blue serge—brass buttons—a glinting shield. She pulled the trigger.

But the muzzle of the gun was in her mouth.

===Chinese===
她的高潮竟要降临在这样一个平淡无奇的时刻——人家问的是些不相干的问题，回的也是些不相干的答话——这可不合她的心意。这并非逞强作势：这个男人是当真不害怕。而她正要他害怕。一个人不该就这么从世上悄无声息地溜走：总该有一团烈焰、一场好戏。

就在这时，汉森在窗玻璃上，极轻地叩了叩。

灵机一动？天才？也许吧。瓦尔考中尉体内那股高卢人的血液，霎时间奔回了它的故土，他简直想在那亲爱的、那绝顶聪明的汉森的两颊——那红润的、聪敏的、北欧人的两颊——各亲上一口。

她猛地旋过身去，像是被什么东西弹了一下。蓝色哔叽——铜纽扣——一面闪亮的徽章。她扣动了扳机。

可那把枪的枪口，正衔在她自己嘴里。

## XXXI 8:37 p.m.—Five Years Later / 三十一 晚八时三十七分——五年之后

===Original===
Mrs. Hollander thought for a moment of simply dialling the operator and saying, “I want a policeman.”

It was what the printed notices in the telephone directory urged one to do in case of an emergency. But it wasn’t an emergency exactly, nor—still exactly—was it a policeman she wanted. She wanted a detective, or an inspector, or something; a man to whom she could explain her worry about Thomas, and who could do something about it if he agreed with her that Thomas was in danger.

Mrs. Hollander wanted most of all a man like Lieutenant Valcour, who had so ably handled that wretched affair five years ago when she had been married to Herbert and Herbert had been shot. She wondered whether Lieutenant Valcour was still on the force, and decided to find out. She dialled Spring 3100. She grew nervous while waiting.

===Chinese===
霍兰德夫人（Mrs. Hollander）有那么一刻想干脆拨给接线员，说一句：“我要找警察。”

电话簿上的告示明明白白地写着，遇到紧急情况就该这么做。可这事说到底算不得紧急情况；而她想找的，也——确切地说——并不只是一个警察。她想要的是一名侦探，或是一位探长，或是诸如此类的什么人；一个能让她把对托马斯（Thomas）的担忧说给他听的人，一个倘若也认同托马斯身陷险境、便能想办法替她处置的人。

霍兰德夫人最想要的，还是一个像瓦尔考中尉（Lieutenant Valcour）那样的人——五年前，她尚与赫伯特（Herbert）做夫妻、赫伯特中弹身亡的那桩惨案，便是经他这般干练地了结的。她不知道瓦尔考中尉是否还在警界任职，决意要打听个明白。她拨了春局（Spring）3100 号分机。等待接通的当儿，她紧张起来。

===Original===
“This is Mrs. Thomas Hollander speaking,” she said, when the same type of impersonal, efficient voice answered her as had been the one five years before. “I am phoning to inquire whether a Lieutenant Valcour is still connected with the police force.… I beg your pardon? Oh.” She gave the address of her apartment house on Park Avenue.

“This is Mrs. Thomas Hollander speaking,” she began again upon a second voice saying, “Hello!” “and I am trying to get in touch with a Lieutenant Valcour who—I beg your pardon?… You *are* Lieutenant Valcour—Inspector, is it? But how perfectly efficient! I am worried, Inspector, about Mr. Hollander, and I wonder whether it would be possible for you to come up and talk it over with me.… No, he hasn’t disappeared. I know exactly where he has gone, but I have reason to believe that something might happen to him.… Yes, I am the Mrs. Hollander who was formerly Mrs. Herbert Endicott.… Yes, that dreadful affair.… Oh, you will? Thank you so much.”

===Chinese===
“我是托马斯·霍兰德夫人，”当一个不带任何情绪、干练利落的声音应答时——一如五年前那一个——她开口道。“我打这通电话，是想打听瓦尔考中尉是否仍在警界任职。……您说什么？哦。”她报出了自己在派克大道（Park Avenue）那幢公寓楼的地址。

“我是托马斯·霍兰德夫人，”等另一个声音说了声“喂！”她又重新开腔，“我想联系瓦尔考中尉，他——您说什么？……您*就是*瓦尔考中尉——是探长（Inspector）了？这效率可真够绝的！探长，我很为霍兰德先生担心，不知您能不能上来一趟，与我好好谈一谈。……不，他并没有失踪。我清楚地知道他去了哪里，但我有理由相信，他可能会出什么事。……是的，我就是从前那位赫伯特·恩迪科特夫人，如今的霍兰德夫人。……对，那桩骇人的旧案。……哦，您肯来？太感谢您了。”

===Original===
Inspector Valcour smiled a curiously satisfied little smile all to himself as he sat in a department limousine, chauffeured by a department driver, and sped smoothly north along Lafayette Street on the way to Mrs. Hollander’s address on Park Avenue.

And he thought of many things.

He thought of Marge Myles and of Herbert Endicott, who were dead; and of Madame Velasquez who, too, had died.

He thought of Mrs. Siddons, returned to her native New England hills, sinking her body and her being into their granite harshnesses and drawing amazing sustenance from them, as a flower will that grows in the imperceptible fissure of some solid rock.

He thought of Roberts whom he had never seen again and of whom he had never again heard, after the violation of the Sullivan Law had been charged against her, and her sentence suspended. She had gone back to England, probably, to lapse into a proper background for her neurotic broodings.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考探长坐在局里的豪华轿车里，由局里的司机开着车，沿拉斐特街（Lafayette Street）一路顺畅地向北飞驰，朝霍兰德夫人派克大道的寓所而去，他独自露出一抹古怪地心满意足的微笑。

他想起了许多事。

他想起了玛吉·迈尔斯（Marge Myles）与赫伯特·恩迪科特，两人都已不在人世；也想起了同样业已辞世的委拉斯开兹夫人（Madame Velasquez）。

他想起了西登斯太太（Mrs. Siddons），她回到了新英格兰（New England）故乡的群山之中，把肉身与整个心性都沉入那花岗岩般的粗砺里，却又从中汲取着惊人的滋养，宛如一朵花长在某块坚硬岩石那不易察觉的裂缝之中。

他想起了罗伯茨（Roberts）——自从她因违反萨利文法（Sullivan Law）被起诉、随后获判缓刑以来，他便再未见过她，也再未曾听到她的任何音讯。她多半回英国去了，让自己重新隐没进一片与她那神经质的郁结相称的背景里。

===Original===
And that partner of Hollander’s—the Southernistic Mr. Smith. He had faded entirely, never to return; nor was the fact of any consequence at all. He had been at best a side issue too unimportant for further bother.

But most of all he thought of Mrs. Endicott, who was now Mrs. Hollander.

The annals of history and the annals of crime were fringed with women just like her: beautiful, astonishing women, who revolved with their uncertainties like satellites about the world of normal beings, trailing their baleful, striking brilliance like an impalpable poisonous gas across the surface of every person whom they plucked and tortured within the intricate enigma of their hearts. The law never could touch her—nor could a person, either. She would escape. She would always escape, with the subtlety of mercury slipping between impotent fingers.

For she *had* escaped.

===Chinese===
还有霍兰德那位合伙人——那位南派做派的史密斯先生（Mr. Smith）。此人早已彻底淡出，再没露过面；而此事也全然无关紧要。他至多不过是个太无关痛痒的旁支，犯不上再为他费神。

可他想得最多的，还是恩迪科特夫人——如今已是霍兰德夫人的这位。

史册与犯罪案卷的边缘，缀满了与她同类的女人：那些美丽而惊人的女子，带着她们各自的种种无常，如卫星一般绕着常人的世界旋转，又把那致命的、夺目的光华，像一缕无从捉摸的毒气，拖过每一个被她们在自家心头那道繁复谜团中把玩、折磨过的人的体表。法律永远奈何她不得——任何一个人，也同样奈何她不得。她会逃脱。她总会逃脱，带着水银一般从无力的指缝间悄然溜走的那种诡巧。

因为她*确已*逃脱了。

===Original===
There wasn’t any doubt in his mind about that. She had been the focal point five years ago in that Endicott case, no matter what the law or men might say. Her forgery of that postscript had had a deeper, a more deliberate intention than the mere breaking up of any affair between her husband and Marge Myles: it was to have been a breaking up of all of his affairs. Of him.

She was the true murderer of her husband, and not Marge Myles. She had simply spread the powder train to a suitably lethal explosive and had then applied the match. The movements of the others had been nothing more than gyrations performed by stringed puppets. And she had held the strings. Some of her puppets had died, committed suicide, and been killed. And it didn’t matter in the least. The world was ageless, she herself was ageless, and plenty of puppets grew perennially every spring.

Inspector Valcour wondered, as he descended to the curb and prepared to enter the lift to her apartment, whether Thomas had become a puppet, too.

===Chinese===
这一点在他心里，没有任何疑问。五年前那桩恩迪科特案里，她始终是那一切的焦点所在，任凭法律、任凭旁人怎样去说。她伪造的那段附笔，其用意比之仅仅拆散丈夫与玛吉·迈尔斯之间的一桩私情，要更深一层、更蓄谋已久：那原是要拆散他所有的私情——拆散他这个人。

她才是她丈夫真正的凶手，而不是玛吉·迈尔斯。她不过是把一根引火线铺到了一份足以致命的炸药旁，然后划着了那根火柴。其余众人的一举一动，不过是些被人牵线的木偶在那里打转。而那些线，就握在她手里。她那些木偶，有的死了，有的自杀了，有的被人所杀。这都丝毫不打紧。这世界万古常新，她自己也万古常新，而每年春天，总会有的是新的木偶一茬茬冒出来。

瓦尔考探长下车走到路边，准备乘电梯上她的寓所，他心里琢磨着：托马斯，是不是也成了一个木偶。

## I 8:37 p.m.—Spring 3100 / 一 晚八时三十七分——3100号分机

===Original===
Mrs. Endicott thought for a moment of simply dialling the operator and saying, “I want a policeman.”

It was what the printed notices in the telephone directory urged one to do in case of an emergency. But it wasn’t an emergency exactly, nor—still exactly—was it a policeman she wanted. She wanted a detective, or an inspector, or something; a man to whom she could explain her worry about Herbert, and who could do something about it if he agreed with her that Herbert was in danger.

Mrs. Endicott had never had any personal contact with the police. Whenever she thought about it at all she thought of the force as an efficient piece of machinery, the active parts of which one observed daily from one’s motor as healthy and generally good-looking young men who controlled traffic. She knew that there was a patrolman whose beat carried him past their door. Upon thinking suddenly about it she realized that she had only seen this man twice or three times at most during the past year. She knew that Herbert always left a ten-dollar gold piece to be given him by one of the maids at Christmas, and a check for twenty dollars as a subscription to some enterprise vaguely designated as the “fund.”

===Chinese===
恩迪科特夫人（Mrs. Endicott）有那么一刻想干脆拨给接线员，说一句：“我要找警察。”

电话簿上的告示明明白白地写着，遇到紧急情况就该这么做。可这事说到底算不得紧急情况；而她想找的，也——确切地说——并不只是一个警察。她想要的是一名侦探，或是一位探长，或是诸如此类的什么人；一个能让她把对赫伯特（Herbert）的担忧说给他听的人，一个倘若也认同赫伯特身陷险境、便能想办法替她处置的人。

恩迪科特夫人此前与警察从未有过任何私人往来。每逢她想起这回事，总把整支警力看作一具运转高效的机器，那些看得见的活动部件，便是她每日坐在汽车里瞧见的、那些身体结实、多半也生得英俊、指挥交通的年轻人。她知道有一名巡警，巡逻路线会经过她家门口。可猛然一琢磨，她才意识到过去这一整年里，自己至多也只见过这人两三回。她知道赫伯特总要在圣诞节留下一枚十元金币，差一个女佣转交给他，另外再开一张二十元的支票，充作某项名目含糊、只笼统叫做“基金”的事业的捐款。

===Original===
She wondered momentarily whether the police characters she had seen in various plays, while at the theatre with Herbert, were true to life. Most of the characters had been brutal, in spite of a pleasant tenderheartedness reluctantly betrayed toward the final curtain, and just at present she wanted quiet, competent understanding—not brutality.

It occurred to her that a private investigator might be better, but she was uncertain as to the extent of their official powers. She decided to rely on the police, because the police could do something if they agreed with her that something ought to be done.

Mrs. Endicott looked up the telephone number of police headquarters and dialled Spring 3100. She grew nervous while waiting.

===Chinese===
她一时也纳闷，自己同赫伯特一道看戏时，在舞台上见过的那些警察角色，到底合不合实情。那些角色多半粗蛮暴戾，纵然到了终幕之前，也总会不大情愿地流露出几分讨人喜欢的柔肠。可眼下她要的，是沉稳而胜任的理解——而不是粗蛮。

她也曾想过，说不定请一位私家侦探更合适，但她拿不准这些人究竟有多大的官方职权。她还是决定倚仗警察，因为只要警察也认同该有所举措，他们便能当真有所举措。

恩迪科特夫人查到了警察总部的电话号码，拨了春局（Spring）3100号分机。等待接通的当儿，她紧张起来。

===Original===
“This is Mrs. Herbert Endicott speaking,” she said, when an undeniably masculine voice answered. It was an impersonal, efficient voice with no overtones about it. “Will you please connect me with your detective department? … I beg your pardon? Oh.” She gave the number of her house on East Sixty-third Street between Fifth and Madison avenues.

“This is Mrs. Herbert Endicott speaking,” she began again, upon a second voice’s saying, “Hello,” “and I am worried about Mr. Endicott. I wonder whether you could send someone up to talk it over with me. … No, he hasn’t disappeared. I know exactly where he has gone, but I have reason to believe that something might happen to him. … Yes, it’s the Mr. Endicott who has been in the papers recently in connection with Wall Street. … Around in a few minutes? But I thought police headquarters were down on Centre Street. … They transferred the call to the precinct station? Really. … Oh, thank you.”

Mrs. Endicott replaced the receiver on its hook. She felt distinctly impressed at the efficiency with which her request had been so instantly transferred to the place where it could be handled competently and with dispatch.

===Chinese===
“我是赫伯特·恩迪科特夫人，”待一个无可争辩的男声应答时，她开口道。那声音不带任何情绪色彩，干练利落，没有一丝弦外之音。“麻烦您替我接到你们的侦探部门好吗？……您说什么？哦。”她报出了自家房子的所在：东六十三街，第五大道与麦迪逊大道之间。

“我是赫伯特·恩迪科特夫人，”等到另一个声音说了声“喂”，她又重新开腔，“我很为恩迪科特先生担心。不知您能不能派个人过来，跟我谈一谈。……不，他并没有失踪。我清楚地知道他去了哪里，但我有理由相信，他可能会出什么事。……是的，就是近来因华尔街的事上了报的那位恩迪科特先生。……几分钟之内就到？可我以为警察总部在下城中央街呢。……他们把电话转到了辖区分局？原来如此。……哦，多谢。”

恩迪科特夫人把听筒搁回挂钩上。她的请求竟能如此迅捷地被转到那个能胜任而利落地处置它的地方，这等效率令她印象格外鲜明。

===Original===
The living room where she had been telephoning was on the second floor of the house. She left it and went to her dressing room, which was toward the rear of a corridor on the same floor. She gave her appearance a preoccupied inspection before a pier glass. The soft and uneven lines of the jade chiffon of her dress would offer a satisfactory mask, she felt, for the nervous tenseness of her body. She renewed the red on her upper lip where she had been biting it. She returned to the living room, lighted a cigarette, and picked up a novel which she did not read.

She smoked three cigarettes.

Her sense of aloneness became stifling. The conceit grew upon her nervous condition that she had changed places with the furniture. She had become inanimate and the furniture endowed with attributes of life, as if her being were under the influence of some dispassionate regard by something that had no eyes with which to see. It was nonsense—nonsense. She never should have listened—at least not attentively—to that wretched old woman. She could very well just have given the appearance … one had to be polite …

===Chinese===
她方才打电话的起居室在房子的二楼。她离开那里，走向自己的梳妆室，那间屋子在同一层一条走廊靠后的位置。她在穿衣镜前心不在焉地打量了一下自己的仪容。她那条翠绿色雪纺裙柔和而不规整的褶纹，她觉得，足以为她身体里那股紧绷的神经质提供一个令人满意的伪装。她重新补了上唇被她咬过处的那点胭脂。她回到起居室，点起一支烟，又拿起一本小说，却并没有看进去。

她一连抽了三支烟。

那种孑然一身的孤寂感压得她透不过气来。在她那焦灼的神经状态下，一种异想渐渐攫住了她：觉得自己与家具互换了位置。她自己变成了没有生气的东西，而家具反倒被赋予了生命的属性，仿佛她的整个存在，正被一种全无情绪的目光审视着，而那审视之物甚至没有眼睛可以视物。这是胡思乱想——胡思乱想。她当初就不该听信——至少不该那么当真地听信——那个可恶老太婆的话。她本可以只做做样子……人总得讲点礼数……

===Original===
Mrs. Endicott moved restlessly to one of the draped windows and stared down on the silent street. About her stretched the city of New York, and yet her environment could not have been quieter in some cabin in the woods. Not as quiet. Her memory swerved to that hellish week with Herbert in the forests outside of Copenhagen … what on earth *was* the name of that little watering place … Trollhättan? … No, that was in Sweden. Names never mattered. She looked up for a while at a slender slice of night sky horizoned by cornices across the street. It was heavy with stars that held her as if they were so many magic mediums arranged in heaven for the express purpose of granting her earthbound wishes. Wishes? She shrugged. She released the drapes, and they settled into place.

A maid opened the living-room door and came in.

“A lieutenant from the precinct station, madam.”

“All right, Jane. Ask him to come up here. Did he give his name?”

“Lieutenant Valcour, madam, I think he said.”

“Try and be more careful in the future about getting names.”

“Yes, madam.”

===Chinese===
恩迪科特夫人焦躁地踱到一扇垂着帷幔的窗前，俯瞰那条静悄悄的街道。她身周铺展着整个纽约城，可她所处的环境，竟比林中某座小木屋还要安静。不，没有那么安静。她的思绪猛地一转，回到了同赫伯特在哥本哈根城外森林里度过的那个炼狱般的星期……那个小小温泉镇到底叫什么名字来着……特罗尔海坦（Trollhättan）？……不对，那是在瑞典。地名从来都无关紧要。她抬眼望了一会儿那一线夜空，它被街对面楼宇的飞檐框成了窄窄一道。天幕上缀满繁星，把她攫住不放，仿佛那些星星全是在天上特意布下的灵媒，专为她这困于尘世的凡人遂其所愿。所愿？她耸了耸肩。她松开帷幔，帷幔垂落归位。

一名女佣推开起居室的门，走了进来。

“辖区分局来了一位中尉，夫人。”

“好的，简（Jane）。请他上楼来吧。他报名字了吗？”

“瓦尔考中尉（Lieutenant Valcour），夫人，我想他是这么说的。”

“往后打听人名，多留点神。”

“是，夫人。”

===Original===
Mrs. Endicott lighted another cigarette. Her sense of having done the proper thing began to desert her in a rush. The police had a habit of finding things out—unexpected things, irrelevant to any matter on hand. She was sure of it, and wondered on what she based the knowledge: books, hearsay. She would have to be careful, but after all, a person with intelligence—He was standing in the doorway.

“My maid,” she said, “wasn’t sure of your name. Is it Valcour?” She noticed with a sense of relief that he was not in uniform and that he had left his hat and overcoat downstairs. Mrs. Endicott had an aversion to discussing things which fringed on possible intimacies with people who were hatted and coated. He was a mild elderly man with features that were homely but not undistinguished, well dressed in tweed, and not smoking a cigar. He affected her with a quieting sense of reassurance.

===Chinese===
恩迪科特夫人又点燃了一支烟。她那股“自己做得对”的笃定，一下子离她而去。警察总有本事把事情查出来——查出些意想不到的事、与手头这桩案子毫不相干的事。对此她深信不疑，却又纳闷自己这份认知究竟从何而来：书本，还是道听途说。她得当心些，可话说回来，一个有头脑的人……他就站在门口。

“我家的女佣，”她说，“没听清您的名字。是瓦尔考吗？”她略感宽慰地注意到，他并未穿制服，帽子和大衣也已留在了楼下。恩迪科特夫人向来反感与那些衣帽齐整的人谈论任何多少触及私隐的话题。他是个温和的长者，五官谈不上英俊却并非全无气度，一身花呢装束颇为考究，嘴里也没有叼着雪茄。他给她带来一种令人安定的踏实感。

===Original===
“Valcour is correct, Mrs. Endicott. I happened to be leaving for home when your call was put in, so I stopped in personally instead of sending a detective as you suggested.”

The faint trace of cultured precision in his speech made her suspect foreign origin. She was sensitive to voices, and while not exactly collecting them, they almost amounted with her to a hobby. They were an essential part in the attraction she felt toward certain people, and it would have been within the bounds of possibility for her to have fallen in love with a voice.

“You are of French origin, Lieutenant?”

“French-Canadian, Mrs. Endicott. I became naturalized twenty years ago.”

She offered her hand. They sat down. Now that he was here she felt that the necessity for hurry had vanished; his air of official protection had erased it. She wondered how it would be best to begin: just where to plunge into the foggy mass that composed her worry.

===Chinese===
“瓦尔考没错，恩迪科特夫人。您这通电话打进来的时候，我正巧要动身回家，所以便亲自顺道过来一趟，而没有照您说的另派一名侦探。”

他谈吐之间那一丝若有若无的、教养良好的精确，让她疑心他原是外邦人。她对嗓音格外敏感，虽说算不上专门搜罗，可各种声音于她近乎一种癖好。声音乃是她之所以为某些人所吸引的要件之一，要说她会单单爱上一个声音，也并非全无可能。

“您是法国血统吗，中尉？”

“法裔加拿大人，恩迪科特夫人。二十年前我已经入籍了。”

她伸出手去。两人落座。既然他已经在此，她反倒觉得那种急迫之感荡然无存；他那副公务在身、护佑来人的气度，把那股急切抹去了。她暗自琢磨该怎样开篇才妥当：究竟从哪里下手，才能扎进那团盘成一气的、迷雾般的忧虑之中。

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour accepted a cigarette and lighted it. He was agreeably impressed with Mrs. Endicott and with the room. Both were unusual, and the competent foundation in culture he had acquired at McGill University in his youth enabled him to place them at a proper evaluation. The furniture was low set in design and severely simple, the general effect one of spaciousness and repose oddly marred by a muted undernote of harshness. It was not bizarre. He suspected it, correctly, of being modernistic. Mrs. Endicott herself had the startlingly clear perfection of features one occasionally finds in blondes. He decided that her age centred on twenty-five. Beneath her authentic beauty—her face seemed planed in pale tones of pink ice—there would be a definite substrata of metal. He noted that the six cigarette butts crushed in the vermilion lacquered tray on a small table beside her chair had not been smoked beyond a few puffs each. A clock standing on the broad-shelved mantel of the fireplace struck nine.

“My husband,” Mrs. Endicott said abruptly, “has been gone now exactly two hours.”

Lieutenant Valcour smiled amiably and settled himself a little less formally in his chair. His manner presented itself to her as a freshly sponged slate upon which she could trace any markings that she might choose.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉接过一支烟，点上。他对恩迪科特夫人和这间屋子都颇有好感。两者都非同寻常，而年轻时在麦吉尔大学（McGill University）打下的那点扎实的文化功底，使他能够恰如其分地估量它们的分量。家具造型低矮、线条极简，整体效果是一种疏朗恬静之感，却又古怪地被一丝压低了声调的生硬所损伤。这并不算怪诞。他疑心——而且猜得不错——这是现代派的路数。恩迪科特夫人本人，则有着那种金发女郎身上偶尔可见的、惊人地澄澈完美的五官。他判定她的年纪当在二十五岁上下。在她那份真实的美貌之下——她的脸仿佛是用粉色冰块那般淡雅的色调刨削出来的——必有一层确凿的金属质地。他留意到，她座椅旁小几上那只朱漆烟碟里摁灭的六个烟头，每一个都不过只抽了几口。壁炉那宽阔炉台上摆着的一座钟敲响了九点。

“我丈夫，”恩迪科特夫人蓦地开口，“到现在已经离开整整两个小时了。”

瓦尔考中尉和蔼地笑了笑，在椅子里略略放松了些坐姿，不再那么端着。他的神态在她看来，好似一块刚刚擦拭干净的黑板，她可以在上面随意描下自己中意的任何字迹。

===Original===
“He left here at seven o’clock this evening,” Mrs. Endicott said, “to go to the apartment of a woman with whom he thinks he is in love. Her name is Marge Myles, and her apartment is on the Drive.”

Lieutenant Valcour’s smile seemed to offer both consolation and an apology.

“I’m afraid there isn’t very much we can do for you,” he said. “It’s always private inquiry agents who handle work of that—well, of that rather delicate character.”

“No—I haven’t made myself plain.” Mrs. Endicott’s indeterminate thoughts began to crystallize. “I’m not looking for evidence to secure a divorce. This woman is nothing of any permanence, but I’m afraid of her—of what she might do to Herbert.” Then she added, as if the simple statement in itself would insure his comprehension, “You see, I’ve seen her.”

===Chinese===
“他今晚七点钟离开这里，”恩迪科特夫人说，“去了一个他自以为爱上的女人的寓所。她叫玛吉·迈尔斯（Marge Myles），寓所就在滨河大道（the Drive）上。”

瓦尔考中尉的笑容，似乎既含着宽慰，又带着几分歉意。

“恐怕这类事我们帮不上您太多忙，”他说，“干这种——嗯，这种颇为微妙的活计的，向来都是私家调查员。”

“不——是我没说清楚。”恩迪科特夫人原本游移不定的思绪，开始渐渐凝结成形。“我不是在找什么可以据此离婚的证据。这女人并非什么长久之人，但我怕她——怕她会对赫伯特下什么手。”接着她又补了一句，仿佛单凭这句话本身就能担保他听懂似的，“要知道，我见过她。”

===Original===
“With him?”

“Yes. They were lunching at the St. Regis. Herbert always was a fool about those things. She’s foreign-looking—the Latin type.” Mrs. Endicott felt the need for being meticulously explicit. “Her eyes are like the black holes you see in portraits of Spanish women. They’re the entire face; everything else blurs into a nonessential whiteness. This woman’s eyes are like that—like weapons. I know she’s the sort who would kill if she got stirred up over something—got jealous or something. People do get jealous enough to kill,” she ended.

“Frequently.” Lieutenant Valcour stored away in his memory the broken nail on the little finger of Mrs. Endicott’s left hand. The uniform perfection of detail in the rest of her appearance made it stand out jarringly. “This is all most unfortunate,” he said sympathetically, “but I still doubt whether there is anything we could do. If there were only something definite—say a threat, for example—we’d be very glad to investigate it and to offer Mr. Endicott suitable protection.”

===Chinese===
“和他一起？”

“是的。他们在瑞吉酒店（St. Regis）吃午餐。赫伯特在这种事上一向是个傻瓜。她一副异国模样——拉丁那一型的。”恩迪科特夫人觉得有把这话说得一清二楚的必要。“她的眼睛，就像你在西班牙女人画像上看到的那些黑洞洞。那双眼睛便是整张脸的全部；其余的一切都模糊成一团无关紧要的苍白。这女人的眼睛就是那样——像武器。我知道她是那种一旦为某件事动了肝火——比如起了醋意什么的——就会杀人的角色。人确实会嫉妒到杀人的地步。”她末了说。

“屡见不鲜。”瓦尔考中尉把恩迪科特夫人左手小指上那片断裂的指甲记进了脑中。她浑身上下其余每一处细节都精致如一，倒衬得那一点格外扎眼。“这事着实太不幸了，”他同情地说，“可我还是怀疑我们能否帮得上什么忙。哪怕有一桩实在的东西——比方说一句威胁——我们也乐意着手调查，并给恩迪科特先生提供相应的保护。”

===Original===
Mrs. Endicott stood up. The abruptness of the movement spread the folds of chiffon that streamed from a bow on her left shoulder, and Lieutenant Valcour’s deceptively indifferent eyes lingered on bruise marks that showed blue smears upon white skin before the chiffon fell back into place.

“Would you come with me to my husband’s room?” Mrs. Endicott said.

“Certainly.”

“There’s something there I’d like to show you—to ask you what you think about it.”

===Chinese===
恩迪科特夫人站起身来。这动作来得猛，从她左肩一个蝴蝶结上披垂下来的雪纺褶子随之扬开，瓦尔考中尉那双看似漫不经心的眼睛，在雪纺重新落回原位之前，停在了白皙皮肤上那几处泛着青蓝的淤痕上。

“您能随我到我丈夫房里去一趟吗？”恩迪科特夫人说。

“当然。”

“那里有样东西我想让您看一看——想问问您作何想法。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour followed Mrs. Endicott along the corridor that led past her dressing room. A door beyond this opened into her bedroom, and directly across the corridor from it was the door to Endicott’s room. The blank end of the corridor served as a wall for the bathroom, which connected the two bedrooms and turned them into a suite which ran the width of the rear of the house.

Lieutenant Valcour sensed a difference in the furnishings of Endicott’s bedroom that set it at sharp variance with the other parts of the house that he had seen. It was done in heavy mahoganies that were antiquated rather than antique, and methodically centred in each panel of its gray-toned walls was a print of some painting by Maxfield Parrish. After a comprehensive glance around he felt as if he had already met Endicott. He had at least evolved a fairly accurate portrait of the man’s sensibilities, if not of his physique. He thought that Endicott would be difficult: a clearly divided neighbouring of the physical and the ideal, assuredly conscious of the fitness of things—which would be responsible for his acquiescence in the tone of the rest of the house—but dominated by an inner stubbornness which faced ridicule in the maintaining of his private room at the level he had accepted as a standard years before.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉跟着恩迪科特夫人沿着走廊走去，经过她的梳妆室。梳妆室再往里的一扇门通向她的卧室，而走廊正对面那扇门，便是通往恩迪科特房间的入口。走廊那头一堵无门的端墙，恰好充作了浴室的一面墙，这浴室把两间卧室连通起来，合成一整组套间，横贯整幢房子的后半部。

瓦尔考中尉察觉到，恩迪科特卧室的陈设与他所见过的这房子其他地方迥然有别。房间里用的是厚重的红木家具，与其说是古董，不如说是老旧，而在灰色调墙壁的每一块嵌板正中，都中规中矩地挂着一幅马克斯菲尔德·帕里什（Maxfield Parrish）画作的印刷品。他把四下打量了个周全之后，竟觉得自己已经同恩迪科特打过照面了。纵然描不出对方的体貌，他至少已颇为准确地勾勒出了这人审美趣味的轮廓。他想，恩迪科特会是个难缠的人：肉体与理想在此泾渭分明地比邻而居，他对何谓得体之事了然于胸——正因如此，他才肯迁就这房子其余部分的格调——可同时又受制于一种内心的执拗，宁可顶着旁人的讪笑，也要把自己这间私室维持在他多年前便已认作标准的那个水平上。

===Original===
“That is his desk.”

Mrs. Endicott indicated a flat-topped desk which was placed before one of the rear windows. A lemon-jacketed book with crumpled pages was lying on it as if it had been slammed there. Near the book was a scrap of paper. Lieutenant Valcour leaned down and stared at the paper without picking it up. On it was printed in pencil:

He looked at Mrs. Endicott. She was evidently waiting for him to speak.

“Today is Thursday,” he said. “Might it not be simply a memorandum?”

“My husband doesn’t print his memorandums, nor is it likely he would use a piece of paper torn from a paper bag.” She added, to clinch her belief, “I can’t imagine Herbert ever having a paper bag.”

“Perhaps he bought something at some haberdasher’s.”

“The paper is too cheap. It’s more like the sort they use at grocers’ or small stationers’.”

“So it is.”

===Chinese===
“那是他的书桌。”

恩迪科特夫人指了指一张摆在后面一扇窗前的平顶书桌。桌面上摊着一本柠檬黄封皮的书，书页皱巴巴的，像是被人重重地摔在那上面。书旁边是一小片纸。瓦尔考中尉俯下身，盯着那片纸看，却没有拿起来。纸上用铅笔印着一行字：

他抬眼看恩迪科特夫人。她显然正等着他开口。

“今天是星期四，”他说，“这会不会只是一张备忘的字条？”

“我丈夫写备忘从不用印刷体的大写字母，他也不大可能用一片从纸袋上撕下来的纸。”她又补上一句，好把自己的判断钉死，“我根本想象不出赫伯特会碰什么纸袋。”

“也许他在哪间男装店买了点东西。”

“这纸太蹩脚了。倒更像是杂货铺或者小文具铺里用的那种。”

“确实如此。”

===Original===
“And there’s a crudeness about the printing. It’s almost an intentional crudeness.” Mrs. Endicott stared fixedly at Lieutenant Valcour. “It’s the sort of printing you’d expect to find in a threat,” she said.

“I have learned to find almost any sort of writing or material used for purposes of conveying a threat,” Lieutenant Valcour said. “People who threaten are invariably unbalanced emotionally, if not actually mentally, and there is never any telling just what they will do. There was a case that recently came to my attention where a woman received a threat which had been engraved on excellent paper and enclosed in the conventional inner envelope one uses for formal announcements or invitations.”

“Really.”

“I’m not, by that, questioning your judgment in the matter of this note, Mrs. Endicott. It might quite well be a threat, as you think.”

“There is nothing else apparent that it could be.”

“When did you find it, Mrs. Endicott?”

“After my husband had left.”

“Lying just about where it is now?”

“Exactly where it is now.”

“I see. You didn’t touch it then—just read it. I wonder why your husband left it there.”

===Chinese===
“而且这印刷体透着一股粗劣。几乎是有意为之的粗劣。”恩迪科特夫人直勾勾地盯着瓦尔考中尉。“这种字迹，正是你会在一封恐吓信里料见到的那种。”她说。

“我已经见惯了人们用来传递恐吓的几乎每一种字迹或材料，”瓦尔考中尉说，“发出恐吓的人，纵然不是当真精神失常，也无一例外地在情绪上失去了平衡，谁也说不准他们到底会干出什么来。前不久就有一桩案子引起过我的注意：有位女士收到一份恐吓，竟是用上好的纸张雕版印制，还套在那种正式宣告或请柬常用的内层信封里。”

“原来如此。”

“我这么说，并不是在质疑您对这张字条的判断，恩迪科特夫人。它确实有可能如您所言，是一封恐吓。”

“显而易见，它不可能是别的什么了。”

“您是什么时候发现它的，恩迪科特夫人？”

“在我丈夫离开之后。”

“就放在现在这个位置上？”

“就在现在这个位置上。”

“我明白了。这么说您当时没有碰它——只是读了一遍。我纳闷您丈夫为什么会把它留在这儿。”

===Original===
She looked at him almost impatiently. “I don’t imagine he did leave it there—that is, purposely. It probably fell out from between the leaves when he slammed the book down.”

“Has it occurred to you that we might call up this Marge Myles—but that’s foolish. Of course you’d have thought of that.”

He observed her obliquely as she answered.

“He’d never forgive me.” Her gesture was faintly expressive of helplessness. “I’m not supposed to know anything about it.”

“Of course. This menace, Mrs. Endicott, this danger that you are fearing, where do you think it lies?”

She became consciously vague. “The streets—indoors—out—”

“And you’re basing it entirely upon this note?”

“Primarily. It’s something concrete, at any rate. I think that he ought to have protection, and yet, if I did do anything about it, he’d put it down as spying.”

===Chinese===
她几乎是带着不耐烦地看着他。“我并不觉得他是把它留在这儿的——也就是说，并非有意留下。多半是他把书摔到桌上时，这纸片从书页之间滑了出来。”

“您有没有想过，我们可以给这位玛吉·迈尔斯去个电话——可这话未免蠢了。您当然早就想到过这一点。”

她回答时，他从旁侧悄悄打量着她。

“他绝不会饶了我。”她的手势微微透出几分无可奈何。“按说我本不该对这事知道半点风声的。”

“那是自然。恩迪科特夫人，这种威胁——您所惧怕的那种危险——您觉得它潜伏在哪里？”

她有意含糊起来。“街头上——屋里头——外头——”

“而您完全是凭这张字条下的判断？”

“主要是。无论如何，这是件摸得着的东西。我觉得他该有人保护，可我要是真为此做了什么，他又会当成我在盯他的梢。”

===Original===
“Well, if this note is a threat there is rarely only one, you know. I wonder whether we might find any others. I haven’t the remotest justification for looking, but I’m willing to do so if you wish me to.”

Mrs. Endicott grew curiously detached. “His papers are in the upper right-hand drawer,” she said.

Lieutenant Valcour opened the drawer. Its contents were in a state of considerable confusion. It was not the sort of confusion which is the result of a cumulative addition of separate notes, letters, and sheets of paper, but a kind that exists when a normally orderly collection of papers has been milled around in suddenly.

“There’s quite a mass of stuff here,” he said. “It might be simpler to eliminate other possible places before tackling it. I must repeat again that I’ll be exceeding any legal rights by doing so, but if you earnestly believe your husband is in danger I’d like to go through the pockets of his clothing.”

“Pockets?”

“It’s a much more usual place to find important things than you would imagine.”

“His clothes are in that cupboard.”

===Chinese===
“那么，倘若这张字条确系恐吓，您要知道，恐吓很少只有一封的。不知我们能不能再找出几封来。我这样做没有任何一点说得过去的理由，可只要您愿意，我也乐意找一找。”

恩迪科特夫人反倒透出一种古怪的疏淡。“他的文件都在右上方的抽屉里。”她说。

瓦尔考中尉拉开抽屉。里面的东西凌乱不堪。这种凌乱，并非一张张便条、信件和散纸日积月累、不断增添所致，而是当一沓原本井然有序的文件被人骤然搅动过一番之后才会出现的那种样子。

“这儿东西可真不少，”他说，“在翻它之前，先把别处可能的地方排除掉，也许更省事。我必须再强调一遍，这样做我已逾越了任何法律所赋予的权限，但倘若您真心相信您丈夫身陷险境，我还想搜一搜他衣物的口袋。”

“口袋？”

“比起您想象的，那可是个寻常得多的、能翻出要紧物件的去处。”

“他的衣服在那个衣橱里。”

===Original===
Mrs. Endicott indicated a door. Lieutenant Valcour went over and opened it. An electric light was automatically turned on in the ceiling. The large hulk of a man crumpled into one corner of the cupboard gave him a severe shock. The man was dead. He closed the door and faced Mrs. Endicott. He nodded toward the desk, on which a telephone was standing.

“I’m going to use that telephone for a few minutes,” he said. “There’s a message I want to put through. Also, please ring for your maid.”

Mrs. Endicott’s eyes widened a little. “There’s something in the cupboard,” she said.

“Ring for your maid, please.”

She went past him and toward the cupboard door. He shrugged. The value of her reaction would offset the brutality of not stopping her. She opened the door and looked in. Her grip tightened on the knob.

“Then he didn’t go out at seven,” she said.

“No, Mrs. Endicott. He didn’t go out at all.”

===Chinese===
恩迪科特夫人朝一扇门示意了一下。瓦尔考中尉走过去把它拉开。天花板上的一盏电灯随即自动亮了起来。一团庞大的身躯蜷缩在衣橱的一角，给了他重重一惊。那人已经死了。他关上门，转过身来面对恩迪科特夫人。他朝书桌上放着的一部电话点了点头。

“我要用那部电话用上几分钟，”他说，“有一通口信我得发出去。还有，劳驾您按铃叫您的女佣。”

恩迪科特夫人的眼睛微微睁大了些。“衣橱里有点东西。”她说。

“劳驾您按铃叫女佣。”

她从他身边走过去，朝衣橱那扇门走去。他耸了耸肩。她反应的价值，足以抵偿不拦着她的那份残忍。她拉开门，朝里望去。她握着门把的手攥紧了。

“这么说，他七点钟并没有出门。”她说。

“是的，恩迪科特夫人。他压根儿就没有出过门。”

## II 9:24 p.m.—Hallmarks of Murder / 二 晚九时二十四分——谋杀的印记

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour felt that the utter stillness of the room would overwhelm him. He—Mrs. Endicott—everything seemed to be taking its cue from death. He reached past Mrs. Endicott and touched the body’s cheek. It was quite cold.

“Where is your room, Mrs. Endicott?”

He carefully pried her fingers from the knob of the cupboard door and then closed it.

“But you can’t leave him in that cupboard.”

Her voice held the toneless qualities of arrested emotion, as if the functioning of her nerve centres had stopped.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉觉得，这间屋子里那种彻头彻尾的寂静，几乎要将他压垮。他——恩迪科特夫人——一切仿佛都在以死亡为号令，循着它的节拍行事。他越过恩迪科特夫人，伸手触了触那具尸体的面颊。冰冷彻骨。

“您的房间在哪里，恩迪科特夫人？”

他小心翼翼地把她扣在衣橱门把手上的手指一根根掰开，随即将橱门合拢。

“可您总不能把他留在那衣橱里呀。”

她嗓音里带着一种平板无调的质地，像极了骤然中止的情绪，仿佛她那些神经中枢的运转已经停摆。

===Original===
“We must leave him in there, Mrs. Endicott, until someone from the medical examiner’s office has seen him. If you’ll tell me the name of your family physician before you lie down—”

“Lie down—I? Lie down?”

“Yes, and rest. I’ll call the doctor up on the possible chance that we’re mistaken, only I’m quite certain, Mrs. Endicott, that we aren’t.”

She stumbled verbally in her rush. “Worth—Dr. Sanford Worth—Calumet 876—it’s 876 something—I know it perfectly well. I—it’s in my book—come with me.”

She seemed mechanically vitalized, and her movements were those of a nervous, jerky toy. She flung open a door adjacent to the cupboard. It led into a bathroom, the fittings of which were of coral-coloured porcelain. A door in the opposite wall led into her bedroom. She went immediately to a leather reference book beside a telephone near her bed.

“It’s Calumet 8769,” she said.

Her finger slipped in the dialling. Lieutenant Valcour gently took the instrument from her hands and put through the call.

===Chinese===
“我们必须把他留在里头，恩迪科特夫人，要等法医办公室派人来验看过。等您躺下之前，若是能告诉我您家家庭医师的姓名——”

“躺下——我？躺下？”

“是的，歇一歇。我会打电话给那位医生，虽说也存在我们弄错了的一线可能——只是恩迪科特夫人，我相当肯定，我们并没有弄错。”

她仓促间说话都打起结来。“沃思——桑福德·沃思医生（Dr. Sanford Worth）——卡卢梅特（Calumet）876——是876什么来着——这号码我明明清楚得很。我——它记在我的本子上——随我来。”

她像是被某种机械的力量驱动着活转过来，一举一动都如同一只神经质的、一抽一抽的玩具。她一把推开紧挨着衣橱的一扇门。门通向一间浴室，里头的卫浴配件都是珊瑚色的瓷器。对面墙上一扇门通向她的卧室。她径直走向床边电话旁一本皮面的通讯录。

“是卡卢梅特8769。”她说。

她的手指在拨号盘上滑了一下。瓦尔考中尉轻轻地把话机从她手里接过来，替她接通了这通电话。

===Original===
“The office of Dr. Worth?” he said, when a woman’s voice answered him. “This is the home of Mr. Herbert Endicott. I am Lieutenant Valcour of the police department. Mr. Endicott is dead. I would appreciate it if Dr. Worth would come here at once and consult with the medical examiner, and also attend to Mrs. Endicott. Thank you.” He replaced the receiver.

“I haven’t the slightest intention of collapsing, Lieutenant.”

“We will need Dr. Worth anyway, Mrs. Endicott.”

Lieutenant Valcour dialled the Central Office and, in a suddenly most efficient voice, gave the requisite information. He then called his own precinct station and told the sergeant at the desk to send over a detail of five men in uniform.

“The chief of the Homicide Bureau, the medical examiner, and some of my own men will be here presently,” he said to Mrs. Endicott.

“And my husband has to stay in that cupboard until they come?”

“Unless Dr. Worth arrives first and disagrees with me that Mr. Endicott is dead.”

“It’s inhuman.”

“Very, but there’s a set routine for these cases that we have to observe. Is this the button you ring for your maid?”

He pressed a push button set in the wall at the head of the bed.

===Chinese===
“是沃思医生的诊所吗？”待一个女声应答，他开口道。“这里是赫伯特·恩迪科特先生府上。我是警察局的瓦尔考中尉。恩迪科特先生已经去世了。若蒙沃思医生即刻赶来，与法医一同会商，并顺便照看一下恩迪科特夫人，我将不胜感激。多谢。”他把听筒搁回。

“我丝毫没有要晕倒的意思，中尉。”

“不管怎样，我们也用得着沃思医生，恩迪科特夫人。”

瓦尔考中尉拨通总局，用一种骤然变得极为干练利落的声调，把该交代的事项一一交代清楚。接着他又拨往自己所在的辖区分局，吩咐值 desk 的警佐派一队五名制服警员过来。

“凶杀科的科长、法医，还有我手下的几个人，马上就到。”他对恩迪科特夫人说。

“那么我丈夫就得在那衣橱里一直待到他们来？”

“除非沃思医生先一步赶到，并且不认同我说恩迪科特先生已经死亡的判断。”

“这未免太不近人情了。”

“确属不近人情，可这类案子有一套既定的程序，我们不得不遵行。您叫女佣按的是墙上这个铃吗？”

他揿了一下嵌在床头墙壁上的一个按钮。

===Original===
“Yes, but I don’t want her.”

“You may, and there’s no harm in her being with you. I’m going to leave you in here for a little while, until the people we’ve telephoned for come.”

“You insist on my staying in this room?”

“Heavens, no. Do anything you like, Mrs. Endicott, or that you feel will help you. As long,” he added gently, “as you don’t leave the house.”

“Oh.”

“You see we’ll have to talk such a lot of things over, just as soon as the usual formalities are finished.”

“It’s rather terrible, isn’t it?”

“Pretty terrible, Mrs. Endicott.”

“So”—she mentally groped for a satisfactory word—“so conclusive.”

===Chinese===
“是的，可我并不需要她。”

“也许会需要的，有她陪着您也没什么坏处。我得把您留在这儿一小会儿，等我们打电话叫的人到了再说。”

“您一定要我待在这间房里？”

“天哪，并非如此。您想做什么都成，恩迪科特夫人，只要您觉得对您有帮助。只消——”他温和地补了一句，“您别离开这宅子。”

“哦。”

“您也看见了，等那些例行的公事一了，我们还有许许多多事情要谈呢。”

“这真是太可怕了，是不是？”

“相当可怕，恩迪科特夫人。”

“就这样”——她在心里摸索着一个称心的字眼——“就这样了结了。”

===Original===
It seemed a peculiar choice. Lieutenant Valcour sensed that it wasn’t just Endicott’s life alone which was concluded by death, but something else as well—such as an argument, perhaps, or a secret and bitter struggle. The precise significance was elusive, and he gave it up, or rather checked it within his memory in that compartment which already contained six barely smoked cigarette butts, a broken finger nail, bruise marks, and a note which, in view of the body, might safely be presumed to have been a threat.

A maid knocked on the door and came in. She stared speculatively for a curious second at Lieutenant Valcour.

“Madam rang?”

“No, Roberts. Lieutenant Valcour rang. Lieutenant Valcour is of the police.”

Any sudden announcing of the police is always shocking. It is a prelude to so many unpleasant possibilities even in the lives of the most blameless. They are in a class with telegrams. Lieutenant Valcour noted that Roberts accepted his identity with nothing further than an almost imperceptible catching of breath. Mrs. Endicott’s attitude puzzled him. It wasn’t resentment, certainly, or any stretching at rudeness; such emotions seemed so utterly inconsequential at this moment when she must have been wrenched by a very severe shock. It reminded him of the aimless play of lightning clowning before the purposeful fury of a storm.

“Mrs. Endicott will explain things to you,” he said. “Stay with her, please.”

===Chinese===
这个选词显得古怪。瓦尔考中尉隐隐感到，被死亡了结的并不只是恩迪科特这一条性命，另外还有些什么——也许是一场争执，又也许是一场隐秘而惨烈的角力。确切的意味一时捉摸不透，他便不再深究，或者更准确地说，把它收进脑中那个专设的格子里——那格子里已经装着六个没怎么抽过的烟头、一片断裂的指甲、几处淤痕，还有一张字条；既然有了这具尸体，那张字条尽可断定是一封恐吓了。

一名女佣敲敲门，走了进来。她朝瓦尔考中尉打量了古怪的一秒钟，眼神里透着揣度。

“是夫人按的铃？”

“不是我，罗伯茨（Roberts）。是瓦尔考中尉按的铃。瓦尔考中尉是警察局的人。”

任何一句突如其来的警察身份的通报，总会令人心头一震。即便在最清白无瑕的人的一生里，它也是许许多多令人不快的可能性的前奏曲。它这一类东西，跟电报同属一等。瓦尔考中尉留意到，罗伯茨在接纳他这身份时，只不过几乎察觉不到地倒吸了一口气，再无别的反应。恩迪科特夫人的态度却叫他纳闷。那当然不是怨恨，也算不上有任何刻意发作的无礼；在她想必已被一桩极重的惊变狠狠绞缠的此刻，这类情绪似乎显得全然无关紧要。那倒叫人联想起，在一场蓄势而来、暴烈凶猛的风暴之前，那漫无目的、嬉戏作态的闪电。

“恩迪科特夫人会把事情的原委讲给您听。”他说，“请您陪着她。”

===Original===
There lingered, as he went into the bathroom, a picture of the two women, separated by the distance of the room, standing quite still and staring at each other: Mrs. Endicott, young, exquisitely lovely looking—the other, older, quite implacable. The connection was absurd, but the effect remained of two antagonists in a strange encounter who are standing in their separate corners of a ring. He closed the bathroom door and slipped the catch. He turned on all the lights.

There was a single window. He parted muslin curtains and looked at a glazed lemon-coloured shade, especially along its hemmed bottom. There were some smudges at its centre that interested him. He believed that they had been made by a dirty thumb. He raised the shade and the lower sash of the window.

The night was clear and cold and windless. A shallow stone balcony ran the width of the rear of the house. It was for ornamentation rather than use, as to get onto it one had to straddle the window sill. Lieutenant Valcour did so, and stood looking down upon the dimly defined outlines of what, in spring, would bloom into a formal garden. He satisfied himself that there seemed no access to the balcony from the ground unless one used a ladder or were endowed with those special and fortunately rare qualities which transform an otherwise normal person into a human fly.

===Chinese===
他走进浴室时，脑中久久留着这样一幅画面：两个女人隔着整间屋子的距离，一动不动地站着，彼此对视——恩迪科特夫人，年轻，生得精致入骨的美——另一个，年长些，神情全然不为所动。这联想未免荒唐，可那种效果却挥之不去：仿佛两个对手在一场古怪的邂逅里，各自站在擂台一角对峙。他合上浴室的门，把门闩悄悄扣上。他把所有的灯统统打开。

这里只有一扇窗。他拨开薄纱窗帘，端详起一面上了蜡的柠檬色遮光帘，尤其细看了它那缝过边的下摆。帘子正中有几处污痕，引起了他的兴趣。他相信，那是一只脏兮兮的拇指留下的。他把帘子卷起，又推开了窗户的下扇。

夜色清朗、寒冷、无风。一道浅浅的石砌阳台沿着房子后部的整面宽度延伸出去。它与其说是供人使用的，不如说是作装饰之用，因为要踏上它，非得跨坐到窗台上才行。瓦尔考中尉就如此这般跨了出去，站在那儿俯瞰底下那片轮廓依稀可辨的地方——待到春日，那里便会绽放成一座规整的花园。他确认了一番，从地面似乎并无通向阳台的去路，除非谁用了梯子，又或者天生具备那种特殊的、万幸十分罕见的本事，能把一个原本正常的人变成一只人蝇。

===Original===
The house was five windows wide; the two on the right of the bathroom belonged to Mrs. Endicott’s room, and the two on its left to her husband’s. He flashed on his electric torch and examined all five sills. None showed a trace of recent passage, and there was no very good reason, he realized, why any of them should. They were clean, windswept, and smooth.

How pleasant it would be, he reflected, to come across the perfect imprint of a shoe, or a rubber, or—what was it that was so popular at the moment?—of course: the footprint of a gorilla. The case would then be what was technically known as an open-and-shut one. He’d simply take the train for California and arrest Lon Chaney, and—But enough.

And the floor itself on the balcony was smugly lacking in clues. He relinquished the keen sharp air, the star-heavy night, and returned to the bathroom by way of its window, which he closed, and again drew down its lemon-coloured shade.

===Chinese===
这幢房子有五扇窗那么宽；浴室右边那两扇属于恩迪科特夫人的房间，左边那两扇则属于她丈夫的房间。他摁亮手电筒，把五个窗台逐一察看了一遍。没有一处显露近来有人经过的痕迹，而他也明白，本也没有什么过硬的理由指望它们显出什么来。窗台干干净净，被风吹得溜光，平平整整。

要是能撞见一只鞋、一只胶鞋的完美印痕，或者——眼下正时兴的是什么来着？——对了：一只大猩猩的脚印，那该有多省事。那样一来，这桩案子便可归入行话里所谓一开门就关了案的那一类。他大可乘火车直奔加州去把朗·钱尼（Lon Chaney）逮捕归案，然后——好了，适可而止吧。

至于阳台的地面本身，则自满似的不见半点线索。他恋恋不舍地告别那凌厉清冽的空气、那缀满繁星的夜空，从窗口折回浴室，关上窗，又把那只柠檬色遮光帘重新拉了下来。

===Original===
A cake of soap in a container set in the wall above a basin attracted his attention. It was so incredibly dirty. Someone with exceptionally dirty hands had used it and either hadn’t bothered to rinse it off or else hadn’t had the time to. The dirt had dried on it.

He couldn’t vision such a condition of uncleanliness in connection with the hands of either Mr. or Mrs. Endicott, unless there had been some obscure reason. He preferred to think for the moment that the hands had belonged, and presumably still did, to the murderer. That, of course, eliminated the gorilla. What a pity it was, he reflected, that he was so constantly obsessed with infernal absurdities. Even though he tried to keep them under triple lock and key when working with his associates on the force, they had a distressing habit at times of cropping out into the open where they could be seen. Nor were they of a humour especially in vogue among his contemporaries; there rarely was an and-the-drummer-said-to-Mabel or an-Irishman-and-a-Jew among them. Rarely? He shuddered. Never. As a result there were occasions when he rested under the cloud of being considered mildly lunatic. It was bad business. He had told himself so firmly again and again. Success and humour formed bedfellows as agreeable as an absentminded dog would be *en négligé* in the boudoir of a surprised cat.

===Chinese===
洗手台上方嵌在墙里的皂盒中那块肥皂，引起了他的注意。它脏得简直令人难以置信。有一双格外脏的手用过它，而那人要么懒得把它冲净，要么根本来不及冲。污迹已经在上面干结了。

无论恩迪科特先生还是夫人的手，他都无从想象会脏成这样，除非其中有什么说不清的缘由。他暂时倒宁可设想，那双手原是属于凶手的，而且十之八九仍属于凶手。这样一来，大猩猩自然也就出局了。多可惜啊，他暗想，自己竟如此没完没了地被这些该死的荒唐念头缠住不放。纵然他在与同僚共事时，恨不得把这些念头上了三重锁牢牢锁起，可它们偏偏有个恼人的脾气，时不时地要冒到光天化日之下，叫人瞧个分明。再说，他这些念头也绝非同行间最时兴的那路幽默；其中难得有一则“推销员对梅贝尔说”或者“一个爱尔兰人和一个犹太人”之类的笑话。难得？他打了个寒噤。是从不。正因如此，他时不时便要顶着一顶被视作多少有些神经兮兮的帽子。这可大大不妙。这一点，他已经一次又一次郑重地告诫过自己。功成名就与幽默感凑成的一对同床人，其融洽程度，大抵与一只心不在焉的狗身着便服闯进一只受惊的猫的闺房相差无几。

===Original===
With a beautiful access of gravity he lifted the lid of an enamelled wicker hamper and peered in at the soiled linen it contained. There were many towels. Towels were, he reflected, one of the few genuine hallmarks of the rich. The Endicotts, hence, must be very, very rich, as it was obvious that they shed—or was it shedded?—towels as profusely as the petals fall from a white flowering tree.

There was a badly soiled and crumpled towel on the very top of the pile. He picked it up and looked at it. It was very dirty and still faintly damp. He folded it, set it on the floor beneath the basin, and placed the cake of soap upon it. They were, he smiled faintly, Exhibits B and C. The distinction of being classified as Exhibit A was already reserved by the threatening note on the desk. As for the smudges on the lemon-coloured shade, they would have to be definitely determined as finger prints before they could have their niche in the alphabet. The prosecuting attorney would be pleased. He was a man whose flair for alphabeted exhibits amounted to a passion. Lieutenant Valcour hoped that he could find a crushed rose. The prosecuting attorney was at his best with crushed roses. For example, take that knifing case in the Ghetto. Three petals were all the prosecuting attorney had had there, but they had bloomed, via the jury, into tears. Into tears, Lieutenant Valcour amended, and tripe.

===Chinese===
他煞有介事、一本正经地掀起一只上了搪瓷的柳条篓的盖子，朝里面装的脏衣物窥视了一番。里头有许多毛巾。毛巾，他暗想，乃是有钱人少有的几样真正印记之一。可见恩迪科特夫妇必定是极其极其有钱的了，因为他们显然甩出——甩毛巾这词该怎么说来着，shed还是shedded？——毛巾之多，不下于白花满树时纷纷坠落的花瓣。

毛巾堆最上头，有一条脏得不成样子、皱成一团的毛巾。他把它拿起来端详。它脏得很，还微微带着潮气。他把它折好，搁在洗手台下面的地上，又把那块肥皂置于其上。这两样，他浅浅地笑了笑，便是乙号证物和丙号证物（Exhibits B and C）了。至于甲号证物（Exhibit A）的殊荣，早已被书桌上那张恐吓条独占去了。至于柠檬色遮光帘上的那几处污痕，则非得先确凿地认定为指纹之后，才能在字母表里谋得自己的一席之地。检察官大人必定会高兴的。那是个对字母编号证物近乎痴狂、把它当成毕生嗜好的人。瓦尔考中尉巴不得自己能再寻到一朵被揉碎的玫瑰。检察官大人拿手的就是处理揉碎的玫瑰。就拿贫民区那桩捅刀子的案子来说吧。当时检察官手头所有的，不过是三片花瓣而已，可那三片花瓣，经由陪审团这一程，竟绽放成了滚滚热泪。热泪，瓦尔考中尉在心里订正道，外加一派胡言。

===Original===
A pair of silver-backed brushes showed no finger marks upon their shining surfaces, nor were there any on the silver rim that backed a comb. One could infer, Lieutenant Valcour decided, and did, that someone later than Mr. Endicott had used them, as Mr. Endicott would never have wiped them off to remove his prints, and had he not done so there certainly would have been some signs of usage. What a careful murderer it was, he thought, to polish the evidence so very clean. And what a grip the subject of finger prints maintained upon the criminal mind, and upon the lay mind as well. It seemed to embrace their Alpha and Omega in the scientific detection of crime. Lieutenant Valcour offered to bet himself his last nickel that the murderer had overlooked entirely the possibility of what might be found left within the bristles of the brushes and between the teeth of the comb. He took a clean hand towel from the rack and wrapped the brushes and the comb up in it. He set the bundle on the floor beside the cake of soap and the dirty towel. The alphabet, he reflected, had now been depleted down to F.

The bathroom could tell him nothing more. He reconstructed its segment of the drama before leaving it: the murderer had entered, gone at once to the window and pulled down its shade. There had been a washing of hands and a brushing and combing of hair. The murderer had wiped the silver clear of finger prints and had left. The whys and wherefors must come later. The shell would remain unchanged until the moment came to pour it full of motive and give it reason and life.

===Chinese===
一对银背发刷，光亮的表面不见半点指痕，一把梳子背面的银质镶边上也同样干净。由此可以推断——瓦尔考中尉也确实这么推断了——在恩迪科特先生之后，另有人用过它们，因为恩迪科特先生绝不会动手去擦掉自己的指纹，而倘若他没有擦过，上面理应留有些许使用过的痕迹。这是何等细心的凶手啊，他想，竟把证据擦拭得如此一尘不染。而指纹这一桩学问，又何等牢固地攫住罪犯的心智，也同样攫住了外行人的心智。在科学侦破犯罪这件事上，它似乎涵盖了他们的始与终、他们的全部。瓦尔考中尉愿意跟自己打赌，赌上自己最后一枚五分镍币：那凶手压根儿就忽略了，发刷的刷毛之间、梳子的梳齿之间，可能还留着些什么。他从架子上取下一条干净的手巾，把发刷和梳子一并裹了起来。他把这包东西放在地上，挨着那块肥皂和那条脏毛巾。字母表，他暗想，如今已然耗到了F。

浴室再也告诉他不了什么了。临出去前，他在脑中把这场戏的浴室这一幕重新搬演了一遍：凶手进来后，径直走到窗前，拉下了遮光帘。洗过手，又刷过梳过头发。凶手把银器上的指纹擦得干干净净，然后离去。其间的缘由与所以然，只能留待日后。这只空壳将原封不动地摆在那儿，直到时机来临，把动机一气灌满其中，赋予它缘由与生命。

===Original===
He went into Endicott’s room and opened the cupboard door. The beam from his electric torch, added to the ceiling light, brought out sharply the waxy pallor of the face’s skin. Its good-looking, homely ruggedness was marred by a slight cast of petulance, as inappropriate as a pink bow on a lion. Cruelty showed, too, a little—and something inscrutable that baffled analysis. Endicott weighed, Lieutenant Valcour decided, close upon two hundred pounds and no fat, either; a strong, powerfully muscled man, and about thirty-five years old. He played the light upon Endicott’s right hand and exposed the wrist a little by drawing up the sleeve. The wrist and hand were normally clean, as he had expected.

He gently inserted his fingers into such of Endicott’s pockets as he could reach without disturbing the body. From the rumpled state of their linings and their complete emptiness it was apparent that they had been hastily turned inside out and replaced.

===Chinese===
他走进恩迪科特的房间，拉开衣橱的门。手电筒的光柱，再配上头顶那盏灯，把那张脸上蜡一般的苍白衬得分外鲜明。那是一张算得上英俊、带着质朴粗犷的脸，此刻却被一抹微微的愠色所损，仿佛给一头狮子缀上一只粉色蝴蝶结般格格不入。脸上还透出些许残酷——还有一种叫人琢磨不透、无从剖析的什么。恩迪科特的体重，瓦尔考中尉判定，将近两百磅，而且一点肥肉都没有；是个筋骨强健、肌肉发达的壮汉，年纪在三十五岁上下。他把电筒的光打到恩迪科特的右手上，又把袖口稍稍捋起，露出一段手腕。手腕和手一如他所料，是寻常的干净。

他尽可能不去惊动尸体，把手指轻轻地探进够得着的几只恩迪科特的口袋里。从那些口袋布里子皱皱巴巴、又空空如也的样子看，它们分明是被人匆匆忙忙地翻过个儿、又塞了回去。

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour began to sniff at a motive. Not robbery, exactly, in the ordinary sense, as an expensive platinum wrist watch and a set of black pearl shirt studs were untouched, but robbery in the extraordinary sense—one that had been indulged in for a certain definite purpose. He strongly began to suspect that there would be the ubiquitous “fatal papers.” It might also develop that Endicott was the secretive owner of some fabulous jewel of a sort usually referred to as a Heart of Buddha, or perhaps some important slice of the Russian crown jewels—the number of which now almost equalled, he reflected, the thousands upon thousands of ancestors who came over to our shores on the *Mayflower*.

The top button was missing from Endicott’s overcoat. It would have been torn away when the murderer had lifted his victim from the floor in order to drag him into the cupboard. Otherwise there wasn’t anything that hinted at a struggle. There wasn’t any blood, or any wound, or sign of contusion visible on the head, and no trace of blood around such parts of the cupboard that Lieutenant Valcour could see.

He suddenly wondered where Endicott’s hat was. It wasn’t on Endicott’s head, nor in the cupboard, nor in the bedroom, which struck him as strange. He was a strong believer in the paraphrase that where the coat is, there the hat lies, too. One could look for it more carefully later. Just at present, of greater importance was Exhibit A.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉开始嗅探起动机来。严格说来，并非寻常意义上的劫财，因为一块昂贵的白金腕表和一整副黑珍珠衬衫钉扣都原封未动，而是非同寻常意义上的劫掠——一种为着某个明确目的而行的劫掠。他开始强烈地怀疑，此事必将牵扯出那无所不在的“致命文件”。说不定还会牵出，恩迪科特暗地里竟是某件传奇珠宝的藏主——那种通常被称作“佛心（Heart of Buddha）”的宝物，又或者是俄国皇室珠宝（Russian crown jewels）里的某件重要碎片——这类碎片如今的数目，他暗想，几乎已可与当年乘“五月花号”（*Mayflower*）渡海而来、成千上万的先辈们相提并论了。

恩迪科特的大衣上少了一颗最上面的扣子。那想必是凶手把猎物从地上提起、拖进衣橱时扯掉的。除此之外，再没有什么暗示曾有过搏斗的痕迹。头上看不到半点血迹，没有伤口，也没有淤伤的迹象，在瓦尔考中尉所能看见的衣橱各处，也寻不到一丝血痕。

他忽然纳闷起恩迪科特的帽子到哪里去了。帽子既不在恩迪科特头上，也不在衣橱里，更不在卧室中，这叫他颇觉古怪。他一向笃信那句改写过的俗话：大衣在哪儿，帽子也就在哪儿。这事大可留待日后更仔细地去找。眼下更要紧的，是那件甲号证物。

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour went to the desk, picked up the note and studied it. The pencil used had been a thick leaded one, almost a crayon. And there, right before his nose in a shallow tray that held an assortment of office things, was a pencil with a very thick lead that was almost a crayon. He copied the note with it on the back of an envelope he took from his pocket. He compared the result with the printing on the note. They were alike.

One begins, he informed himself gently, to wonder.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉走到书桌前，拿起那张字条细加研看。写字用的那支铅笔芯很粗，几乎算得上是一支蜡笔。而就在他鼻子底下，一只盛着各色办公零碎的浅盘里，正躺着一支芯很粗、几乎算得上蜡笔的铅笔。他从口袋里摸出一只信封，用那支笔在信封背面照着字条临摹了一遍。他把这结果与字条上的印刷体两相对照。两者如出一辙。

人啊，他轻声地自语，不由得开始要起疑了。

## III 9:45 p.m.—Guards Are Stationed at the Doors / 三 晚九时四十五分——守卫分立各门

===Original===
There are knocks, Lieutenant Valcour believed, and knocks. He ranged them from gentle careless rappings, through sly sinister taps, to imperative demands and, finally, thumps. He classified the ones at the moment being bestowed upon the hall door as official whacks. He was right. He put the scrap of paper and the crayon pencil in his pocket and turned to greet five men from the station house who flooded into the room on the heels of his “Come in.”

They were intelligent-looking young men, well built, alert, and their uniforms were immaculate—five competent blue jays outlined sharply against gray walls. Lieutenant Valcour knew each one of them both by reputation and by name.

He nodded to the starchiest and youngest looking of them. “Cassidy,” he said, “stay in here. O’Brian, stay by the front door, and keep Hansen with you to carry messages. There’s a servants’ entrance at the front, McGinnis. It’s yours. And you, Stump, watch the door from the back of the house into the garden. If anyone wants to leave the house send him to me first. You can let anyone in, with the exception of reporters, and find out their business. Now in regard to the reporters just be your natural genial selves and say that apart from the plain statement that Mr. Herbert Endicott, the owner of this house, is dead and that—” Lieutenant Valcour choked slightly—“foul play is suspected, you can tell them nothing. The police, as usual, are actively on the job, have the case well in hand, and there is every reason to believe that in view of our customary efficiency the guilty parties will soon be brilliantly apprehended etcetera and so forth Amen. Excuse-it-please.”

===Chinese===
敲门声这东西，在瓦尔考中尉看来，是分许多种的。他把它们从轻描淡写的随意叩击，排到鬼祟阴险的笃笃声，再到不由分说的强求，直至末了的砰砰擂门。此刻正落在大厅房门上的那阵，他归类为公事公办的拍击。他没判断错。他把那片碎纸和那支蜡笔铅笔揣进口袋，转过身来迎见分局来的五个人——他一声“进来”，话音未落，他们便涌进了房间。

他们都是些看着精明强干的年轻人，体格健壮、神色机警，制服一尘不染——五只利落的蓝松鸦，鲜明地衬在灰墙之前。瓦尔考中尉对他们每一个人，无论声名还是姓名，都了如指掌。

他朝其中最衣冠楚楚、看上去也最年轻的那位点了点头。“卡西迪（Cassidy），”他说，“你留在这屋里。奥布赖恩（O’Brian），守在前门那边，把汉森（Hansen）留在你身边跑腿传话。前面有个佣人进出的门，麦金尼斯（McGinnis），归你看守。还有你，斯坦普（Stump），守住从房子后部通往花园的那扇门。要是有谁想离开这房子，先让他来见我。要进来的人，除了记者，你们都可以放行，但要问清楚他们的来意。至于那些记者嘛，你们只管拿出你们天生和蔼可亲的样子，告诉他们：除了这房子的主人赫伯特·恩迪科特先生已经身亡、而且——”瓦尔考中尉微微噎了一下——“死因可疑这一明明白白的说法之外，其余无可奉告。警方一如既往地正在积极履职，对本案已牢牢掌握，完全有理由相信，凭我们一贯的高效，罪魁祸首很快就会被漂亮地捉拿归案，云云，阿门。失陪了。”

===Original===
“Cuckoo,” confided O’Brian to Hansen as, with Stump and McGinnis, they filed out.

“Cuckoo as a fox,” agreed Hansen, who had worked under Lieutenant Valcour on a case before.

“Yeh?”

“Yeah.”

Lieutenant Valcour and young Cassidy were alone.

“Tell me, Cassidy, how are the servants taking all this, if you bumped into any of them?”

“Sure, I only saw the girl at the front door, Lieutenant. She’s a sorry piece, and was shivering worse than one of them new and indecent dances.”

“Did she say anything?”

“She did not, beyond telling us to follow her upstairs. She took us to that door across the hallway first, and some lady said you was in here.”

“How did that lady’s voice sound to you, Cassidy?”

“Smooth, sir.”

“Not nervous?”

“Devil a bit.”

“What are you looking for, Cassidy?”

“The corpse, sir.”

“It’s in that cupboard.”

“Is it now?” said Cassidy, casually removing himself as far from the cupboard door as he could. “It ain’t one of them Western hammer murders, is it?”

“I don’t know what kind of a homicide it is, Cassidy. There are no marks on him that I can see.”

“Will it be poison, then?”

“Maybe.”

“Well, let’s hope it’s one or the other. I hate them mystery cases where the deceased got his go-by from a Chinese blow gun, or some imported snake from Timbuktu, or parts adjacent.”

===Chinese===
“神经病。”奥布赖恩一边随着斯坦普和麦金尼斯鱼贯而出，一边对汉森悄悄说道。

“狡猾得像狐狸一样的神经病。”汉森附和道，他此前曾在某桩案子里在瓦尔考中尉手下办过事。

“是吗？”

“是。”

瓦尔考中尉和年轻的卡西迪单独留了下来。

“跟我说说，卡西迪，那些佣人对这一切反应如何，你要是撞见他们中哪个的话？”

“报告长官，我只在前门见到了那个姑娘，中尉。她一脸惨相，抖得比那些新派又不正经的舞还要厉害。”

“她有没有说什么？”

“她没说什么，只让我们跟她上楼来。她先把我们领到走廊对面那扇门前，后来一位女士说您在这儿。”

“那位女士的嗓音在你听来如何，卡西迪？”

“挺从容的，长官。”

“不紧张？”

“一点也不。”

“你在找什么，卡西迪？”

“那具尸体，长官。”

“在那个衣橱里。”

“是吗？”卡西迪说着，不动声色地把身子挪到离衣橱门尽可能远的地方。“这总不会是那种西部锤子杀人案吧？”

“我还说不准这是哪一种凶杀，卡西迪。他身上我能看到的，没有任何伤痕。”

“那莫非是毒杀？”

“也许。”

“那就好，但愿是这两者之一。我最烦那种神秘兮兮的案子——死者要么是被中国吹管射死的，要么是被从廷巴克图或附近一带进口的什么蛇弄死的。”

===Original===
“When did you ever work on such a case, Cassidy?”

“Sure, Lieutenant, you can read about them every week in the magazines. There’s one that’s in its fourth part now where some louse of foreign extraction kills a dumb cluck of a Wall Street magnet with a package of paper matches, the tips of which was so fixed that they exploded when struck, instead of acting decent like, and shot dabs of poison into the skin of his fingers. Can you imagine it? Just say the word and I’ll bring it around to the station house and you can read it for yourself.”

“Thanks, Cassidy.”

“It’ll be no trouble at all, Lieutenant.”

An important knock on the door disclosed a stranger. Lieutenant Valcour addressed him, correctly, as Dr. Worth.

Dr. Sanforth Worth did not merely imagine that he cut a distinguished figure; he was sure of it. A certain grayness clung impressively about the temples of an intellectual brow, and he was probably one of the few physicians left in New York who had both the audacity and ability to wear a Vandyke. He was dressed in evening clothes and had not bothered to remove his overcoat or to give up his hat.

“Dr. Worth? I am Lieutenant Valcour, of the police. Mr. Endicott is in here.”

Dr. Worth bowed gravely, and with a sparklingly manicured hand stroked his Vandyke once. “I have been afraid of something like this for quite a while, Lieutenant,” he said. His voice, in company with everything else about him, sounded expensive.

===Chinese===
“你什么时候办过这样的案子，卡西迪？”

“哎，中尉，这种案子您每星期都能在杂志上读到。眼下就有一部正连载到第四期，讲一个外籍的无赖，用一包纸火柴杀了一个华尔街大亨那样的蠢货，那火柴头被做了手脚，划着的时候不会规规矩矩地点火，反倒会炸开，把一丁点一丁点的毒射进他手指的皮肉里。您能想象吗？您只消一句话，我就把它带到分局去，您自个儿看。”

“多谢了，卡西迪。”

“一点也不麻烦，中尉。”

一阵分量颇重的敲门声，送进来一个陌生人。瓦尔考中尉一口就准确无误地唤出了他：沃思医生。

桑福德·沃思医生并不仅仅是自以为仪表出众；他是确信无疑。两鬓那种颇具分量的灰白，醒目地贴在一副饱学的额头之上，而在整个纽约，仍既有胆量又有本事蓄一茬范戴克胡的医生，怕是所剩无几，他大约便是其中之一。他身着晚礼服，却嫌麻烦，没有脱掉大衣，也没摘下帽子。

“沃思医生？我是警方的瓦尔考中尉。恩迪科特先生就在这里面。”

沃思医生庄重地一躬身，用一只修剪得闪闪发亮的手捋了一把他的范戴克胡。“这一类事，我已经担心好些时候了，中尉。”他说。他的嗓音，连同他浑身上下其余的一切，听上去都很昂贵。

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour raised his eyebrows. “It begins to seem, Doctor, as if everybody except Mr. Endicott himself anticipated his murder.”

“Murder?”

It was Dr. Worth’s eyebrows’ turn. They raised. They fell. They became, in conjunction with pursed lips, judicious. He removed his overcoat and placed it, with his hat, upon a chair.

“I believe you will find, Lieutenant, that it is just his heart. His—Dear God in heaven, man, what have you left him slumped down like this for?”

“You mustn’t touch him, Doctor, unless you think he isn’t dead.”

Dr. Worth stiffened perceptibly. “Fancy that,” he said. “Well, one would infer that he is dead, all right. Just the same, Lieutenant, is there any legal objection to opening his coat and shirt bosom? I dare say I could slit them, if you preferred. You see, it might be advisable to test for any trace of heart action with the stethoscope.”

“I had no intention of offending you, Doctor. Go right ahead and do anything you think is absolutely essential to establish life or death.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉扬了扬眉。“事情开始显得，医生，仿佛除了恩迪科特先生本人之外，人人都在等着他被谋杀。”

“谋杀？”

这一回轮到沃思医生的两道眉毛了。它们扬起。又落下。继而与抿紧的双唇一道，变得审慎起来。他脱下大衣，连同帽子一起搁在一张椅子上。

“我想您会发现，中尉，不过是他的心脏罢了。他——老天爷在上，伙计，您怎么把他这样瘫着丢在这儿？”

“您不该碰他，医生，除非您觉得他还没死。”

沃思医生明显地一僵。“真没想到。”他说。“好吧，人总会推断他已经死了，这没问题。不过中尉，解开他的外衣和衬衫前襟，在法律上有什么妨碍吗？要是您愿意，我大可以把它们割开。您瞧，用听诊器测一测还有没有丝毫心脏搏动，也许还是得当的。”

“我并无冒犯之意，医生。您只管放手去做您觉得为判定生死所绝对必要的任何事情。”

===Original===
Dr. Worth melted conservatively. “You see, sir, I know his heart. He had a nervous breakdown two years ago which left its action impaired.” He loosened Endicott’s overcoat and the black pearl studs set in a semisoft shirt bosom. He listened for a moment, and then removed the stethoscope. “No trace,” he said. “He’s dead. Shall I button up the shirt front and the coat again?”

“It isn’t necessary, Doctor.”

The hall door opened abruptly. The homicide chief and the medical examiner came in, followed by a squad of detectives. Lieutenant Valcour was well acquainted with both officials. He introduced them to Dr. Worth and placed at their disposal such information as he had gained while waiting for them to arrive.

The department’s experts automatically began to function at once. A photographer was already arranging his apparatus to make pictures of the body from as many angles as its position in the cupboard would permit. A fingerprint man went about his duties along the lines laid down by established routine. The medical examiner and Dr. Worth gravitated naturally together and plunged into a discussion of Endicott’s medical history.

===Chinese===
沃思医生有节制地缓和下来。“您要明白，先生，我对他的心脏了如指掌。两年前他得过一次神经衰弱，把心脏的功能拖垮了。”他解开恩迪科特的大衣，以及那件半软前襟衬衫上一颗颗黑珍珠衬衫扣。他听了一会儿，随即取下听诊器。“没有丝毫迹象。”他说。“他已经死了。要我把衬衫前襟和外衣重新扣上吗？”

“不必了，医生。”

大厅那扇门猛地打开。凶杀科主管和法医走了进来，身后跟着一队侦探。瓦尔考中尉与这两位官员都相熟。他把他们介绍给沃思医生，并把自己在等候他们到来期间所获得的那些情况，悉数提供给他们参考。

局里的各路专家立刻像上了发条似地各司其职。一名摄影人员已经在架设器材，准备就尸体在衣橱中所处的位置尽可能从多个角度拍摄。一名指纹人员循着既定规程的条条框框着手履行自己的职责。法医和沃思医生自然而然地凑到一处，深入探讨起恩迪科特的病史来。

===Original===
The homicide chief, a well-built, alert-looking man of fifty, by the name of Andrews, drew Lieutenant Valcour a little to one side.

“What do you really make of it, Valcour?” he said.

“Oh, it’s undoubtedly murder, Chief, but I doubt whether there’ll even be an indictment unless we get a lucky break, establish a definite motive, and get a confession.”

“I feel that way about it, too. Any signs of an entry having been forced?”

“I haven’t looked. I’ve been in here all the time, and my men just came.”

“Well, Stevens and Larraby are making the rounds now. They’ll let us know. If the autopsy doesn’t show poison or some wound it’ll be a nuisance. If it’s a straight heart attack, as Dr. Worth claims, we might just as well drop it. Can you imagine getting up before a jury that’s been shown a picture by the defense of a big husky like Endicott and saying, ‘This man was scared to his death?’ Suppose a woman was the defendant. They’d laugh the case out of court.”

“Maybe it won’t be as bad as all that, Chief. While you’re busy in here I’ll wander around and try to scare up something. Would you mind sending for me when the medical examiner reaches some decision as to the manner of death?”

“Sure thing, Valcour. I’ll see to it, too, that those brushes and comb are looked into.”

“I’ll probably be in Mrs. Endicott’s room. That’s the door just across the corridor.”

Andrews was aware of Lieutenant Valcour’s reputation in the department for the painless extraction of useful information from people. “Go to it,” he said. “And squeeze every drop that you can.”

===Chinese===
凶杀科主管是个五十岁、体格结实、神色机警的人，名叫安德鲁斯（Andrews），他把瓦尔考中尉拉到稍偏的一旁。

“你究竟怎么看，瓦尔考？”他说。

“哦，毫无疑问是谋杀，长官，可我怀疑，除非我们撞上好运、坐实一个确凿的动机、再拿到一份口供，否则怕是连起诉都立不起来。”

“我也是这么想的。有强行闯入的迹象吗？”

“我还没查。我一直待在这儿，我手下的人也才到。”

“好吧，史蒂文斯和拉拉比正在各处巡查。他们会通知我们的。要是尸检既验不出毒、也找不到什么伤口，那可就麻烦了。倘若真如沃思医生所言，是一次单纯的心脏病发作，那我们倒不如干脆撒手。你能想象自己站到陪审团面前——辩方已经把恩迪科特那样一条大汉的照片给他们看过——然后说：‘这人是被活活吓死的’吗？要是被告还是个女人。他们会把这案子当成笑话，笑出法庭去的。”

“也许不至于那么糟，长官。您在这儿忙的时候，我就四处转转，看能不能搜出点什么。等法医对死因的判定有了结论，麻烦您派人叫我一声？”

“没问题，瓦尔考。那些刷子和梳子，我也会让人去查一查。”

“我大概会在恩迪科特夫人房里。就是走廊正对面那扇门。”

安德鲁斯清楚瓦尔考中尉在局里素有口碑，擅从人嘴里无痛地套出有用的情报。“去吧，”他说，“能榨多少，就榨多少出来。”

## IV 10:02 p.m.—Pale Flares the Darkness / 四 晚十时零二分——幽光破暗

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour wondered concerning Mrs. Endicott as he walked slowly across the corridor and knocked on the door of her room. A curious, curious woman, with youth and beauty that almost passed belief. He knew her instinctively as one of life’s misfits: complex to a note far beyond the common tune; essentially an individualist; essentially unhappy from an inevitable loneliness which is the lot of all who are banished within the narrow confines of their own complexity; a type he had seldom met, but of whose existence he was well aware.

Roberts opened the door. The woman’s face was butchered and her eyes had the quality of glass.

“Ask Mrs. Endicott, please, whether she feels strong enough to see me for a moment.”

Mrs. Endicott’s voice was definitely metallic. As it reached him in the corridor, disembodied from any visual association with herself, it seemed to hold a muted echo of brass bells.

“Certainly, come in. I wish, Lieutenant, you would give up the tiresome fiction that I am going to collapse. I’ll ring, Roberts, when I want you.”

“Yes, madam.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉缓步穿过走廊，叩响恩迪科特夫人房门的时候，心里仍在揣度着她。一个古怪、古怪的女人，年轻与美貌近乎令人难以置信。他凭直觉便认出，她是生活中那种与周遭格格不入的人物：复杂到一个远远超出寻常曲调的音符；骨子里是个个人主义者；骨子里也不快乐，源于一种无可避免的孤独——这种孤独，是所有被放逐在自身那狭隘而错综的内心牢笼里之人共同的宿命；这是他鲜少遇见的一类人，可他深知世上有这样一种人存在。

罗伯茨（Roberts）开了门。这女人的脸像是被屠宰过一般，眼睛则带着一种玻璃般的质地。

“请问恩迪科特夫人，她可还有气力见我片刻吗？”

恩迪科特夫人的声音透着一种确凿的金属感。当这声音传到站在走廊里的他耳中、与她的形貌脱离了任何视觉上的关联时，仿佛裹挟着一缕被压低了的铜铃声。

“当然，请进。中尉，我但愿你能舍弃那套叫我快要垮掉的乏味假想。罗伯茨，需要你的时候我会按铃。”

“是，夫人。”

===Original===
As Roberts passed him on her way to the door Lieutenant Valcour felt an imperative awareness of an attempt at revelations—an attempt to impart to him some special knowledge. Her eyes, as she glanced at him, lost their cobwebs and grew sharply informative. It was entirely an unconscious reaction on his part that forced from his lips the word “Later.” The cobwebs reappeared. She left the room.

Lieutenant Valcour drew a chair close to the chaise longue upon which Mrs. Endicott was nervously lying. Flung across her knees was a robe of China silk, a black river bearing on its surface huge flowers done in silver and slashed at its fringes with the jade chiffon of her dress. He launched his campaign by first swinging, wordily, well wide of its ultimate objective. His tone, from a deliberate casual friendliness, was an anodyne to possible reservations, or fears.

“It is the tragedy of a detective’s life,” he said pleasantly, “that the sudden slender contact he has with a case affords such a useless background for human behaviour. You can see what I mean, Mrs. Endicott. Were I you, or some intimate friend either of yourself or of your husband, I would already be in possession of the countless little threads that have woven the pattern of Mr. Endicott’s life for the past five or ten years. You’ll forgive me for outraging oratory? It’s a nasty habit I’ve contracted in later years whenever dealing with the abstract. I’m not making a speech, really. What I’m trying to express is that in that background, that pattern of Mr. Endicott’s life, one thread or series of interrelated threads would stand out pretty plainly as the reason why someone should wish to kill him.”

===Chinese===
罗伯茨从他身旁经过、向门口走去时，瓦尔考中尉强烈地觉察到，对方正竭力想要透露些什么——想要传达给他某种特别的知识。她瞥他一眼，眼中的蛛网般的迷蒙倏然褪去，变得锋利而意味深长。他几乎是出于全然无意识的反应，从唇间挤出了一个词：“回头。”蛛网重新结起。她退出了房间。

瓦尔考中尉把椅子拉近恩迪科特夫人正紧张地躺卧着的那张睡榻。她膝上横搭着一件中国绸的晨袍，像一条黑色的河流，面上浮着硕大的银线绣花，流苏处则与她衣裙的翠绿雪纺交错相裁。他开启了这番试探——头一步便是滔滔不绝地、远远地绕开最终的目标打转。他那刻意为之的、随和友善的语调，是对她可能心存的一切戒备或恐惧的一剂安抚。

“侦探一生的悲剧在于，”他愉悦地说，“他与一桩案件之间那点突兀而单薄的接触，对于解读人的行为而言，所提供的背景实在太过无用。您明白我的意思，恩迪科特夫人。倘若我是您，或者是您或您丈夫的某位密友，那我早就已经掌握那无数细小的丝线——正是它们，在过去的五年或十年间，织就了恩迪科特先生生活的图样。您会原谅我这番冒犯了演说辞的话吧？这是近年来我沾染上的一个坏毛病，每逢处理抽象之事便要发作。我并不是当真在发表演说。我想表达的不过是：在那段背景——也就是恩迪科特先生生活的那幅图样里——总会有某一条丝线，或一连串相互交织的丝线，会相当显眼地凸显出来，成为某人想要取他性命的缘由。”

===Original===
“I,” said Mrs. Endicott, “have several times wished to kill him.”

Lieutenant Valcour nodded. “There is nothing left for me but the trite things to say about marriage. And trite things, after all, are the true things, don’t you think?”

“If they’re just discovered. I mean by that, that to the person just discovering their deadly aptness they’re true. Rather terribly so sometimes.”

“But the aptness wears off with usage?”

Mrs. Endicott’s slender hand and arm were models of quietness in motion as she reached for a cigarette. “Everything wears off with usage,” she said. “Love quicker than anything else.”

“But it doesn’t wear off completely, love doesn’t, ever.”

Mrs. Endicott looked at him sharply. “Why are you a detective?” she said.

===Chinese===
“我，”恩迪科特夫人说，“就有好几次恨不得杀了他。”

瓦尔考中尉点了点头。“剩下的，我也只能说说关于婚姻的那些老生常谈了。而老生常谈，归根结底，也就是真话，您说是不是？”

“若是刚刚才发现的话。我的意思是，对那个刚刚才发觉它们那种致命的贴切的人来说，它们才算是真。有时甚至真得相当骇人。”

“可这种贴切会随着反复使用而磨钝的吧？”

恩迪科特夫人探身去取一支烟，她那纤细的手与臂是动静间克制的典范。“任何东西都会随着反复使用而磨钝，”她说，“爱情磨钝得比任何东西都更快。”

“可它并不会被磨得荡然无存，爱情不会，永远不会。”

恩迪科特夫人锐利地看他一眼。“你为什么要当侦探？”她说。

===Original===
“The accident of birth—of environment. Only geniuses, you know, ever quite escape those two fatalities. My parents emigrated from France to Canada, where my father held a certain reputation in my present profession. My parents died. There was enough money to secure an education at McGill—one had contacts here in the States…” Lieutenant Valcour smiled infectiously. “I reversed Caesar in that I came, was seen, was conquered.”

Mrs. Endicott was amused. “How utterly conceited.”

“Isn’t it?”

The smile vanished from her face with the peculiar suddenness of some conjuring trick. She veered abruptly. “What are they doing in my husband’s room now?” she said.

“Dr. Worth and the medical examiner are determining the cause of death.” Lieutenant Valcour transferred his attention to a Sargent watercolour above the mantel. “Dr. Worth has already expressed the opinion that it was heart failure,” he said.

Mrs. Endicott offered no immediate comment. She withdrew, for a moment, into some private chamber, and her voice was rather expressionless when she spoke. “But that isn’t murder.”

“It could be—if the disease itself were used as a weapon.”

“I don’t believe that I understand.”

“Why, if some person who knew that Mr. Endicott was subject to heart attacks were deliberately to shock or scare him suddenly, or even give him a not especially forceful blow over the heart, and he were to die as a result of any one of those things, that would be murder. It would have to be proved pretty conclusively, of course, that it had been done deliberately.”

===Chinese===
“身世使然——环境使然。你也知道，唯有天才，才能完全逃得过这两桩宿命。我的父母从法国移居加拿大，在那里，我父亲在我如今这门行当里颇有几分名气。后来父母相继过世。留下的钱足以供我到麦吉尔大学（McGill）求学——在美国这边又有些人脉……”瓦尔考中尉一笑，那笑意颇能动人。“我倒过来重演了凯撒那一套：我来，我被看见，我被征服。”

恩迪科特夫人被逗乐了。“何其自负。”

“不是吗？”

笑容从她脸上消失，那种特有的猝然，像极了某一种戏法。她话锋陡然一转。“他们这会儿在我丈夫房里做什么？”她说。

“沃思医生和法医正在确定死因。”瓦尔考中尉把注意力转到壁炉上方一幅萨金特（Sargent）水彩画上。“沃思医生已经表示过，那是心力衰竭。”他说。

恩迪科特夫人并未立即作答。她退入了一处私密的内室片刻，待她再开口时，声音相当平淡。“可那不是谋杀。”

“也有可能是——如果那病症本身被人当作武器来用的话。”

“我想我并未听明白。”

“这样想，倘若有个清楚恩迪科特先生易发心疾的人，蓄意地骤然惊吓他、恐吓他，甚至并不怎么用力地在他心脏处击上一记，而他因此便一命呜呼，那便是谋杀。当然，必须相当确凿地证明，这一切是蓄意为之。”

===Original===
Mrs. Endicott joined him in his continued inspection of the Sargent. “It would indicate a rather circumscribed field for suspects, too, don’t you think?”

“Yes. One would confine one’s suspicions to those who were intimate enough with him to know of his physical condition. But apart from all that phase, there are those things we technically speak of as ‘attendant circumstances.’ They point to murder.”

Their glances brushed for a second in passing and then parted.

“Such as?”

Lieutenant Valcour explained, with certain reservations. “The note you showed me—the position of Mr. Endicott in the cupboard—the fact that he is completely dressed for out of doors, but there is no trace of his hat—oh, several little things that speak quite plainly.” He focused her directly. “Where did Mr. Endicott usually keep his hats?”

“I’ve never noticed particularly. There’s a cupboard downstairs in the entrance hall, and of course the one—”

“Yes, I’ve looked for it up here. I wonder whether you’d care to tell me what happened—what you did, I mean, and what you remember of Mr. Endicott’s movements from the time, say, of his reaching home this afternoon.”

Mrs. Endicott’s face sought refuge in the very pith of candour. “Why, nothing much—nothing unusual.”

===Chinese===
恩迪科特夫人也加入他，继续端详那幅萨金特。“这同样意味着嫌疑人的范围相当有限，你不觉得吗？”

“是的。人们会把怀疑收拢到那些与他亲密到知晓他身体状况的人身上。可除了这一层之外，还有一些我们技术上称之为‘伴随情形’的东西。它们指向的是谋杀。”

两人的目光交错擦过一瞬，旋即各自移开。

“比方说？”

瓦尔考中尉有所保留地解释道。“你给我看的那张字条——恩迪科特先生在衣橱里的位置——他整套出门的装束齐备，却独独不见他的帽子——哦，好些细小之处都说得相当明白。”他直视着她。“恩迪科特先生平日把帽子搁在哪里？”

“我没怎么留意过。楼下门厅里有个衣橱，当然还有那一个——”

“是，我在楼上这一带找过了。不知你是否愿意把经过告诉我——我的意思是，你做了些什么，还有，就你记忆所及，从恩迪科特先生今天下午到家那时起，他都有过哪些举动。”

恩迪科特夫人的脸躲进了坦荡二字的最深处寻求庇护。“怎么，没什么大不了的——没什么不寻常的。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour laughed pleasantly. “That is where I fail in my background,” he said. “The things done were usual to both of you and therefore of no importance. To me, however, they would prove interesting because of their unfamiliarity. Did you talk at all?”

“Elaborately.”

“I beg your pardon?”

“I said elaborately. Herbert makes a point of talking elaborately whenever he’s lying.”

“I see—he was lying, then, about Marge Myles.”

“And unoriginally. But Herbert never was original, much, in his emotions. He told me he was going to an impromptu reunion of some men in his class at the Yale Club. These reunions have occurred with astonishing regularity once a week for the past month, in spite of their impromptu character. I detest having my intelligence insulted,” she ended, not unfiercely, “more than anything else in the world.”

“You will forgive me for becoming personal, but I doubt whether Mr. Endicott understood you very well.”

“He didn’t understand me at all.”

“And you, him?”

Mrs. Endicott momentarily disarranged the perfect arch of her eyebrows. “I could see through him perfectly,” she said. “A child could see through him. But understand him? I don’t think anyone could understand Herbert. He made a fetish of reticence. He was,” she concluded, “half animal.”

“And the other half rather cloudily complex?”

“A fog.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉愉快地笑了起来。“这正是我背景不足之处，”他说，“你们俩习以为常的举动，于彼此而言自然无关紧要。可对我而言，正因为陌生，反倒会显得颇有意思。你们之间交谈了吗？”

“煞有介事地交谈了。”

“你说什么？”

“我说煞有介事地交谈了。赫伯特每逢撒谎，必是煞有介事。”

“我明白了——那么，关于玛吉·迈尔斯的事，他是在撒谎。”

“而且撒得毫无新意。可赫伯特在情感上向来也没什么新意可言。他告诉我，他要去耶鲁俱乐部参加他那个年级几位同窗的一场临时聚会。过去一个月里，这类聚会以惊人的规律一周一次地举行着，尽管它们号称临时。我最恨的，”她末了说，语气并不算不凌厉，“莫过于让人侮辱我的智力，这甚于世上任何一件事。”

“你肯原谅我触及私事才好，可我怀疑恩迪科特先生是否很了解你。”

“他压根儿就不了解我。”

“那你呢，了解他吗？”

恩迪科特夫人一时打乱了她那两道完美拱形的眉。“我能把他看个透，”她说，“一个孩子都能看透他。可了解他？我想没有人能了解赫伯特。他把缄默奉为神明。他呀，”她下了结论，“一半是禽兽。”

“另一半则相当混沌复杂？”

“一团迷雾。”

===Original===
“And when he came home this afternoon at five?”

“Five-thirty—nearer six, even.”

“Toward six, he joined you in the living room and gave you the weekly excuse.”

“I didn’t say the living room. It was the top floor—you may have noticed that this house has a peaked roof—in what would correspond in the country to an attic—” She stopped sharply, and her defensive veneer cracked for an instant, long enough to show that she was definitely startled. “I—”

“You feel that you shouldn’t have told me that. Perhaps you shouldn’t. If the fact of your having met Mr. Endicott in the attic has nothing to do with the case at all, it will cause us to snoop around among your personal affairs unnecessarily.”

“He didn’t ‘meet’ me there, as you say. He—I don’t know why he came up there. I never will know why.”

“You didn’t ask him?”

Mrs. Endicott forced Lieutenant Valcour’s full attention by the almost startling intentness of her eyes. “There has never been a direct question put or answered between Herbert and me during the whole period of our married or unmarried life,” she said. “My hold on him was the static perfection of my features and a running, superficial smartness in attitude and mind that passed for intellect. His hold on me was that I loved him.”

“Even when you wished to kill him?”

“I suppose even then. Mind you, I never wished him dead—there’s a difference.”

===Chinese===
“那么他今天下午五点钟回到家时呢？”

“五点半——甚至快六点了。”

“将近六点时，他到起居室与你会合，给了你那每周一次的托词。”

“我并没有说是起居室。是在顶层——你或许留意到了，这幢房子有一个尖顶屋顶——在那个乡间相当于阁楼的地方——”她陡然止住，那层防备的外壳裂开了一瞬，足以让人看出她确凿无疑地吃了一惊。“我——”

“你觉得这话本不该告诉我。或许确实不该。倘若你在阁楼上与恩迪科特先生相遇这件事与本案毫无瓜葛，它只会引得我们不必多事地在你那些私事里头打探。”

“他并不是如你所说到那里与我‘会合’的。他——我不知道他为什么上那里去。我也永远不会知道为什么。”

“你没有问过他？”

恩迪科特夫人以一种近乎惊人的专注盯住他，逼得他不得不全神贯注。“在赫伯特与我之间，无论婚前的整个日子还是婚后的整个日子，从来没有过一句直截了当的提问，也没有过一句直截了当的回答，”她说，“我攥住他的，是我五官那种静止般的完美，是举手投足与心智之间一路流淌的、浮于表面的那份机灵——它被人当作了才智。他攥住我的，则是我爱他。”

“即便在你恨不得杀他的时候？”

“我想即便那时候也是。告诉你，我从来没有盼着他死——这其间的分别，你是懂的。”

===Original===
“Oh, quite.” Lieutenant Valcour smiled engagingly. “You often felt like killing him, but you wanted it to stop right there.”

“You know, I wish you’d come to tea sometime—” Mrs. Endicott’s eyes contracted sharply. Her voice became a definite apology, not to Lieutenant Valcour, but as though its message were being sent along obscure and private channels to some port where it would find her husband. “There are moments,” she said, “when you make me forget.”

“Forgetting isn’t a sin. That’s natural. It’s not loving—being mentally hurtful—that’s a sin. There isn’t any word exactly for what I mean. Did you both stay in the attic and go through the trunk together, or whatever it was you were going through?”

Mrs. Endicott smiled as if at some secret knowledge. “I wasn’t going through a trunk,” she said.

“No? I just mentioned it, as nine times out of ten that’s what people do in attics.”

“And the tenth customary thing,” said Mrs. Endicott, reaching for a cigarette, “is suicide.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那是。”瓦尔考中尉讨喜地一笑。“你时常有杀他的冲动，可你想要的不过是适可而止。”

“你知道，我真盼着你哪天能来喝下午茶——”恩迪科特夫人的眼睛猛地一缩。她的声音化作了一句确凿的致歉——那致歉并非向着瓦尔考中尉，倒仿佛这番话是沿着某条幽暗而私密的通道，送往某个港口，而她的丈夫将会在那里收到它。“有些时刻，”她说，“你让我忘了一切。”

“忘并不是罪过。那是人之常情。不爱——在心思上伤害对方——那才是罪过。我要说的那种意思，世上还没有一个贴切的词。你们俩都留在阁楼里，一道翻看了那只箱子，或者无论你们当时在翻什么吗？”

恩迪科特夫人笑了，仿佛怀揣着某种隐秘的知晓。“我没有在翻什么箱子。”她说。

“没有吗？我不过随口一提，因为十之八九，人们在阁楼里干的就是这个。”

“而那第十件寻常事，”恩迪科特夫人伸手去取一支烟，说道，“是自杀。”

## V 10:17 p.m.—Living or Dead? / 五 晚十时十七分——是死是活？

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour’s eyes narrowed slightly. He had a habit of dividing suicides into two classes—those who talked about killing themselves, and those who did so. He knew that the two rarely overlapped. He felt a shocking conviction that in Mrs. Endicott’s case she might well have been the exception which proved the rule. “I suppose an attic is the conventional place for suicide,” he said. “Or at least to think about it.”

Mrs. Endicott’s laugh was without humour. “One doesn’t need an attic in order to think about it.”

“That’s true. And so you went downstairs with him, then?”

“He followed me in here. That is,” she corrected herself with noticeable carelessness, “we went into the living room and he wondered, while he kissed me, whether I’d mind very much being alone for dinner. I doubt whether you’ve ever experienced, Lieutenant, the rather perfect torture of a, well, an abstract kiss. Men don’t.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉的眼睛微微眯了起来。他有个习惯，把自杀者分成两类——一类是把自杀挂在嘴上的人，一类是当真动手的人。他知道这两类人鲜有重叠。他却生出一种令人心惊的笃信：恩迪科特夫人这一案，她极有可能正是那证明规则的例外。“我想，阁楼是自杀的寻常去处吧，”他说，“至少也是动这种念头的寻常去处。”

恩迪科特夫人的笑全无笑意。“要动这种念头，倒未必非得有个阁楼。”

“这话不假。那么，您是和他一起下楼的？”

“他随我进了这里。也就是说，”她以一种分明的漫不经心更正道，“我们进了起居室，他一边吻我，一边问我介不介意一个人独自用晚餐。中尉，我怀疑您是否曾领教过那般堪称完美的酷刑——嗯，一个抽象的吻。男人们没有。”

===Original===
“We’re too self-centred, I’m afraid, or conceited or something, or else our sensibilities aren’t refined enough to be hurt by it.”

“But you could understand—if you could vision the background?”

“Everybody knows what love is, Mrs. Endicott.”

“That’s just it—it’s the comparison of what is with what has been. It’s an indescribably vulgar subject—kissing—but it’s either very wonderful or very painful. People who claim it can be a combination talk nonsense. We can eliminate, of course—”

“Of course—‘petting’ they call it, or did. You never know from one minute to the next just what a thing is being called. And then he went to his room to dress?”

===Chinese===
“恐怕是我们太自我中心了，要么就是太自负什么的，再不然，就是我们的敏感还不够细腻，不至于为此受伤。”

“可你能够体谅的——倘若你能想见那番底细？”

“谁都知道爱情是什么，恩迪科特夫人。”

“问题恰恰在这儿——那是现状与往昔的较量。接吻这题目，俗不可耐得难以形容，可它要么美妙无比，要么痛苦万分。那些声称二者可以兼而有之人，尽在胡说八道。我们当然可以把那一种排除——”

“当然——“调情”他们这么叫，至少从前这么叫。你永远也说不准一个东西下一刻又被叫成什么。随后他就回自己房里更衣去了？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

“Alone?”

“Certainly.”

“Has he a valet?”

“Herbert? Heavens, no.”

“And you dressed?”

“Yes.”

“Roberts helped you?”

“Of course.”

“Then when Mr. Endicott said goodbye?”
