“He called it through the closed door.”

===Chinese===
“是的。”

“独自一人？”

“当然。”

“他有贴身男仆吗？”

“赫伯特？天哪，没有。”

“那您更衣呢？”

“是的。”

“罗伯茨伺候您？”

“当然。”

“那么恩迪科特先生道别的时候呢？”

“他是隔着关着的门喊的。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour almost visibly showed his surprise. “He did say goodbye?”

“Herbert insists upon saying goodbye. He rapped on the door and called in. If it would interest you to know his exact words,” she said bitterly, “they were in the falsetto voice he uses when he thinks he’s being especially funny and were, ‘Don’t be angry with Herbie-werbie, sweetheart. Goodie-byskie.’”

“They’re almost a motive in themselves,” said Lieutenant Valcour, smiling. “Which door did he rap on, Mrs. Endicott?”

“The hall door.”

“I see. And you heard him going down the stairs?”

“One can’t hear footsteps with the door closed.”

“And that was at—?”

“The clock over there on my mantel was striking seven.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉几乎是肉眼可见地露出了惊讶。“他当真道了别？”

“赫伯特执意要道别。他叩了叩门，朝里喊了一声。您若有兴趣知道他的原话，”她苦涩地说，“那是用一种假嗓喊出来的——每当他自以为格外风趣时便会用那假嗓——喊的是：‘别生小赫赫的气呀，心肝儿。拜拜斯基。’”

“这话本身几乎就可算作一个杀人动机了。”瓦尔考中尉微笑着说。“他叩的是哪一扇门，恩迪科特夫人？”

“门厅那扇门。”

“我明白了。那您听见他下楼去了？”

“门关着，是听不见脚步声的。”

“那是在——？”

“我那边壁炉台上的钟正敲着七点。”

===Original===
“And after that there is nothing further you can tell me about Mr. Endicott?”

“Nothing.”

“You dined. You went to his room. You found the note. You began to worry, and then you called us up.”

“That is it.”

“Was it in this room here or up in the attic, Mrs. Endicott, that you told him you were going to kill him?”

“Here, after he—That wasn’t exactly fair, was’t it?”

“Heavens no, but awfully smart.” Lieutenant Valcour’s smile was the essence of pleasantness. “I do wish you’d continue with the ‘after he.’ After he did what? Or was it something he said?”

===Chinese===
“那之后，关于恩迪科特先生您就没有别的能告诉我的了？”

“没有了。”

“您用了晚餐。您去了他的房间。您发现了那张字条。您开始担忧，于是您给我们打了电话。”

“就是这样。”

“您告诉他您要杀他，是在这间房里，还是在楼上的阁楼里，恩迪科特夫人？”

“在这里，在他之后——这话可不怎么公道，是吧？”

“天哪，是不公道，却精明得很。”瓦尔考中尉的笑容，是和悦二字的化身。“我真心希望您把那句“在他之后”说下去。在他做什么之后？还是他说了什么？”

===Original===
“Did.”

“Yes?”

“I told you,” she blazed, “that he was half animal. You can hardly expect me to become more explicit.”

Lieutenant Valcour was genuinely upset. “I do beg your pardon, Mrs. Endicott,” he said. “About this afternoon, were you in the house?”

“Partly. I had tea at the Ritz, early, about four-thirty—with,” she added defiantly, “a man.”

“Ah.”

“Exactly so. That will permit you to reverse another tradition and go *cherchez l’homme*.”

Lieutenant Valcour found instant good humour. “So you decided to fight fire with fire,” he said.

“If you care to call it that.”

===Chinese===
“做了什么。”

“是吗？”

“我跟你说过了，”她勃然道，“他一半是禽兽。你总不能指望我说得更露骨。”

瓦尔考中尉是当真过意不去。“我恳请您原谅，恩迪科特夫人，”他说，“今天下午这一段时间，您在宅子里吗？”

“有一阵子在。我早早地在里兹用了下午茶，大约四点半——和，”她挑衅似地补充道，“一个男人。”

“啊。”

“正是如此。这下您可以颠倒另一条老例，去“找那个男人”了。”

瓦尔考中尉登时来了兴致。“原来是您决定以火攻火。”他说。

“您乐意这么叫，也未尝不可。”

===Original===
“Just who is Marge Myles, and what?” Lieutenant Valcour said suddenly.

“There are several terms one might apply to her. They all mean the same thing. I believe that recently, however,” Mrs. Endicott said very distinctly, “she has lost her amateur standing.”

“Recently?”

“The past year or so.”

“Mr. Endicott had known her as long as that?”

“Until the past month or two my husband had not known her at all. He’d heard of her, of course, and so had I.”

“Then she is a woman who once had position?”

“She was the wife of one of Herbert’s friends, a man who died two years ago and left her penniless. They say, incidentally, that she killed him.”

===Chinese===
“玛吉·迈尔斯究竟是何许人，干什么的？”瓦尔考中尉蓦地问。

“可以安在她头上的称呼有好几个。它们的意思都一样。不过我相信，近来，”恩迪科特夫人字斟句酌地说，“她已经失了业余的身份。”

“近来？”

“过去一年左右。”

“恩迪科特先生认识她有那么久了？”

“直到一两个月前，我丈夫根本就不认识她。当然，他听说过她，我也听说过。”

“那么，她曾是个有身份的女人？”

“她是赫伯特一个朋友的妻子，那人两年前死了，一文不名地把她撇下。顺便说一句，人家都说，是她害死了他。”

===Original===
“Killed him?”

“It was just gossip, of course. They had a camp near some obscure lake up in Maine. The canoe they were in one evening upset. Harry Myles couldn’t swim.”

“And Marge Myles?”

“Marge Myles was famous for her swimming.”

“Then the inference is that she, well, neglected to save her husband?”

“That—and that she deliberately upset the canoe. I repeat it’s all gossip. People dropped him, you see, after he married her. That’s a commentary for you.”

“You mean they still accepted him while he was—that is, before the ceremony.”

===Chinese===
“害死了他？”

“当然，那不过是流言。他们在缅因那边一个无名小湖附近有个营地。一天傍晚，他们乘的独木舟翻了。哈里·迈尔斯不会游泳。”

“那玛吉·迈尔斯呢？”

“玛吉·迈尔斯却以善游著称。”

“那么言外之意就是，她，嗯，见死不救，没有去救自己的丈夫？”

“那是——而且她还是故意把独木舟弄翻的。我再说一遍，这全是流言。您瞧，他娶了她之后，人家便不再与他来往。这本身就可作一个注脚。”

“您的意思是，在他还——也就是说，在那场仪式之前，人家仍然接纳他。”

===Original===
“Yes, while he was living with her. It’s thoroughly natural, of course. People didn’t have to recognize her then; they could ignore her. But you can’t ignore a man’s wife; you either have to recognize her or not. The nots had it. If she had been a genuinely nice person, or an amusing one, I doubt whether the fact of their having lived together really would have mattered. But she wasn’t.”

“What was she before her marriage?”

“A member of that much-maligned group known as the chorus.”

“And recently she had got in touch with your husband?”

“She looked up all of Harry’s old friends. Don’t you see? As a widow she again had a standing—a shade higher, but similar to the one she held before Harry married her. I don’t know how many others she landed, but she certainly landed Herbert.”

===Chinese===
“是的，在他与她同居的时候。这自然是天经地义的事。那时候人家不必承认她；尽可以当她不存在。可你不能无视一个男人的妻子；你要么承认她，要么不承认。结果是不承认的那一派占了上风。倘若她是个真正讨人喜欢的人，或者是个有趣的人，我倒怀疑他们同居过这件事是否真的会有什么要紧。可她偏偏不是。”

“她婚前是干什么的？”

“属于那个饱受诟病的群体——歌舞团女郎。”

“那么近来她和您丈夫搭上了？”

“她把哈里所有的旧友都找了出来。您还不明白？做了寡妇之后，她又有了身份——比从前略高那么一点，却与哈里娶她之前她所有的身份相仿。我不知道她还勾搭上了别的多少人，但赫伯特她是着实勾搭上了。”

===Original===
“And you were afraid she would do something to him?”

“Well, she killed Harry.”

“Then you personally believe the gossip?”

Mrs. Endicott did not bother to give a direct reply. She shrugged, and twisted a little on the chaise longue.

“And do you associate her in any way, Mrs. Endicott, with what has happened here tonight?”

She continued to evade further direct responsibility for an opinion. “Who else?” she said.

“But the actual mechanics of it, Mrs. Endicott—how could she have got into the house?”

“It could be done. Herbert himself might have let her in.”

“That’s going a little far, isn’t it?”

“Yes. It was rotten of me to suggest it. I never really thought it, Lieutenant. I just said it.”

===Chinese===
“而您怕她会对他下手？”

“毕竟，她杀了哈里。”

“那么您个人是信了那些流言？”

恩迪科特夫人懒得直接作答。她耸了耸肩，在睡榻上微微扭动了一下。

“那您是否把她和今夜这儿发生的事，以任何方式联系在一起，恩迪科特夫人？”

她继续躲闪着不肯为一种看法承担直接的责。“除了她还能是谁？”她说。

“可这事实际的门道，恩迪科特夫人——她怎么可能进得了这宅子？”

“这是办得到的。赫伯特本人可能就放她进来过。”

“这话未免过了一点，是不是？”

“是的。我提这个，未免太下作了。我从没有当真这么想过，中尉。我不过是随口一说。”

===Original===
“And after all, Mrs. Endicott, why should she want to kill your husband? You weren’t trying to keep him from her.”

“He might have been trying to keep himself from her.”

“He might. It’s stretching it a little, though, to think she’d deliberately kill him for that.”

“She wouldn’t do it deliberately.”

“I don’t know. When a woman starts out to kill she invariably chooses some weapon, or a poison. Every case has proved it again and again. But we’re only speculating, aren’t we? Who was it who took you to tea?”

“I haven’t any intention of telling you.”

“Because it might involve him?”

“He couldn’t possibly be involved. If I thought he were I’d tell you in a minute.”

Someone knocked on the door.

===Chinese===
“再者，恩迪科特夫人，她到底为何要杀您丈夫？您并不曾试图把他从她身边夺走。”

“可能倒是他自己想把自己从她身边拉开。”

“也许吧。不过，要是想她竟会为此蓄意杀他，未免就扯得远了些。”

“她不会是蓄意的。”

“那可说不准。一个女人一旦起了杀心，总会挑选某件凶器，或者某种毒药。每一桩案子都一而再、再而三地印证了这一点。可我们不过是在臆测，不是吗？带您去用下午茶的那位是谁？”

“我不打算告诉您。”

“因为那会牵连到他？”

“他绝无可能被牵连进来。倘若我认为有可能，我立刻就会告诉您。”

有人敲了敲门。

===Original===
“Just the same, Mrs. Endicott, I wish you would tell me who he was.”

“No.”

Lieutenant Valcour was able not only to recognize finality, he could accept it. He considered Mrs. Endicott’s very definite refusal to answer his question as of small consequence; there were so many more ways than one for frying an eel. He stood up and crossed to the door. He opened it and stepped into the corridor, closing the door behind him. Even in the dimmish light young Cassidy’s normally ruddy face was the colour of chalk.

“What’s happened, Cassidy?”

“Honest to God, Lieutenant, I’m scared stiff. They’re getting things ready in there to bring that corpse back to life.”

===Chinese===
“纵然如此，恩迪科特夫人，我还是希望您肯告诉我他是谁。”

“不。”

瓦尔考中尉不仅能辨认出一句话的无可挽回，他还能承受得住。他把恩迪科特夫人如此断然的拒答看作无足轻重；煎鳗鱼的法子，可远不止一种。他站起身，走到门前。他拉开门，跨进走廊，随手把门带上。即便是在那昏沉沉的光线里，年轻的卡西迪那张平日红润的脸，也白得像粉笔。

“出了什么事，卡西迪（Cassidy）？”

“老天在上，中尉，我吓坏了。他们正在里头张罗着，要把那具尸首救活过来呢。”

## VI 10:32 p.m.﻿—Pictures in Dust / 六 晚十时三十二分——尘埃里的图画

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour stared for a puzzled instant at the white face.

“What do you mean, Cassidy?” he said.

“Honest to God, Lieutenant, I mean just what I say.”

“But that’s impossible.”

Cassidy went even further. “It’s sacrilege,” he said.

“Nonsense,” Lieutenant Valcour said sharply. “You have simply misunderstood Dr. Worth. It is possible that Mr. Endicott was not dead at all but in some state of catalepsy. No one, Cassidy, can bring back the dead.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉困惑地盯着那张苍白的脸看了一瞬。

“你什么意思，卡西迪（Cassidy）？”他说。

“对天发誓，中尉，我说的就是字面上的意思。”

“可这不可能。”

卡西迪还更进一步。“这是亵渎。”他说。

“胡说，”瓦尔考中尉厉声道，“你纯粹是误解了沃思医生（Dr. Worth）。恩迪科特先生也许根本就没死，只是处于某种强直性昏厥的状态。卡西迪，没有人能把死者唤回人间。”

===Original===
“I’m glad to hear you say so, sir.”

“Then let us go in.”

“Must I go back in there, too?”

“You must. Forget the fact that you’re a superstitious Irishman, Cassidy, and remember that you’re a cop. Cops, as you’ve been told more times than one, should be noble, firm, and perpetually cool, calm, and collected.”

“Sure now, you’re kidding.”

“Tut, tut.”

“Well, and I’ll try, Lieutenant﻿—but cripes!”

“But nothing,” advised Lieutenant Valcour as he opened the door to Endicott’s room.

===Chinese===
“您能这么说，我太高兴了，长官。”

“那我们进去吧。”

“我也非得再回到那里面去不可吗？”

“你必须。忘了你自己是个迷信的爱尔兰人这回事，卡西迪，记住你是个警察。警察，别人对你说的不止一回了，应当高尚、坚定，永远冷静、镇定、从容不迫。”

“得了吧，您在开我的玩笑。”

“啧啧。”

“好吧，我会尽量的，中尉——可是我的老天！”

“没什么可是的，”瓦尔考中尉一边说着，一边推开通往恩迪科特房间的门。

===Original===
The effect was shockingly garish. All shades had been removed from their lamps, and the various details of the furnishing stood out in the painful white light brightly clear.

Andrews was alone. He stood near the bed upon which Endicott had been placed, looking in rather shocked bewilderment at the body. Lieutenant Valcour joined him. A blanket had been drawn up to Endicott’s chin, and the face which remained exposed looked very waxlike﻿—very still﻿—very much like a dead man’s indeed.

===Chinese===
屋内的景象刺眼得惊人。所有灯上的灯罩都已被摘了下来，家具陈设的每一处细节，都在那惨白的灯光下格外分明地凸显出来。

安德鲁斯（Andrews）独自一人。他站在那张安放着恩迪科特的床边，一脸颇受震动的茫然，望着那具躯体。瓦尔考中尉走到他身旁。一条毛毯一直拉到恩迪科特的下颌，露在外面的那张脸看上去很有些像蜡做的——纹丝不动——的的确确像是个死人的脸。

===Original===
“This is the damnedest thing, Valcour.”

“What is, Chief?”

“They say there’s a chance that this man isn’t dead. Worth is going to operate.”

“Operate? But Dr. Worth himself admitted that the heart had stopped beating after testing with a stethoscope. What sort of an operation?”

“Worth’s going to inject adrenaline into the cardiac muscles.”

“I wonder just how much value there is in that stuff.”

“Well, unless Endicott’s been poisoned, the medical examiner and Worth both seem to think there’s a chance. They feel there’s no harm in trying, anyway. It sounds silly to me, but they reminded me of that recent case in Queens﻿—you probably read about it﻿—where a man had been pronounced dead for six hours and was revived. Of course, they said he wasn’t really dead, just as they now think that Endicott may not be really dead. No one can bring back the dead.”

===Chinese===
“这事真是蹊跷透顶，瓦尔考。”

“什么蹊跷，长官？”

“他们说这人有还没死的可能。沃思打算动手术。”

“动手术？可沃思医生本人用听诊器测过之后，都承认心脏已经停止跳动了。什么样的手术？”

“沃思打算往心肌里注射肾上腺素。”

“我倒想知道那玩意儿究竟有多大用处。”

“呃，除非恩迪科特是中了毒，法医和沃思似乎都觉得有这个机会。他们觉得试一试总归没坏处。我听着像无稽之谈，可他们提醒我前阵子皇后区（Queens）的那桩案子——你多半在报上读到过——一个人被宣告死亡六个小时之后又给救活了。当然了，他们说他其实并没真死，正如他们现在认为恩迪科特也许并没有真死。没有人能把死者唤回人间。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour threw a bland look to Cassidy, who was standing in as convenient a position to the hall door as he could possibly get.

“They say,” Andrews went on, “that adrenaline’s been used off and on for years. Worth says they try it quite often when a baby is born ‘dead.’ Sometimes it starts the heart pumping and the baby lives.”

Lieutenant Valcour shrugged. “It will make things pretty simple for us if it works with Endicott,” he said. “He can make a statement and prefer charges himself. Where is everybody?”

“The medical examiner and Worth are downstairs telephoning and making arrangements for the operation. My men have finished and have gone back to headquarters. There wasn’t any sign of forcing an entry, so it looks like an inside job, if there was any job. I tell you, Valcour, if it wasn’t for your suggestion that robbery was a motive, or for that note that might have been a threat, I’d drop the whole thing. It’s a different matter if the adrenaline doesn’t work and an autopsy proves poison or something. Find out much from Mrs. Endicott?”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉朝卡西迪投去一个不动声色的眼神，后者正尽可能贴近通往走廊的那扇门站着。

“他们说，”安德鲁斯接着道，“肾上腺素断断续续已经用好些年了。沃思说，婴儿‘死产’的时候他们常常试这一招。有时候它能让心脏重新搏动，婴儿就活下来了。”

瓦尔考中尉耸了耸肩。“要是这对恩迪科特管用，那事情对我们来说就简单多了，”他说，“他自己就能作证，亲自提出控告。人都到哪儿去了？”

“法医和沃思在楼下打电话，为手术做安排。我的人手已经干完活，回总部去了。没有任何强行入内的迹象，所以如果真有什么‘案子’的话，看上去是内部人干的。我跟你说，瓦尔考，要不是你提出抢劫可能是动机，再加上那张也许算是恐吓的字条，我整个就撒手不管了。如果肾上腺素不起作用、解剖证明是中毒什么的，那又另当别论。你从恩迪科特夫人那儿问出多少东西？”

===Original===
“Enough to be interested in learning more. Want the details?”

“Later, if I have to get to work on the case. You want to keep on handling it?”

“Yes.”

“Go ahead. Call for any outside stuff you want us to check up on for you. I’ll send you a report on the brushes and comb as soon as they finish with them downtown.”

“You going, Chief?”

“No use in my sticking around, Valcour. We haven’t a case yet, really, that calls for any Central Office work. Hell, according to those two six-syllable specialists downstairs, we haven’t even got a corpse. Robbery there may have been, and it’s your precinct﻿—so go to it. I’ll find out from the medical examiner when he gets back how the operation turned out, and if there’s going to be an autopsy. If poisoning is proved and you haven’t pinned it on anyone by then, I’ll get on the job again. I suppose you’ll see that the people in the house are given the once-over?”

“Certainly, Chief.”

“I’ll run along then. Good luck, Valcour.”

“Thank you, Chief.”

Andrews left the room and closed the door.

===Chinese===
“够让我有兴致进一步查下去了。要听细节吗？”

“回头再说，如果我得接手办这案子的话。你想接着管这事？”

“是的。”

“放手去办吧。你要我们替你核实什么外围的材料，尽管开口。那些刷子和梳子，等总局的人检验完，我马上把报告给你送来。”

“您要走吗，长官？”

“我在这儿耗着也没用，瓦尔考。说真的，眼下的案子还没到需要总局出手的程度。见鬼，照楼下那两位满口六音节术语的专家的说法，我们连一具尸体都还算不上有。抢劫说不定是有的，而这是你的辖区——放手干吧。等法医回来，我去问问他手术的结果如何，要不要验尸。如果证实是中毒，而你到那时还没把这案子钉到什么人头上，我就重新接手。我想，屋里这些人你会让人统统盘查一遍吧？”

“当然，长官。”

“那我走了。祝你好运，瓦尔考。”

“多谢，长官。”

安德鲁斯离开了房间，带上了门。

===Original===
“I bet he’s got a date,” said Cassidy.

“He’d stay here if he had twenty dates, if he thought it was necessary,” said Lieutenant Valcour.

“Well, I wish I had a date.”

“You’ll have a whole vacation if you don’t brace up. I’m going to take a look in that cupboard, now that Endicott’s no longer in it.”

Even a cupboard seemed preferable to Cassidy to being in the room. “Can’t I help you, sir?” he said with almost fervent politeness.

“No, Cassidy, you can’t. You can stay just where you are.”

“Oh, very well, sir.”

===Chinese===
“我打赌他有约会去了。”卡西迪说。

“他要觉得有必要，就是有二十个约会也会留在这儿。”瓦尔考中尉说。

“唉，我倒希望我有个约会。”

“你要是不打起精神来，等待你的就是整整一个长假了。我要去那衣橱里瞧瞧，既然恩迪科特已经不在里面了。”

对卡西迪来说，哪怕是衣橱也比待在这间屋里强。“我就不能帮帮您吗，长官？”他近乎热切地、谦恭地说。

“不行，卡西迪，你帮不上。你就待在原地。”

“哦，那好吧，长官。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour picked up a straight-backed chair and took it into the cupboard with him. He held a sincere respect for the Central Office men, but at the same time felt that their work was too methodically routine to permit their darting along interesting tangents or wasting their time in strolls along bypaths that might lead to fertile fields. There was no criticism in his mind at all. He admired the system that had been established, and the expert functioning of its units and departments. He knew very well that its average of successes was greater than its average of failures. But it was deficient in that elusive, time-taking, and sometimes expensive thing known as the “personal equation.” It remained, at its best, a machine.

A certain amount of carelessness, too, ran in the general plan. In many cases some things were slurred over, some missed entirely. This again was not surprising when one considered that the personnel was recruited largely from the more intelligent men in the ranks. Intelligent, yes, but hardly specialists, nor could one in all fairness expect them to be.

When working on a case they functioned along two distinctly separate but parallel lines. One department of specialists handled the technical and chemical investigation of material things and clues found on the scene of the crime﻿—just as the brushes and comb were shortly to be examined by the proper men down at Central Office. A second department dealt with the human aspect﻿—examining witnesses, looking up all friends or connections of the victim; a large, competent organization that would stretch feelers, no matter how many were necessary, to every contact point of the victim’s life within the city, and from whose findings some possible motive could be established and some possible suspect or group of suspects be evolved.

The two branches would then compare notes, and if a satisfactory amount of evidence had been obtained by the technical department to establish a case against one or several of the suspects, arrests would be made or the suspects brought in for questioning. According to the temperament and station of the suspects, one of the various forms that go to make up the properly dreaded third degree would be employed and a confession obtained. The work of the Central Office would then be finished, and the case up to the prosecutor.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉端起一把直背椅，把它带进了衣橱里。他对总局的人怀有真诚的敬意，可同时又觉得，他们的工作太过按部就班，容不下他们顺着有趣的旁枝急转直下，也容不下他们把时间浪费在那些也许会通向沃野的幽径上信步闲逛。他心里全无批评之意。他钦佩这套业已建立的体系，钦佩它各个单位与部门那专家般的运转。他深知，它成功的平均比例大于失败的平均比例。但在那种被称为“个人方程”的、难以捉摸、耗费时日、有时还所费不赀的东西上，它是有所欠缺的。它再怎么好，也终究是一台机器。

整套总体方案里，也夹杂着一定程度的马虎。许多案子里，有些东西被一带而过，有些则被整个儿漏掉。考虑到这些人员大多是从基层那些较聪明的警员里选拔上来的，这倒也并不出人意料。聪明是聪明，但算不上专家，公平而论，也不能指望他们个个都是专家。

办案的时候，他们沿着两条截然分离却又并行的路子运转。一个专家部门负责对犯罪现场发现的实物与线索进行技术与化学上的检验——正如那些刷子和梳子一会儿就要由总局里对口的行家加以检验那样。第二个部门则处理人事这一面——盘问证人，查阅被害人所有的朋友或关系；这是一个庞大而胜任的组织，无论需要多少触角，都会伸到被害人在本城生活圈里的每一个接触点上，而从他们查到的结果里，可以确立某种可能的动机，进而推演出某个或某群可能的嫌疑对象。

随后两个分支会互通情况，如果技术部门已经取得了足够分量的证据，足以对其中一个或几个嫌疑人立案，便会实施逮捕，或者把嫌疑人传来问话。视嫌疑人的性情与身份地位而定，他们会动用构成那套理应令人闻风丧胆的“第三级”审讯的种种手段之一，逼出一份口供。总局的工作到此便告完成，案子移交检察官。

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour was glad that in the present instance the homicide chief had felt it useless to set in motion the machinery of the second branch until more definite developments should occur. The case interested him. Mrs. Endicott interested him﻿—her astonishing beauty, her mind, her contradictions﻿—Roberts﻿—Marge Myles﻿—three women who offered an assurance of satisfying an almost blatant curiosity he possessed for discovering the source springs of human behaviour. This talk about reviving Endicott and Endicott himself making a statement﻿—well, perhaps. But until it was accomplished he preferred to think of Endicott as a corpse, the case a definite homicide, and of possible suspects right in the house.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉很高兴，在眼下这桩案子里，凶杀科科长觉得在更确切的事态出现之前，启动第二个分支的那台机器纯属徒劳。这案子引起了他的兴趣。恩迪科特夫人引起了他的兴趣——她那惊人的美貌、她的头脑、她的自相矛盾——罗伯茨（Roberts）——玛吉·迈尔斯——三个女人，这三个人担保能让他满足，能满足他那几乎算得上直白外露的好奇心，去探究人类行为背后那眼源之泉。这番让恩迪科特起死回生、由恩迪科特本人亲口作证的议论——好吧，也许成。但在它成为事实之前，他宁可暂且把恩迪科特当作一具尸体，把这案子当作一桩确凿的凶杀，把可能的嫌疑人就放在这幢房子里去想。

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour concentrated his attention upon the cupboard. There were shelves along the back of it, the lowest one being at the height of a man’s head. Numerous suits of clothes were hanging from beneath this lowest shelf. He stood on the chair and played his flashlight along the top of it. There was nothing there but an accumulation of dust. He felt a distinct and highly satisfactory thrill when he noted that streaks showed where the dust had very recently been rubbed away, as if somebody had deliberately wiped both his hands in it. It linked with the dirty cake of soap. Andrews had said nothing about the streaks. It was pretty obvious that the Central Office men had overlooked them﻿—had casually observed that the shelves were empty and had let the matter go at that.

Lieutenant Valcour began to feel quite pleasant and informed himself gravely that a deduction was in order. For a happy moment he considered the possibility of that curious and sinister Oriental influence that crops up so perennially in the very finest of murder cases﻿—of Cassidy’s murder cases: that elusive figure swathed in gray, whitely turbanned above coffee-coloured skin, who has a penchant toward religious fanaticism the esoteric rites of which involve dust. This breath-shocking villain would ultimately be trapped by the bright detective through the wretch’s occult passion for this dust. Had one, Lieutenant Valcour wanted to know, such an enigma to deal with here? No, he informed himself sternly, one knew damned well one had not. But in the place of such a handy and beautiful deduction﻿—what?

He stared at the dust and began to see pictures in it: a crouching person tormented by hate or fear, or both, who knows that Endicott is going to open the cupboard door. What, in the name of the lighter humorists, to do? The person dreads recognition. Is there no disguise? No, curse it﻿—but yes﻿—the dust! The person’s hands are smeared, and by means of the hands, the face…

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉把注意力集中到那口衣橱上。橱里靠墙一面排着几层搁板，最低一层恰在一个成年男子头顶的高度。许多套衣裳挂在最低一层搁板的下方。他站到椅子上，用手电筒沿着搁板顶面照过去。那上面什么也没有，只有一层积灰。他注意到，灰里有几道蹭痕，那处的灰是不久前才被抹掉的，仿佛有人故意把两只手在里面擦拭过——这时他感到一阵分明而极令人满足的战栗。这与那块脏肥皂联系了起来。安德鲁斯对那些蹭痕只字未提。显然是总局的人忽略了它们——漫不经心地扫一眼，看见搁板是空的，便就此罢手了。

瓦尔考中尉开始觉得相当愉快，郑重其事地告诉自己：该来一番推演了。有那么一会儿他愉快地设想了一下，是不是碰上了那种古怪而阴森的东方影响——这种影响在最上乘的凶杀案里总是那样经久不衰地冒头——在卡西迪那些凶杀案里：那个飘忽的身影，裹着灰袍，咖啡色的皮肤上顶着白色的头巾，倾向于某种宗教狂热，其秘传的仪式与尘土有关。这个令人倒吸凉气的恶棍，最终会因他对这尘土的玄秘癖好，被那位聪明的侦探逮住。瓦尔考中尉想要知道，自己眼下要对付的，可是这样一个谜一样的角色？不，他严厉地告诫自己，自己心里明镜似的，压根儿就不是。可是在这么一个现成又漂亮的推演的位置上——取而代之的是什么？

他盯着尘土，开始看见里面浮现出一幅幅图画：一个蹲伏着的人，被仇恨或恐惧、或二者兼有所折磨，知道恩迪科特马上就要拉开衣橱的门。凭那些轻松派幽默作家的名义起誓，该当如何？此人怕被认出来。难道就没有什么伪装吗？没有，该死——可是有的——尘土！这人的双手抹上了灰，再借由这双手，把脸……

===Original===
“Ain’t there nothing I can do for you, Lieutenant?”

Lieutenant Valcour sighed and got down from the chair.

“Yes, Cassidy,” he said. “You can take this chair and put it over by the hall door. Then you can sit down.”

“Very well, Lieutenant,” said Cassidy bitterly. “But when you’re in that cupboard there ain’t nobody in the room with me but that live corpse.”

“Then sit where you can’t see it.”

“Cripes, Lieutenant, I don’t have to see it. I get the chills just thinking about it.”

“You’ll get the gate, Cassidy, if you don’t snap out of it.”

“All right, sir, but if you come out and find me keeled over, don’t blame me.”

“I wouldn’t dream of it, Cassidy.”

===Chinese===
“难道就真没一点我能替您做的事吗，中尉？”

瓦尔考中尉叹了口气，从椅子上下来。

“有的，卡西迪，”他说，“你可以把这张椅子搬过去，搁在通往走廊的那扇门旁边。然后你就可以坐下了。”

“很好，中尉，”卡西迪苦着脸说，“可等您进了那衣橱，这屋里除了我，就只剩那具活尸了。”

“那就坐在看不见它的地方。”

“我的老天，中尉，我压根儿不用看见它。我光想想就浑身发冷。”

“你要是不从这股劲儿里挣出来，卡西迪，你就等着被扫地出门吧。”

“知道了，长官，可要是您出来发现我直挺挺地晕倒在地，别怪我。”

“我做梦都不会怪你，卡西迪。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour reentered the cupboard. He examined the corner in which Endicott had been slumped. The suits on the hangers had fallen back a little into shape. He carefully went through their various pockets. They were empty, and from the rumpled condition of their linings he knew that they had been hastily gone through before. Perhaps the Central Office men had done so, but he doubted it. They would concern themselves pretty exclusively with the effects taken from the clothes Endicott had been wearing at the time of the attack.

It interested him to note that the suits against which Endicott’s body had been slumped showed evidence of having been searched with the rest. It confirmed his theory that that was what the attacker had been doing when caught in the cupboard by Endicott’s sudden appearance in the bedroom, and it also strengthened his theory of the ingenious use of dust from the shelf top as a disguise.

Shoes lined a low shelf along the bottom of one side, and hat boxes occupied a corresponding shelf on the other. Lieutenant Valcour dismissed the possibility that the particular hat he was searching for﻿—the one that Endicott was wearing or intended to get at the moment of the attack﻿—would be in a box. Perhaps it was in the cupboard Mrs. Endicott spoke about downstairs in the entrance hall. The point kept nagging at him irritatingly, and he considered it important enough to go down and find out.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉重新走进衣橱。他检查了恩迪科特先前瘫靠的那个角落。衣架上的那几套西装稍稍恢复了原来的形状。他仔细地翻遍了它们各个口袋。口袋都是空的，从内衬皱巴巴的样子看，他知道这些口袋此前已经被人匆忙翻过一遍。也许是总局的人翻的，但他不大相信。总局的人会相当专心地只去关心恩迪科特遇袭时所穿衣物里取出的那些物件。

他饶有兴味地注意到，恩迪科特尸身所靠着的那几套西装，也有与其余几套一并被搜过的迹象。这印证了他的推断：袭击者被恩迪科特骤然出现在卧室里、堵在衣橱之中时，正在干的正是这件事；这也加强了他另一个推断——把搁板顶上的积灰巧妙地当作伪装。

鞋子一字排开，占满了一侧靠下的一层低搁板，另一侧对应的那层搁板上则放着帽盒。瓦尔考中尉排除了这样一种可能：他要找的那顶特定的帽子——就是恩迪科特遇袭时所戴的，或是当时正打算去拿的那顶——会装在盒子里。它也许就在恩迪科特夫人在楼下门厅里提到过的那个衣橱里。这一点一直恼人地纠缠着他，他觉得它要紧得很，值得下楼去问个明白。

===Original===
Cassidy barely restrained himself from clutching Lieutenant Valcour’s arm by the hall door.

“Honest to God, you ain’t going to leave me in here alone, Lieutenant?”

“Honest to God, Cassidy, I am.”

Lieutenant Valcour went out. Cassidy took one bleak look at his charge, the living corpse, carefully crossed the fingers of both his hands, and sat down.

“I just knew,” he muttered truculently, “that this case was going to be one of them printed damn things.”

===Chinese===
到了通往走廊的那扇门边，卡西迪差点没忍住去抓瓦尔考中尉的胳膊。

“对天发誓，您不是真要把我一个人撂在这屋里吧，中尉？”

“对天发誓，卡西迪，我就是。”

瓦尔考中尉走了出去。卡西迪阴郁地望了一眼他看守的对象——那具活尸——小心翼翼地把两只手的十指都交叉了起来，坐下了。

“我就知道，”他气哼哼地嘟囔着，“这案子准会是那种印在纸上、见鬼的玩意儿。”

## VII 11:01 p.m.—Banked Fires / 七 晚十一时零一分——封炉余烬

===Original===
The corridor was deserted.

Lieutenant Valcour walked along it to the top of the stair well and looked down into the entrance hall. He could see the broad athletic back of Officer O’Brian on guard at the door. O’Brian’s snub nose was pressed against the plate glass, and his eyes, one presumed, were staring out through the door’s bronze grille upon the street.

As Lieutenant Valcour went down he wondered at the complete stillness of the house. There was no sound of any nature at all. There was a waiting quality about the stillness: a definite waiting for something that would shatter the hush into bedlam.

===Chinese===
走廊里空无一人。

瓦尔考中尉沿着它走到楼梯井顶端，朝下望进门厅。他能看见奥布赖恩（O’Brian）警官宽阔结实的背影，那人正在门口站岗。奥布赖恩那只短翘的鼻子贴在平板玻璃上，想来他的两眼正透过门上那青铜栅栏，凝望着外头的街道。

瓦尔考中尉一边下楼，一边诧异于这房子里那种彻底的死寂。没有任何一种声响，什么都没有。这寂静里有一种等待的意味：分明是在等着某种东西——某种会把这片静默劈成一片混乱喧嚣的东西。

===Original===
“What are you pressing your nose against the glass for, O’Brian?” he said.

The young policeman turned and grinned at him broadly.

“Sure, it’s them boys from the papers, sir,” he said. “They’re all stirred up over what the medical examiner has just told them.”

Lieutenant Valcour groaned faintly. “When was this, O’Brian?”

“Not two whisks of a lamb’s tail ago, sir—out there in the vestibule.”

“Did the medical examiner go out into the vestibule?”

“He did that, Lieutenant, and the last mother’s son of them has just beaten it off down the street like a jumping jack rabbit. They were crazy after photographs, but he drew the line at that now.”

===Chinese===
“你把鼻子贴在玻璃上做什么，奥布赖恩？”他说。

那年轻警察转过身来，冲他咧嘴一笑。

“可不是嘛，是报社那帮小子，先生，”他说，“法医刚对他们说了几句，他们就全炸开了锅。”

瓦尔考中尉轻轻呻吟了一声。“什么时候的事，奥布赖恩？”

“也就一眨眼的工夫，先生——就在外头那前厅里。”

“法医到前厅里去了？”

“可不，中尉，他去啦。这帮家伙一个个撒腿就顺着街溜了，跟受惊的兔子似的。他们疯了一样想拍照，可法医这一回在那上头划了道杠，不依了。”

===Original===
“Really?” Lieutenant Valcour was politely astounded.

“Sure and he did—with the exception of a flash or two he let them take of himself.”

“And were you the little birdie, O’Brian?”

“Was I the which, Lieutenant?”

“Did you say ‘peet-tweet’ over his left shoulder as the flashlights went off?”

“Ah, sure now, sir, and I did have the door open a wee bit. I was just explaining to the boys that they couldn’t come in without your permission nohow, and it was then that the medical examiner came along and, hearing the talking, went outside to pacify them.”

“A modern martyr throwing himself to the lions. Except for the tea party, O’Brian, has anything happened down here?”

“Not a thing, sir.”

“Any of the servants been drifting around?”

“Only one old dame in black, and seven foot tall if she’s one inch. She came halfway down the stairs, took one dirty look at me, and then stalked back up as stiff as a poker. Her bonnet was on her head.”

“You don’t know who she was, I suppose?”

“That and I don’t, sir. She looked like she might be a housekeeper.”

“She probably was. By the way, O’Brian, just what was it the medical examiner told the boys?”

===Chinese===
“当真？”瓦尔考中尉彬彬有礼地作出一副惊愕之态。

“千真万确，中尉——他只让他们给自己拍了一两张闪光灯照片，除此之外一概免谈。”

“那你就是那只多嘴的小鸟儿了，奥布赖恩？”

“我是哪只什么，中尉？”

“闪光灯一闪的时候，你是不是在他左肩头那边‘啾啾’叫了两声？”

“噢，先生，瞧您说的，我确实把门开了那么一小道缝儿。我正跟那帮小子解释呢，说没您发话他们无论如何也进不来，就在那会儿法医走了过来，听见说话声，便到外头去安抚他们了。”

“一位舍身喂狮子的现代殉道者。除了这场茶话会之外，奥布赖恩，底下还出过什么事没有？”

“一桩也没有，先生。”

“有仆人在附近晃悠过吗？”

“只有一个穿黑衣的老太婆，七尺高，少一寸都不止。她下楼梯下到一半，恶狠狠地瞪了我一眼，就僵直得跟拨火棍似的，转身又上了楼。她头上戴着一顶软帽。”

“我猜你不知道她是谁？”

“正是不知道，先生。瞧那模样，倒像是这家的管家。”

“多半就是。顺便问一句，奥布赖恩，法医到底跟那帮小子说了什么？”

===Original===
“Lieutenant, I could make neither the head nor the tail out of it. I’d been telling them myself that the boss upstairs was dead and that foul play was suspected, and they were hot after the medical examiner for a further word, and I’m damned if he didn’t give it to them.”

“What was the word, O’Brian?”

“Indeed and it sounded like crinoline, sir—the stuff the missus do be talking about in old dresses.”

“Was that all he said?”

“It was enough, sir. ‘Crinoline,’ said he, and looked very wise at that. Then he added, ‘For the present, boys, no more,’ and off they scampered like the devil in person was after them.”

“All right, O’Brian. Just stick where you are.”

===Chinese===
“中尉，我听得丈二和尚摸不着头脑。我刚自个儿跟他们说过，楼上那位东家已经死了，疑为谋财害命，他们还死缠着法医，非要再讨一句话不可，妈的，他还真就给了他们一句。”

“是哪一句，奥布赖恩？”

“听着像是‘克里诺林’，先生——就是太太们谈老式衣裳时常说的那种料子。”

“他就说了这一句？”

“这一句就够了，先生。‘克里诺林，’他说，那神气煞有介事、深不可测。接着他又添了一句：‘眼下就这些，小伙子们。’于是他们撒腿就跑，活像魔鬼亲自在屁股后头撵着似的。”

“行了，奥布赖恩。你就在原地守着。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour wandered around the entrance hall but encountered, beyond his own and the medical examiner’s, no hat. He knew that Dr. Worth’s was still upstairs where the doctor had left it in Endicott’s bedroom. He found the cupboard Mrs. Endicott had referred to. There was no hat. The subject was becoming a fixed idea. It was growing increasingly believable that the attacker had taken the hat and worn it out of the house. But why should the attacker leave the house? And what was the matter with the attacker’s own hat? Time, if not Endicott himself, would have to tell.

From a reception room opening off the entrance hall he caught the murmur of Dr. Worth’s and the medical examiner’s voices in consultation. He passed the door indifferently and went upstairs.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉在门厅里踱了一圈，可除了他自己和法医的帽子之外，再没撞见第三顶。他知道沃思医生那顶还在楼上——医生把它留在了恩迪科特的卧室里。他找到了恩迪科特夫人提到过的那个衣橱。里面没有帽子。这件事渐渐成了盘踞心头的执念。他越发愿意相信：是凶手拿走了那顶帽子，戴着它走出了这幢房子。可凶手为什么要离开房子？凶手自己的那顶帽子又出了什么问题？这个问题，倘若不是恩迪科特本人，就只能让时间来作答了。

从门厅一侧开门进去的接待室里，他听见沃思医生与法医正低声商议的咕哝。他从那扇门前漠然走过，上楼去了。

===Original===
… an old dame in black, seven foot tall if she was an inch. Her bonnet was on her head.

… and her bonnet, Lieutenant Valcour repeated softly to himself, was on her head.

He continued on up a second flight of stairs to the third floor. A door toward the end of the hall was open, and light flooded out through the doorway. He walked to it and looked in.

A tall, thin woman sat on a chair before a grate in which some coals burned bleakly. She was unbelievably gaunt—her silhouette a pencil, rigidly supporting an austere face beneath a smooth inverted cup of steel gray hair. Black taffeta sheathed her, tightly pressing against flat narrow planes, and smoothly surfacing two pipelike arms that ended in the tapering, sensitive hands of an emotional ascetic.

===Chinese===
……一个穿黑衣的老太婆，七尺高，少一寸都不止。她头上戴着一顶软帽。

……她头上，瓦尔考中尉暗自轻轻重复了一遍，戴着一顶软帽。

他继续登上第二段楼梯，上了三楼。走廊尽头附近一扇门敞着，灯光从门里倾泻而出。他走到门口朝里望去。

一个高高瘦削的女人坐在椅上，面前的炉栅里，几块煤正惨淡地燃着。她瘦得令人难以置信——剪影像一根铅笔，笔挺地撑起一张严峻的脸，脸上一顶钢灰色头发倒扣如光滑的圆杯。黑色塔夫绸紧裹着她，把她那些扁平狭窄的棱角紧紧包住，又平平地覆出两条管子似的手臂，末端是那一双渐细而敏感的手——一个感情丰富的苦行僧才有的那种手。

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour rapped on the door jamb.

The woman did not start. Her head alone turned and faced him, and her eyes were a contradiction of nature—black planets glowing coldly in a sky of white.

“Pardon me, I am Lieutenant Valcour of the police. Are you, by any chance, the housekeeper?”

Her voice was of New England—low almost to huskiness, a trifle harsh, and completely stripped of all nuances.

“Yes, Lieutenant. I am Mrs. Siddons.”

“May I come in? Thank you—please don’t get up. I’ll only stay a minute or two, if you don’t mind.”

He took a chair and placed it before the fireplace beside her own. He sat down and did nothing beyond observing obliquely for a moment the curiously artificial placidity of Mrs. Siddons’s clasped hands.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉叩了叩门框。

那女人并未受惊。只有头转过来，正对着他，她的双眼简直是大自然的一个悖论——漆黑的行星，在一片白色的天穹里冷冷发光。

“打扰了，我是警察局的瓦尔考中尉。您莫非就是这家的管家？”

她的口音是新英格兰那片的——低得近乎沙哑，略带几分粗粝，所有细微的抑扬都被剥得干干净净。

“是的，中尉。我是西登斯太太（Mrs. Siddons）。”

“我可以进来吗？谢谢——请别起身。您要是不介意，我只待一两分钟。”

他搬过一把椅子，放在壁炉前、她自己那把椅子旁边。他坐下，除此之外什么也没做，只是斜着眼端详了片刻，端详西登斯太太那双交握的手上一种古怪而刻意的平静。

===Original===
“There is no use in questioning me, Lieutenant, because I have nothing to say.”

Her tone was the chill clear winds that sweep the rigorous mountains of Vermont.

Lieutenant Valcour warmed his hands before the lazy coals and smiled amiably. “And I,” he said, “have absolutely nothing to ask.”

“That is a lie.”

There was nothing abusive in the remark. It was simply a statement of fact, coldly, dispassionately pronounced by the remarkable pencil dressed in black who spired beside him. Lieutenant Valcour was shocked into a nervous laugh. He discarded his mask of indifference and stared at Mrs. Siddons openly and with complete interest. Not planets, her eyes—rather were they banked fires beneath whose ash hot coals smouldered deeply.

===Chinese===
“盘问我没有用的，中尉，因为我无话可说。”

她的语调，是佛蒙特那严酷群山间席卷而过的清冽寒风。

瓦尔考中尉在那几块慵懒的煤前烤了烤手，和蔼地笑了笑。“而我呢，”他说，“根本没什么要问的。”

“这是谎言。”

这话里没有任何辱骂的意味。它不过是一句事实的陈述，由他身边那位一袭黑衣、笔直如笋的非凡人物冷冷地、不动声色地说了出来。瓦尔考中尉被震得神经质地笑了一声。他索性褪下那副淡漠的面具，公然且兴致勃勃地凝视起西登斯太太来。她的眼睛不是什么行星——倒更像是封了炉的余火，灰烬之下，灼热的煤块正深深入眠。

===Original===
“I shouldn’t wonder,” he said, “but that your forbears came from Salem.”

A look of interest stirred sleepily in the coals.

“Why so, sir?”

“Because there’s a look of witch-burning in your eyes.”

Mrs. Siddons gestured a slow negation.

“I would never abrogate the rights of God.”

“But you would approve, Mrs. Siddons.”

“I would *rejoice*, sir, in the crushing out of any evil or”—her tone became implacably stern—“of any evil thing.”

“Or even of a human being?”

Her look did not waver.

“Yes, Lieutenant—or even of a human being.” She went on steadily and unemotionally. Her words were fragments of stone chipped from some elemental quarry of granitelike conviction and harsh purpose. “That is why you find me dry-eyed, sir, in spite of the tragedy which has been visited upon this house.”

===Chinese===
“我倒也不奇怪，”他说，“您的先辈没准儿是从塞勒姆来的。”

煤火里有一种兴味，慵懒地翻动了一下。

“这话怎么说，先生？”

“因为您眼里有一种烧女巫的神情。”

西登斯太太缓缓做了一个否定的手势。

“我绝不会僭越上帝的权柄。”

“可您会赞同，西登斯太太。”

“我会*欢欣*，先生，为任何邪恶被碾碎”——她的语调变得不容分说的冷峻——“或任何邪物被碾碎。”

“乃至是一个人？”

她的目光纹丝不动。

“是的，中尉——乃至是一个人。”她平静而不动声色地继续说下去。她吐出的字句，是从某种花岗岩般的信念与峻厉意志所构成的原始采石场上，一下下凿下来的碎石片。“正因如此，先生，您才见我眼眶干涸——纵然这宅子里已经降临了一场惨祸。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour felt that there was a catch in it somewhere. If she held Endicott’s condition in the light of a tragedy then she scarcely regarded his death as an act of vengeance on the part of her unquestionably inflexible god.

“Tragedy?” he repeated softly.

“A tragedy, sir, for blinded eyes.”

He hoped that she wasn’t going to be allegorical. He endeavoured to interpret. “It is hard on Mrs. Endicott,” he said.

For a moment he thought she was going to melt. “That poor young thing,” she said, and her voice fringed on unaccustomed softnesses. “That sweet young child of beauty—what a bitter ending for the journey of her tormented heart.”

He stepped delicately out upon the fragile ice. “But she’s really better off, don’t you think?”

“She will never know to the full the fortune of her release.” Mrs. Siddons’s incredibly thin body was suddenly shaken with passion as she added, “From that hateful—that filthy beast.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉觉得这话里某处藏着一个扣儿。倘若她把恩迪科特的处境视为一桩惨祸，那她就不大会把他的死，看作她那位无可争辩地冷峻无情的上帝所施的报应。

“惨祸？”他轻声重复。

“对蒙蔽的眼睛而言，是一桩惨祸，先生。”

他暗自盼着她别往寓言那条路上走。他竭力去解读这话。“这桩事对恩迪科特夫人是够受的。”他说。

有那么一瞬间，他以为她要软下来了。“那可怜的年轻人，”她说，嗓音触到了几缕她素所没有的柔软。“那美貌的、可爱的年轻孩子——她那颗备受折磨的心所走的那趟旅程，竟是这样一个苦涩的结局。”

他小心翼翼地踏上了那层薄冰。“可她其实倒好了些，不是吗？”

“她永远不会充分知道自己这一遭解脱是多大的福分。”西登斯太太那瘦得难以置信的身躯，忽然被一阵激愤撼动，她紧接着说：“从那个可恨的——那个肮脏的畜生手里解脱出来。”

===Original===
“Oh, come, Mrs. Siddons—no man is quite as bad as all that.”

Her eyes blazed with the heat of a strange malevolence. “You didn’t know him, Lieutenant, as we did.”

“‘We,’ Mrs. Siddons?”

“Myself, sir, and the servants under my charge.”

“You found him disagreeable—overbearing?”

Mrs. Siddons stared fixedly at the coals, as if finding in their vibrant reds some adequate illustration of her angered thoughts. “I found him such a man, Lieutenant, that I am glad to know that he is dead.”

“But you see, Mrs. Siddons, he isn’t dead.”

He thought for a minute that she was going to faint and instinctively leaned forward to support her. She stood up unsteadily but refused the offer of his hands.

“If you will pardon me, sir, I believe I will lie down. There has naturally been a certain strain—a—”

She bowed and found her way to a door that led into an inner room. Lieutenant Valcour listened for a moment at its panels after she had closed it.

He could not determine whether the muffled sound he heard was of peculiar laughter or a sob.

===Chinese===
“哎，得了吧，西登斯太太——再坏的人也不至于坏到那步田地。”

她的眼里燃起一种古怪恶意的炽热。“您是不曾了解他的，中尉，不像我们这样了解他。”

“‘我们’，西登斯太太？”

“我本人，先生，还有我手下管着的那些仆人。”

“您是觉得他惹人厌——专横跋扈？”

西登斯太太直勾勾地盯着那几块煤，仿佛要从它们那跳动的红光里，为自己的愠怒寻到某种恰如其分的写照。“我看他就是这样一个人，中尉，所以我一得知他已死了，便觉欣慰。”

“可您瞧，西登斯太太，他并没有死。”

有一刻，他以为她要晕过去，本能地俯身去扶。她摇摇晃晃地站起来，却推开了他伸过来的手。

“请您见谅，先生，我想躺一躺。自然难免有几分紧张——一种——”

她躬了躬身，摸索到一扇通往内室的门，走了进去。她把门关上之后，瓦尔考中尉在门板外驻足倾听了片刻。

他分辨不出自己听见的那声闷响，究竟是一阵古怪的笑，还是一声啜泣。

## VIII 11:28 p.m.—Mrs. Endicott Screams / 八 晚十一时二十八分——恩迪科特夫人尖叫

===Original===
The tangents and the bypaths were beginning to increase. Lieutenant Valcour tabulated them as he went thoughtfully down the stairs and along the corridor toward Endicott’s room: Mrs. Endicott herself, and the Spartan Mrs. Siddons—both had been partially explored; Roberts, with her astonishing glance that had hinted so definitely at revelations. Then what of Marge Myles? And what of the unknown man with whom Mrs. Endicott, that afternoon, had taken tea? He opened the door to Endicott’s room and went in.

Preparations for the operation were practically complete. Dr. Worth and the medical examiner were beside the bed, and hovering near them were two trained nurses in uniform—middle-aged, competent women, starched and abstract looking, moving a bit aloofly in their private world which was so concisely separated from the sphere of laymen.

Cassidy, who seemed bleaker than ever, still stiffly occupied the chair near the doorway. He continued to inspect with an almost feverish interest an unsullied expanse of white ceiling above his head.

===Chinese===
那些横生的枝节与岔路，正变得越来越多。瓦尔考中尉一边若有所思地下楼、沿着走廊走向恩迪科特的房间，一边在心里逐一列出清单：恩迪科特夫人其人，还有那位斯巴达式的西登斯太太——这两个他都只摸到了一点边；罗伯茨，她那惊人的一瞥，分明暗示着某种将要吐露的隐情。那么玛吉·迈尔斯呢？还有那天下午与恩迪科特夫人一同喝茶的那个陌生男人，他又算怎么回事？他推开恩迪科特房间的门，走了进去。

手术的准备工作已基本就绪。沃思医生和法医守在床边，在他们身侧忙前忙后的，是两名身穿制服的训练有素的护士——两个中年、能干的女人，浆洗得挺括，神情抽象疏淡，带着几分超然地走动在她们那个与外行人泾渭分明的小天地里。

卡西迪（Cassidy）神情似乎比先前愈发冷峻，仍僵直地占着门口附近那把椅子。他继续以近乎狂热的兴致，审视着头顶上方那片纤尘不染的白色天花板。

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour seated himself on the corner of a long mahogany chest that was placed before the window farthest from the bed and gravely watched Dr. Worth. He began to feel a little sickish and hoped that he wasn’t going to make an ass of himself and faint. He had witnessed any number of accidents and stabbings, but had never been present at an operation, and it worked on his nerves. Even if Endicott weren’t dead, he certainly looked it. Suspended animation and catalepsy were all right as figures of speech, but the human illustration was rather ghastly. Lieutenant Valcour felt justified in believing that he knew his corpses. He wondered why Dr. Worth was delaying—hesitating—no, bending over now, and in his hand, ready to give the injection of adrenaline into the cardiac muscles, was …

The response was immediate.

With the aid of the stethoscope Dr. Worth heard Endicott’s heart throbbing again, growing steadily stronger. Quite noticeably beneath the bright white lights a faint flush started to run through Endicott’s skin. Lieutenant Valcour saw it, and he moistened with his tongue the dry pressed surface of his lips.

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉在一个长条红木箱子的角上坐下——那箱子摆在离床最远的那扇窗前——他神情凝重地注视着沃思医生。他开始觉得有点犯恶心，只盼自己别出洋相昏过去。形形色色的车祸、刺伤他见过无数，却从未在旁目睹过一场手术，这叫他神经一直受着煎熬。纵然恩迪科特没死，他看上去也确确实实是一副死相。“假死”和“僵直性昏厥”当作修辞来说固然无碍，可落到活人身上作演示，未免有些触目惊心。瓦尔考中尉自忖有充分的理由相信自己还是识得尸体的。他纳闷沃思医生为何拖延——犹豫——不，此刻已经俯下身去了，而在他手中，正准备把肾上腺素注入心肌的，是……

反应立竿见影。

借助于听诊器，沃思医生听见恩迪科特的心脏重新搏动起来，并且一下比一下有力。在那片亮白的灯光下，一缕淡淡的红润开始流过恩迪科特的皮肤，清晰可辨。瓦尔考中尉看在眼里，伸出舌头，润了润自己那干涸紧绷的嘴唇。

===Original===
Dr. Worth straightened up and handed the stethoscope to the medical examiner. “Endicott lives,” he said.

No one had noticed Mrs. Endicott standing in the doorway. No one had even noticed that the door was open. It was her terrific scream, her dropping to the floor, that shocked everyone into instant awareness of her presence. Dr. Worth nodded to one of the nurses. With her aid he lifted Mrs. Endicott and carried her from the room. Everyone else remained quite literally spellbound, still chained within the influence of that extraordinary scream. It didn’t seem more than a second or two before Dr. Worth returned. He went directly to Lieutenant Valcour.

“I have given Mrs. Endicott a narcotic that will keep her quiet for the night,” he said. “It was outrageous—her being here. That guard at the door should have seen to it that it was kept closed.”

===Chinese===
沃思医生直起身，把听诊器递给法医。“恩迪科特活过来了。”他说。

谁也没有发觉恩迪科特夫人就站在门口。甚至谁也没有发觉那扇门是开着的。是她那声骇人的尖叫，是她瘫倒在地，才把在场所有人猛然震醒，意识到她就在那里。沃思医生朝其中一名护士点了点头。在那名护士的协助下，他把恩迪科特夫人抱起，将她抱出了房间。余下众人当真如中了定身法一般，仍被那声非同寻常的尖叫所钳制，动弹不得。仿佛也只过了一两秒钟，沃思医生便回来了。他径直走向瓦尔考中尉。

“我给恩迪科特夫人打了一针镇静剂，能让她这一夜安生。”他说，“她出现在这里——简直岂有此理。门口那个守卫本该盯紧，让这扇门一直关着才是。”

===Original===
“Most outrageous, Dr. Worth. I believe all of us were hypnotized by watching you.”

“And I don’t care what the law is, she can’t be questioned or disturbed in any way at all until I say so.”

“But that *is* the law, Doctor. You are quite within your rights to dictate concerning your patient.”

“I don’t want to dictate. I’m just as willing as anybody to have the criminal side of this mess cleared up, if there is a criminal side.”

“Endicott would hardly have crawled into a cupboard to have a stroke, would he, Doctor?”

“No.” Dr. Worth’s intelligent eyes stared speculatively at Lieutenant Valcour for a minute. “Not unless he’d hidden in there to overhear something, and did overhear something that gave him a stroke,” he said.

===Chinese===
“确实岂有此理，沃思医生。我想我们大伙儿都给您那一手给看入迷了。”

“我不管法律是怎么规定的，只要我还没发话，她就既不能被盘问，也不能以任何方式被打扰。”

“可这恰恰*就是*法律呀，医生。关于您的病人，您完全有权发号施令。”

“我并不想发号施令。这桩烂摊子倘若当真有刑事犯罪的成分，我也和任何人一样巴不得它水落石出。”

“恩迪科特总不致于自己爬进衣橱里去中风吧，您说是吗，医生？”

“不至于。”沃思医生那双聪敏的眼睛，揣度地盯着瓦尔考中尉看了一瞬。“除非他本是藏进去偷听什么，结果真偷听到了什么，才中风的。”他说。

===Original===
The cesspool, Lieutenant Valcour decided, was beginning to show strange depths within its depth. The medical examiner came over and joined them. He complimented Dr. Worth briefly on the success of his operation, assured Lieutenant Valcour that the homicide chief would be given a full report of Endicott’s recovery, and presumed that from now on the case would be left in Lieutenant Valcour’s hands. Lieutenant Valcour would deal with whatever charges of robbery or assault might develop from it. He said goodbye and left the room, with the fullest intention of going right straight home to bed; and so he promptly did, as soon as he had made the promised report to Andrews.

Dr. Worth pointedly raised his eyebrows. “Then there will be charges, Lieutenant?”

“That will depend largely upon Endicott, Doctor. As he is now revived he will tell us himself who attacked him, or the nature of the circumstance that gave him the shock.”

“I trust so.”

“There isn’t any doubt, is there?”

===Chinese===
这潭污水，瓦尔考中尉断定，正在它那深不见底之中，露出几重古怪的层次来。法医走过来，加入了他们。他简短地向沃思医生致意，称道他手术成功，又向瓦尔考中尉保证，凶杀科科长将收到一份关于恩迪科特复苏的完整报告，并设想从今往后这桩案子便交由瓦尔考中尉全权处理。日后若由此生出什么抢劫或人身侵害的指控，概由瓦尔考中尉处置。他说了声告辞便离开了房间，满心打算径直回家睡觉；他也确实这么做了——一俟向安德鲁斯（Andrews）作了所承诺的报告，便立马照办。

沃思医生意味深长地扬了扬眉。“那么说，是要立案起诉了，中尉？”

“这在很大程度上取决于恩迪科特本人，医生。他既已苏醒，自会亲口告诉我们，是谁袭击了他，又或者，是何种性质的变故给了他这一惊。”

“但愿如此。”

“这还有什么可怀疑的吗？”

===Original===
Dr. Worth grew expansive. “Certainly there is a doubt,” he said. “While it is true that Endicott has been revived, it is impossible to state definitely that he will recover consciousness. And even granting that he should recover consciousness, there is also a chance that he might prefer not to make any statement at all. What would you do then, Lieutenant?”

“Fold my tents, Doctor, and fade away.”

Dr. Worth looked down a long straight nose for a minute at tips of low patent-leather shoes. “And if Endicott does not recover consciousness,” he said softly, “what will you do then?”

“You’ll be surprised at the number of things I will do then.”

Dr. Worth’s eyes, surfeited with patent leather, snapped up sharply. “I must impress on you that Mrs. Endicott is not to be disturbed,” he said.

“She won’t be, Doctor.”

===Chinese===
沃思医生来了谈兴。“怀疑自然是有的，”他说，“恩迪科特固然已被救醒，却没法断言他一定能恢复意识。退一步说，就算他恢复了意识，也还有这样的可能——他宁肯不作任何陈述。到那时您又当如何，中尉？”

“那我就卷起帐篷，医生，悄然退场。”

沃思医生顺着一只又长又直的鼻梁，垂眼盯了一会儿自己那双低帮漆皮鞋的鞋尖。“倘若恩迪科特始终未能恢复意识，”他轻声说，“您那时又当如何？”

“到那时我要做的事之多，准叫您大吃一惊。”

沃思医生那双看饱了漆皮的眼睛，倏地锐利地一抬。“我必须向您强调，恩迪科特夫人不得受到任何打扰。”他说。

“她不会被打扰的，医生。”

===Original===
“Nurse Vickers, who helped me into her room with her, is going to stay with Mrs. Endicott all night. Two day nurses will come in the morning: one for her, if necessary, and surely one for Endicott. I need scarcely impress upon you the seriousness of *his* condition.” Dr. Worth made a gesture of irritated bewilderment. “I wish I knew him more intimately—who his friends are, I mean.”

“He never talked with you about them?”

“Never. He seems an unusually reticent man, with an almost abnormally developed feeling for privacy concerning his intimate affairs.” Dr. Worth’s manner grew definitely severe. Mentally, he wagged a finger under Lieutenant Valcour’s nose. “He mustn’t have any further shock. There must be nothing, absolutely nothing that will shock him when, and if, he regains consciousness.” He directed his attention momentarily to the nurse. “Get those shades back on the lamps, please, Miss Murrow, and turn out the ceiling lights. And now, Lieutenant, to continue about Endicott. As she is under the influence of the narcotic I gave her, it is out of the question that his wife be here. I wish she could be. I want the first person he sees to be someone he knows—loves. His mind, you see, will pick up functioning at the precise second where it left off—at least, such is my conclusion.”

“And that was one of shock.”

“Yes, Lieutenant, evidently one of shock or of great fear. We cannot overestimate the importance of getting him past it safely. Personally, I shall sleep here in the house tonight, and Nurse Murrow will call me if Endicott shows any signs of coming to. That may not be before morning. I hope so, in a way, as the effect of the narcotic will have worn off by then, and Mrs. Endicott can be in here with him.”

===Chinese===
“扶我送她回房间的维克斯小姐，今夜会整宿守着恩迪科特夫人。明早会来两名日班护士：一名照看她——如有必要——另一名必定是照看恩迪科特的。*他*病情之严峻，我无须再向您多加强调。”沃思医生做了一个又恼又惑的手势。“我真希望对他能更知根知底些——我是说，他都有哪些朋友。”

“他从未与您谈起过吗？”

“从未。他这人似乎格外寡言，对涉及自己私事的那份隐私感，几乎到了一种近乎反常的发达地步。”沃思医生的语气明显严厉起来。在心里，他朝着瓦尔考中尉的鼻子竖起了警告的手指。“他绝不能再受任何刺激。在他恢复意识的那一刻——倘若他能恢复的话——绝不能有任何事、任何事都绝不能惊动他。”他一时把注意力转向护士。“默罗护士，请把那几盏灯的灯罩重新罩上，把天花板的灯关掉。那么，中尉，我们接着说恩迪科特的事。她既已受我所打镇静剂的影响，他的妻子便不可能守在这里。我倒巴不得她能。我希望他睁眼所见的第一个人，是他认识的——他所爱的。您要知道，他的意识会从他中断的那一秒精确地继续运转——至少，我是这样推断的。”

“而那一秒，正是受了惊吓的一秒。”

“是的，中尉，显然是惊吓，又或者是巨大的恐惧。能否助他平安越过这一关，其重要性怎么估计都不为过。就我个人而言，今夜我会睡在这宅子里，若恩迪科特有任何苏醒的迹象，默罗护士会叫我。那也许要等到早晨了。从某种角度说，我倒希望如此，因为届时镇静剂的药效已经过去，恩迪科特夫人便可进来陪在他身边。”

===Original===
“One of the servants might know of some friend,” Lieutenant Valcour suggested. “I take it you would like a friend to sit here with him during the night?”

Dr. Worth was emphatic. “It is almost a necessity that there should be. The mental and nervous viewpoints, you see, predominate in the case.”

“There is just one thing that I would like to arrange, too, Doctor.”

“Yes?”

“I want to keep a couple of men posted all night in the bathroom. They can sit on chairs just inside the doorway there, where they can watch the bed, but where Endicott can’t see them. He need never know they are there.”

“What on earth would be the need for that?”

===Chinese===
“仆人里也许有人知道他哪位朋友，”瓦尔考中尉提议道，“我想，您是希望夜里能有位朋友在此陪他坐着吧？”

沃思医生语气坚定。“这几乎是必不可少的。您瞧，这一案里，心理与神经的层面才是主导。”

“我也正有一件事想安排一下，医生。”

“什么事？”

“我想在浴室里彻夜派两个人守着。他们可以坐在门里侧的椅子上，从那儿能盯着这张床，可恩迪科特却瞧不见他们。他根本不必知道他们在那里。”

“这究竟所为何需？”

===Original===
“Why, it’s quite simple, Doctor. When Endicott comes to he will be in a position to tell us who gave him the shock—a shock sufficient almost to kill him—one which would have killed him if we hadn’t found him tonight—and if,” he added thoughtfully, “Mrs. Endicott hadn’t had her suspicions.”

“But why the men in the bathroom?”

“Because I don’t want to take any chances of there being a repetition before Endicott makes his statement.”

Dr. Worth pursed his lips and looked very wise indeed. “I see,” he said. “I see. You are afraid that the same person might get at him again and, well, silence him before he could talk.”

“Something like that, Doctor.” Lieutenant Valcour became courteously formal. “As the physician in charge of this case, sir, have you any objection to my stationing the two men in the bathroom?”

“Providing Endicott isn’t able to see them and won’t be disturbed by them in any way at all.”

“Then that’s settled. You’ll have a nurse in here all the time, I suppose?”

“Naturally.”

“Then I’m going to ask her to keep this hall door locked on the inside. She can open it if anyone knocks, and my men will keep their eyes on whoever comes in.”

“The precautions seem extraordinary, Lieutenant.”

“And so does the case. I’ll go downstairs now and try to find out something from the servants about his friends. I’ll tell them, if you like, about your staying here, in case there is anything that has to be got ready.”

“Thank you, Lieutenant.”

“Not at all, Doctor.”

===Chinese===
“怎么，这事再简单不过了，医生。等恩迪科特醒过来，他便能够告诉我们是谁给了他这一惊——一惊之重，几乎要了他的命——若不是今夜我们及时找到他，这一惊便真要了他的命——还有，倘若，”他若有所思地添了一句，“恩迪科特夫人没有起疑的话。”

“可在浴室里派人，又是为何？”

“因为我不想冒任何风险——在恩迪科特作出陈述之前，再发生一次同样的事。”

沃思医生抿了抿嘴，一副煞有介事、深谋远虑的神情。“我明白了，”他说，“我明白了。您是担心，那个人有可能再度接近他，嗯，在他开口之前就让他永远噤了声。”

“差不多就是这个意思，医生。”瓦尔考中尉变得彬彬有礼而公事公办起来。“作为本案的主治医师，先生，您可反对我把这两个人安在浴室里？”

“只要恩迪科特瞧不见他们，也绝不会被他们以任何方式打扰，我便无异议。”

“那就这么定了。您这里会一直有护士守着，我估摸着？”

“那自然。”

“那么我就去请她把这扇通走廊的门从里头锁上。有人敲门她可以开，而我的人则会盯紧每一个进来的人。”

“这些防范未免太过周章了，中尉。”

“这桩案子也未免太过蹊跷了。我这就下楼，设法从仆人那里打听些他朋友的事。您要是愿意，我便顺道把您要留宿的事告诉他们，免得到时候有什么要预备的。”

“有劳您了，中尉。”

“不必客气，医生。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour went outside. He found the maid Jane in the hallway, seated on a chair near the stairs, trembling. A tray with an empty glass was on the floor beside her. She saw him, picked up the tray, and stood up.

“I’m that upset, sir,” she said, “*that* upset.”

“Something has startled you?”

“Startled! Glory be, sir—what with this bringing back of the dead and the missus gone into a coma—if it wasn’t for them three cops at the downstairs doors I’d be out of this house this minute, and so would the rest of us, too.”

“How many of the ‘rest of you’ are there?”

“Sure and including the housekeeper there’s eight of us, sir.”

The Endicotts, Lieutenant Valcour was now quite certain, must be multimillionaires.

“All women?”

“Except for the houseman and chauffeur.”

“And do they sleep in the house?”

“The chauffeur does not, sir. He has an apartment for himself and his wife and his three-year-old child, named Katie, over the garage in East Sixty-sixth Street, sir.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉走了出去。他在走廊里看见女佣简，正坐在楼梯附近的一把椅子上，浑身发抖。她身旁的地上放着一只托盘，上面是一只空玻璃杯。她看见他，端起托盘，站起身来。

“我都吓坏了，先生，”她说，“*吓*坏了。”

“有什么事惊着您了？”

“惊着了！老天爷呀，先生——又是这把死人往活里救，又是太太昏死过去——要不是楼下三道门都有警察守着，我这一刻早就从这宅子里跑出去了，我们一干人也都跑光了。”

“你们这‘一干人’有多少？”

“可不是嘛，连管家算在内，我们一共八个人，先生。”

恩迪科特夫妇，瓦尔考中尉此刻十分笃定，必是家资巨万的大富翁。

“都是女佣？”

“除开男仆和司机，都是。”

“他们都睡在这宅子里吗？”

“司机不住这儿，先生。他和他的太太，还有他三岁的孩子凯蒂（Katie），在东六十六街车库楼上有一套自家的公寓，先生。”

===Original===
“Have all of you been in service here a long time?”

“Indeed and we haven’t, sir—except for Roberts and the housekeeper. I’ve been here a month myself, and the rest of us not more than two or three.”

“And Roberts has been Mrs. Endicott’s maid for the past several years, say?”

“And sure and ever since she landed here from England, sir.”

“Roberts is an Englishwoman?”

“Hold your whisht, sir, and I’ll tell you that she’s of the aristocracy, no less.”

Lieutenant Valcour considered this gravely. It was not improbable. Many English families were utterly wrecked financially by the war, and the children had scattered whither they could, like sparrows, in search of bread. “You’re sure of this?” he said.

“And indeed it is common knowledge, sir. The housekeeper herself, it was, who told me.”

===Chinese===
“你们大家都在这儿做事很久了吗？”

“哪里哪里，先生——除了罗伯茨和管家。我自个儿才来了一个月，其余的人也至多两三个月。”

“那么罗伯茨是恩迪科特夫人的贴身女佣，已有好些年了吧？”

“可不是嘛，自打她从英国来到这儿起，就跟着夫人了，先生。”

“罗伯茨是英国人？”

“您且别声张，先生，我跟您说，她还是贵族出身呢，货真价实。”

瓦尔考中尉郑重地思忖着这话。这倒也并非不可能。战事使许多英国家庭在经济上彻底倾覆，家中的儿女便如麻雀一般，四散到各处去谋一口饭吃。“这话当真？”他说。

“千真万确，先生，这是尽人皆知的事。就是管家她老人家亲口告诉我的。”

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour switched suddenly. “I wonder whether you could tell me who Mr. Endicott’s intimate friends were,” he said.

“Well, sir, there’s quite a few people have called on the madam off and on, and a few on Mr. Endicott, too. I couldn’t say, though, as to just how intimate.”

“But didn’t he ever discuss his friends?”

“Not before me, sir. I’m one of the downstairs girls. Perhaps Roberts would know. She’s often in the room with the madam and Mr. Endicott even when the pair of them is quarrelling that hard that—Glory be to—”

“Tut, tut,” said Lieutenant Valcour gently. “Married couples are always quarrelling together. There’s nothing unusual in that.”

“Indeed and there ain’t.”

“I wonder whether you’d ask Roberts to come out here and see me.”

“I will, sir.”

“Oh—and will you also tell whoever has to know about it that Dr. Worth plans to stay here all night? And then let him know, please, where he is to sleep.”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉陡然一转话头。“不知您能否告诉我，恩迪科特先生都有哪些至交好友？”他说。

“这个嘛，先生，时不时来登门拜访太太的人倒着实不少，来拜访先生的也有几位。不过要说有多亲近，我可就说不上来了。”

“可他难道从不谈起自己的朋友？”

“当着我的面可没有，先生。我是楼下做事的女佣。也许罗伯茨会晓得。她时常在太太和先生所在的房里伺候，哪怕这两口子吵得再凶再——老天爷——”

“得啦，得啦，”瓦尔考中尉温和地说，“做夫妻的，哪有不拌嘴的。这算不得什么稀奇。”

“可不是，先生，算不得稀奇。”

“麻烦您去请罗伯茨出来一趟，见一见我，可好？”

“好的，先生。”

“噢——还有，麻烦您也告诉一声该知道的人，沃思医生打算在此留宿一整夜？然后再告诉他，他夜里睡在哪里。”

“是，先生。”

===Original===
Jane went to the door of Mrs. Endicott’s room and knocked. Nurse Vickers opened it and stepped halfway out, blocking the entrance. Their voices were too low for Lieutenant Valcour to hear, but he saw the nurse retreat into the room, caught an affirmative nod from Jane, and presently Roberts came out and toward him.

“You wished to see me, Lieutenant?”

There was still that curious shielding in her eyes—a hinting at definite information kept closely guarded behind twin gates.

“I want you to tell me,” he said quietly, “why you compelled me a while ago in Mrs. Endicott’s room to say ‘Later.’”

“I don’t believe I quite understand.”

“And I believe that you do.”

Roberts became coolly detached. “One is justified in having one’s beliefs.”

“Just why do you hate Mrs. Endicott so?”

She flinched as if he had struck her physically.

“Is that why you sent for me?” she said.

Lieutenant Valcour himself indulged in a veiling of eyes. “I wish,” he said, “that you would sit down.”

===Chinese===
简走到恩迪科特夫人房门前，敲了敲门。维克斯小姐把门拉开，侧身出来半步，挡住了入口。她们说话的声音压得太低，瓦尔考中尉听不见，但他看见那护士退回房中，又瞥见简点头应了一声，不一会儿，罗伯茨便走了出来，朝他走来。

“您要见我，中尉？”

她眼中仍带着那种古怪的戒备——分明在暗示，确凿的消息被紧紧护在两扇门后。

“我要您告诉我，”他平静地说，“方才在恩迪科特夫人房里，您为什么迫着我答了一句‘回头再说’。”

“我怕是没太听懂您的意思。”

“而我却相信，您是听得懂的。”

罗伯茨变得冷淡而疏离起来。“人总归有权利存着自己的揣测。”

“您究竟为何这般恨恩迪科特夫人？”

她瑟缩了一下，仿佛挨了他实实在在一记耳光。

“您就是为了这个才叫我来？”她说。

瓦尔考中尉自己也索性把目光遮了起来。“我希望，”他说，“您能坐下。”

## IX 11:55 p.m.—Queer Deeps / 九 晚十一时五十五分——诡谲深渊

===Original===
Roberts went indifferently to the chair that Jane had been using and sat down. Lieutenant Valcour drew another up beside her. He began with the usual distant skirmishing before launching the main body of his attack.

“I will explain why I wanted to see you,” he said. “It’s concerning Mr. Endicott—concerning his condition.” He noted the sudden reflex from tension on the part of her hands as he summed up concisely the statement made to him by Dr. Worth. “I understand,” he concluded, “that Mrs. Endicott is under the influence of a narcotic and will not be available before tomorrow morning at the earliest. Dr. Worth naturally wants to prevent all risk, and so we’ve turned to you.”

He felt her staring through him, as if by some fourth-dimensional process his being had been erased from her vision.

“Mr. Endicott has very few friends,” she said.

===Chinese===
罗伯茨（Roberts）走到简方才坐过的那把椅子前，神色淡漠地坐了下来。瓦尔考中尉在她身旁另拽过一把椅子坐下。他先照例从远处的试探开始周旋，然后才会发起正面的攻势。

“我来解释一下为什么要见您，”他说，“是关于恩迪科特先生的事——关于他的状况。”他把沃思医生（Dr. Worth）对他陈述的那番话扼要复述了一遍，留意到她的双手蓦地一松，那是一种由紧张而起的下意识反应。“我听说，”他收尾道，“恩迪科特夫人正受药物镇静作用的影响，最早也要到明天早上才能答话。沃思医生自然不愿冒任何风险，所以我们就转而来找您了。”

他感到她的目光径直穿透了他，仿佛凭借某种第四维度的法术，他这个人的存在已从她的视野里被抹去了。

“恩迪科特先生的朋友很少。”她说。

===Original===
“You are taking the word at its literal meaning.”

“Oh, quite. His acquaintances are numerous and transient.” She focused him into an entity again. “They are mostly women. I don’t suppose one of them would do?”

Lieutenant Valcour smiled slightly. “Not if their status is so uncertain—their emotional status, I mean.”

“Exactly.” The masked effect of her attitude remained unchanged as she asked with almost perfunctory detachment, “Would a man do?”

“Why not?”

“Because there is one man of whom Mr. Endicott speaks quite frequently as being his ‘best’ friend.”

===Chinese===
“您是把这个词照字面意思来理解的。”

“哦，一点不错。他相识的人多如过江之鲫，却又都是萍水之交。”她重新把他焦距成一个实体。“他们大多是女人。我想其中哪一位怕是不成吧？”

瓦尔考中尉微微一笑。“倘若她们的身份这般含糊——我是说，她们在感情上的身份——那是不成的。”

“正是如此。”她那副戴着面具般的态度丝毫未变，近乎敷衍地淡然问了一句：“换成一个男人成吗？”

“有何不可？”

“因为确有这么一个男人，恩迪科特先生常常提起，称他是自己‘最要好的’朋友。”

===Original===
“Here in town?”

“In a bachelor apartment on East Fifty-second Street.”

“You have his exact address?”

“It is in the memorandum book beside the telephone in Mrs. Endicott’s room.”

Lieutenant Valcour grew markedly casual. “A mutual friend, then?”

“One couldn’t say.”

“He is your only suggestion?”

“He is the only man to whom I have heard Mr. Endicott refer in terms of friendship and of intimacy.”

===Chinese===
“就在本城？”

“在东五十二街的一处单身公寓里。”

“您有他的确切地址吗？”

“在恩迪科特夫人房里电话机旁的那本备忘录上。”

瓦尔考中尉的神态变得分外松弛起来。“那么，是位共同的朋友咯？”

“这可说不好。”

“他是您所能想到的惟一人选？”

“他是我所听闻恩迪科特先生惟一以友情和亲密口吻提及过的男子。”

===Original===
“Then there really isn’t any choice.”

Roberts’ smile signified nothing. “No choice.”

“Have you ever seen this man?”

“His name is Mr. Thomas Hollander. I have never seen him.”

“Has anyone in the household ever seen him, to your knowledge?”

“I dare say. I don’t know. One could inquire.”

Lieutenant Valcour recognized the rising inflection at each period mark, a habit so much in vogue among certain types of English people when they wish to be mildly disagreeable. He felt a Gallic insistence to retaliate even at the expense of chivalry. At the worst, he thought, he would only be living up to the popular conception of the men in his profession. And there *was* some link of peculiar intimacy between this woman and Endicott. …

===Chinese===
“那就实在是别无选择了。”

罗伯茨那一笑，意味不着边际。“别无选择。”

“您可见过此人？”

“他名叫托马斯·霍兰德（Thomas Hollander）先生。我从未见过他。”

“据您所知，这宅子里有谁见过他吗？”

“我猜有吧。我说不准。倒是可以打听打听。”

瓦尔考中尉辨认出她每一句话末尾那个上扬的语调——这是某类英国人想要摆出几分不痛不快的别扭姿态时颇为时兴的做派。他感到一种高卢式的冲动在催促他还以颜色，哪怕有失风度也在所不惜。他想，往最坏处说，他这也不过是恰合世人对他这一行当里男人的成见罢了。况且，这个女人和恩迪科特之间，确乎有某种特殊亲密的牵连。……

===Original===
“If we cannot get hold of Mr. Hollander,” he said, “would you consider it advisable for the post to be taken by yourself?”

He repented instantly at the sight of her deadly whiteness. It seemed impossible that blood could drain so swiftly from the skin. His own face blazed like fire from the slap of her hand across his cheek. He noticed, as he sat very still, the strange terror that hid beneath her bitter, staring eyes: it wasn’t any terror of the law, the cheek of which she had symbolically in his person just so vigorously slapped; it wasn’t any terror of what he or the machine he represented could do to her—what anyone or anything could do to her. … It was baffling; baffling as the undiscoverable source of any intense emotional reaction is baffling—something that drew its sustenance from roots imbedded not in the immediate present but in the past. …

===Chinese===
“倘若我们寻不着霍兰德先生，”他说，“您可会觉得，由您自己来坐这个位子是妥当之举？”

一见到她那死人般的惨白，他立时便懊悔了。血色竟能如此迅疾地从皮肤底下褪尽，仿佛全无可能。他自己的一张脸却被她那一巴掌掴得如火灼烧。他端坐不动，留意到她那双苦涩而直瞪瞪的眼睛底下，藏着一股古怪的恐惧：那不是对律法的恐惧——这一点方才已由她借他这个人作象征、如此用力地掴在了脸颊上；也不是对他、或他所代表的那部机器能把她怎样的恐惧——不是对任何一个人、任何一桩事能把她怎样的恐惧。……它叫人摸不着头脑；就像任何一种无从追根的情绪反应的源头那样叫人摸不着头脑——那是一种养分，而它的根并不扎在眼下的此刻，而是扎在过去。……

===Original===
“You will permit me to offer my apologies?” he said.

She returned vividly to the moment, and her colour swept back in a succession of bright waves.

“I am not usually so unmannerly,” she said.

“Nor usually subjected to insult. The fault was mine.”

Her laugh was quite harsh. “On the contrary, Lieutenant, I am accustomed to insult.”

“Then why do you stay with Mrs. Endicott?” he said softly.

“Because there are some people, Lieutenant, who can only find their happiness in hell.”

===Chinese===
“可否容我向您致歉？”他说。

她一下子鲜活地回到了此刻，脸上血色如一波接一波明亮的潮水般涌了回来。

“我平日里并不这般失礼。”她说。

“您平日里也不至于受人这般侮辱。过错在我。”

她那一笑相当冷厉。“恰恰相反，中尉，受辱于我是常事。”

“那您为何还要留在恩迪科特夫人身边？”他轻声说。

“因为有一种人，中尉，他们只能在炼狱里寻得自己的幸福。”

===Original===
“Martyrs.”

“Not martyrs, precisely.”

“Just what, then, precisely?”

“It’s a sharing, if you wish—sort of a sharing of torture.”

Vague—vague. Lieutenant Valcour felt quite convinced that he would shortly begin to gibber, if the mysteries of hearts, of minds that he had dipped into during the past few hours, did not soon coalesce within the mould of reason. He began to envy his sterner compatriots on the force who confined their processes to the comfortable fields of hard, cold facts—the “did you at five-forty-five this afternoon place the silver teaspoon on the pantry shelf, or did you not?” sort of facts. He conceded that their wholesome, plein-air tactics were quite right, and that his own, in spite of their usually successful results, were hopelessly wrong. They at least were never called liars, or slapped in the face, or found themselves helplessly swirling in a sea of metaphysics with a splendid chance of being thoroughly drowned. He forced himself to concentrate. What was it that slash of pale lips had been saying? A sharing of something … Of course, of torture.

===Chinese===
“殉道者。”

“准确地说，算不得殉道者。”

“那究竟是什么，准确地说？”

“是一种分担，假如您愿意这么说的话——某种对折磨的分担。”

含糊——含糊。瓦尔考中尉深信不疑：倘若这几个钟头里他所窥探到的那些心、那些脑中的谜团，还不赶紧在理性的模子里凝结成形的话，他眼见着就要开始胡言乱语了。他开始羡慕警队里那些更硬派的同胞，他们把自己的办案手段局限在冷硬事实那片舒坦的田野里——“今天下午五点四十五分，究竟是不是你把那只银茶匙搁到了餐具室的搁板上？”那一类的事实。他承认，他们那套有益身心、风清气爽的户外法子才是对的，而他自己的法子，纵然常常奏效，却是无可救药地错了。他们至少不会被人骂作骗子，不会挨耳光，也不至于无助地在一汪玄学的汪洋里打着旋儿，眼看着要被彻底淹没。他强自收摄心神。那两片苍白的嘴唇方才在说什么来着？是对某种东西的分担……对了，是对折磨的分担。

===Original===
“You mean,” he said, “a sharing that is now going on?”

“Perhaps—but especially in the past. Do you believe, Lieutenant, that the dead remain in emotional touch with the living?”

“And that, my poor fish,” he told himself severely, “is what your interminable probing into people’s souls has got you into.”

“I have never thought about it. But I should like to believe that it is true. I should like to believe in anything that offers corroborative proof of immortality.”

“You are convinced of the finality of death?”

“It is a dread, not a conviction.”

===Chinese===
“您的意思是，”他说，“一种当下仍在进行着的分担？”

“也许是吧——但尤其是在过去。中尉，您可相信，死去的人与活着的人在情感上仍彼此相通？”

“瞧瞧，”他严厉地暗自斥责自己，“你这没完没了地朝人灵魂深处探头探脑的下场，可不就是这样。”

“我从未想过这事。可我倒愿意相信这是真的。我倒愿意相信任何能印证灵魂不死的旁证。”

“您确信死亡便是终局吗？”

“那是恐惧，不是确信。”

===Original===
Roberts nodded her head swiftly. “And with me—with me—if I could only *know*.”

“So that you would be quite certain that your sacrifice is not being made in vain.” Lieutenant Valcour spoke very softly. He was approaching, he felt, no matter how grandiloquently, that goal toward which he had been aiming: the answer to the amazing look she had given him in Mrs. Endicott’s room.

The mood broke. She stood up abruptly.

“You wished that address book?” she said.

It was of no great matter. Moods, at least, did not die. They were always there—somewhere—waiting to be recaptured.

“If you will be so kind,” he said.

===Chinese===
罗伯茨飞快地点了点头。“而于我——于我——只要能*知道*就好了。”

“好让您确信，您这份牺牲不至白费。”瓦尔考中尉说得极轻。他觉得自己正在逼近——无论措辞多么夸诞——他一直瞄准的那个目标：她在恩迪科特夫人房间里投给他的那一眼，那个令人费解的眼神背后的答案。

那点情绪倏然中断。她蓦地站了起来。

“您不是要那本地址簿吗？”她说。

这倒不打紧。至少，情绪是不会消亡的。它们总在那里——在某处——等着被重新拾起。

“有劳了。”他说。

===Original===
She went to the door of Mrs. Endicott’s room, opened it, was swallowed up. Lieutenant Valcour waited outside. The case was becoming mired in evasions. That was the trouble with cases whose milieu rose beyond a certain social and mental level. They invariably grew kaleidoscopic with overtones. Crime in the lower strata was noteworthy for its crudenesses rather than its subtleties: an intrigue among animals, with the general patentness of some jackal filching its prey. But breeding and intellect generally presupposed masks: the inbred defensiveness of manner and social combativeness with the world which offered barriers most difficult to pierce. Roberts opened the door and handed him the small leather reference book Mrs. Endicott had used when verifying the telephone number of Dr. Worth.

“Thomas Hollander,” she said. “The names are listed alphabetically.”

The door closed even in that short second which preceded his thanks. It was a gesture of retreat from hinted intimacies. It wasn’t so much the door of the room she had closed as it was the door guarding her secrets. He felt that she wanted to show him she had already repented of having gone so far—not that she *had* gone any distance, really, but there were beacons, faint pin points of light toward which he would chart a course over the surface of her troubled seas.

===Chinese===
她走到恩迪科特夫人房门前，推开门，整个人被吞了进去。瓦尔考中尉在外面候着。这桩案子正陷进躲闪迂回的泥沼里。但凡案子所涉的圈子在社会地位和心智上越过了某一层，麻烦就在这里。它们总会染上万花筒般的弦外之音。下层社会的罪案，惹人注目的是其粗陋而非其精巧：一场禽兽之间的勾当，透着那种豺狗窃取猎物时一览无余的直白。可教养与智识，大体上便预设了面具：那与生俱来的举止上的戒备、以及与这世界周旋时的社交上的好斗，筑成了最难刺穿的壁垒。罗伯茨打开门，把恩迪科特夫人先前核实沃思医生电话号码时所翻过的那本小皮面备查簿递给了他。

“托马斯·霍兰德，”她说，“人名是按字母顺序排列的。”

门关上了，就在他道谢之前那短短一瞬。这是一个从被暗示的亲密里退守的姿态。她关上的，与其说是那间房的门，毋宁说是守着她秘密的门。他觉得她是想让他看见：她已然后悔自己走得这样远了——其实她并未真走出多远，但总有几盏信标，几点针尖般微弱的光，他可以循之在她那动荡海面上标出一条航路来。

===Original===
He took the reference book and sat down. He began with *A* and started to go systematically through it. At *H* he fixed in his memory the street and telephone number of Hollander’s house. He continued without interest to turn the pages.

At the end of the *M*’s he came, to his marked bewilderment, upon the address and telephone number of Marge Myles.

===Chinese===
他接过那本备查簿，坐了下来。他从 *A* 起，开始有条不紊地翻检。翻到 *H* 时，他把霍兰德住所的街址和电话号码记进了脑中。他兴味索然地继续往下翻。

翻到 *M* 字头末尾，他竟——且是大为错愕地——撞见了玛吉·迈尔斯（Marge Myles）的地址和电话号码。

## X 12:06 a.m.—The Stillness of a Grave / 十 凌晨十二时零六分——坟墓般的寂静

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour went to the head of the stairs.

“O’Brian!” he called down.

O’Brian looked up at him from below.

“Yes, Lieutenant?”

“Send Hansen up here, please.”

“Yes, sir.”

A painting on the wall held Lieutenant Valcour’s attention while he waited. A Gauguin, he thought, and, going closer, confirmed it. His eye drifted over the entire corridor. Everywhere were the details of great wealth, and the young owner of it all not a happy child of kind fortune, but a detested, a passionately hated, and a passionately loved man. There flashed again before him in brief review Mrs. Endicott, a storehouse of mountain storms in summer; Mrs. Siddons, spiritual ash; Roberts, the shortest step this side of some fervour bred in the swamps of lunacy; Hollander—Marge Myles—who knew? And would one ever know? Suppose, as Dr. Worth had more than hinted, Endicott should refuse to speak—if that strange reticence harped upon so insistently both by his wife and his physician should resist…

===Chinese===
瓦尔考中尉走到楼梯口。

“奥布赖恩（O’Brian）！”他朝楼下喊道。

奥布赖恩从楼下仰起头看他。

“什么事，中尉？”

“请派汉森（Hansen）上来。”

“是，先生。”

等待的当儿，墙上的一幅画吸住了瓦尔考中尉的目光。他以为是高更（Gauguin）的手笔，走近一验看，果然不错。他的目光飘过整条走廊。触目皆是巨额财富的细节，而这一切的年轻主人，却并非命运垂青的幸福之子，而是一个为人深恶痛绝、又为人狂热痴爱的人物。他眼前再度一幕幕闪过那几张脸——恩迪科特夫人，夏日山中风暴的聚藏所；西登斯太太（Mrs. Siddons），灵性的灰烬；罗伯茨（Roberts），距那在疯狂沼泽里滋生出的某种狂热，仅一步之遥；霍兰德（Hollander）——玛吉·迈尔斯（Marge Myles）——谁知道呢？而人究竟会不会知道呢？倘若正如沃思医生（Dr. Worth）不止一次暗示过的那样，恩迪科特竟不肯开口——倘若他那妻子和医生都如此执拗地反复提及的那种古怪缄默，竟会顽抗到底……

===Original===
“Lieutenant, sir, Officer Hansen reporting.”

Lieutenant Valcour dragged his eyes from the Gauguin unwillingly.

“All right, Hansen,” he said. “Come with me.”

They went down the corridor and stopped before the door to Endicott’s room.

“Do you know what’s gone on here tonight, Hansen?”

“From what I’ve heard, sir, the man who was thought dead is now alive.”

“That is correct.”

Lieutenant Valcour opened the door and beckoned to Cassidy. Cassidy came out and joined them.

===Chinese===
“中尉先生，警员汉森前来报到。”

瓦尔考中尉恋恋不舍地把目光从那幅高更上移开。

“好，汉森，”他说，“跟我来。”

两人沿走廊走去，在恩迪科特房间门前停下。

“你知道今夜这儿出了什么事吗，汉森？”

“据我所闻，先生，那个原以为已经死了的人，眼下还活着。”

“不错。”

瓦尔考中尉拉开门，朝卡西迪（Cassidy）招了招手。卡西迪从房里出来，与他们会合。

===Original===
“When you two men go back into that room,” Lieutenant Valcour said, “I want you to get a couple of chairs and sit down just inside the bathroom doorway. Put the chairs where you can watch the bed and this hall door. If you talk, use a low voice that won’t disturb either the patient or the nurse, and from the moment when she indicates that he’s returning to consciousness, say nothing at all and sit still. The shock of knowing that you were there might disturb his heart again. Is that clear?”

They assured him, in unison, that it was.

“This hall door,” Lieutenant Valcour went on, “is going to be kept locked on the inside by the nurse. Every time she opens it, watch carefully. Keep your eye on anyone who comes into the room, especially if they offer some excuse for wanting to be there—and when I say ‘anyone,’ I mean just that. For instance: the nurse might want some coffee and ring for a servant. Watch that servant every second, until she goes and the door is locked again. While on the subject of coffee, you will drink none that may be offered you while you’re on watch.”

“I never drink coffee, Lieutenant,” said Cassidy. “Now if it was a cup of tea—”

“If you get thirsty,” said Lieutenant Valcour severely, “take some water from the tap. And eat nothing at all. I don’t want to have to come back here and find you both groggy with knockout drops and with heaven-knows-what happened to Endicott. Mind you, I’m not suggesting that anything like this will happen—but it might. Clear?”

Again, in unison, they assured him it was all most clear.

===Chinese===
“你们两个回到房里去以后，”瓦尔考中尉说，“去搬两把椅子来，在浴室门口里侧坐下。椅子要摆在能同时盯着床和这扇朝走廊门的位置。若要说话，压低嗓门，别惊扰了病人，也别惊扰了护士；从她示意他正要恢复知觉那一刻起，你们就再别吭一声，老老实实坐着不动。他若猛地察觉你们在场，那惊吓兴许又要扰了他的心脏。听清楚了吗？”

两人异口同声地向他保证：听清楚了。

“这扇朝走廊的门，”瓦尔考中尉接着说，“将由护士从里头反锁。她每开一次门，你们都要留神细看。盯紧每一个走进房里的人，尤其要是有人找借口想待在里头——我说的‘每一个人’，就是字面那个意思。比方说：护士也许想喝点咖啡，按铃唤一个仆人。在那仆人离开、房门重又锁上之前，每一秒都要盯住她。既然说到咖啡——你们值守期间，无论谁递给你们的咖啡，一口都不许喝。”

“我向来不喝咖啡的，中尉，”卡西迪说，“可若换作一杯茶嘛——”

“你们要是渴了，”瓦尔考中尉严厉地说，“就拧开水龙头喝点水。什么东西都别进口。我可不想回头再赶到这里，发现你俩被蒙汗药放倒了，而恩迪科特又出了天晓得的岔子。说一句，我可不是说这种事当真会发生——但它有可能发生。清楚了吗？”

两人又异口同声地保证，全都清清楚楚。

===Original===
“Keep in mind,” Lieutenant Valcour went on, “that primarily you are in a sickroom over which Dr. Worth has absolute charge. You are not to interfere with anything he may do, or with any arrangements he may make during the night. You are only to step in if you see that Endicott’s life is threatened through the action of some person who may approach him. Try to prevent this by physically overpowering the attacker if you can, but if there is no time for that do not hesitate to shoot.”

“Even if it’s a woman, Lieutenant?” said Hansen quietly.

Lieutenant Valcour shrugged. “There are no such things,” he said evenly, “as sex or chivalry in murder.”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
“还要记住，”瓦尔考中尉继续道，“首先，你们置身的这间是病房，沃思医生是这里绝对的主事之人。他无论做什么、夜间无论做怎样的安排，你们都不得干预。你们只在一件事上插手：一旦你们察觉，有某人逼近恩迪科特、欲以行动危及他的性命。能凭气力制服袭击者便尽量制服；但若来不及，便不要犹豫——开枪。”

“哪怕是个女人，中尉？”汉森低声道。

瓦尔考中尉耸了耸肩。“在谋杀这桩事上，”他平静地说，“既无所谓性别，也谈不上什么骑士风度。”

“是，先生。”

===Original===
“I am painting, incidentally, the darkest prospect of the picture. In all probability nothing will happen at all. You’ll spend a sleepless and tiresome night, get cricks in your necks, and damn the day you ever joined the force. Now, then, there is one thing more, and that concerns a man by the name of Thomas Hollander. Dr. Worth believes it advisable that an intimate friend of Endicott be near him and be the first person whom Endicott sees when he recovers consciousness. Mr. Hollander is that friend. I am going to try to get in touch with him shortly, explain matters to him, and get him to come up here. Mr. Hollander is naturally the exception to my previous instructions. Let him alone. Don’t interfere with him, but—” Lieutenant Valcour’s pause was significantly impressive “—watch him. Watch him, my good young men, as two harmonious cats might watch a promenading and nearsighted mouse. Shall I repeat?”

“I get you, Lieutenant,” said Cassidy. And Hansen, he was assured, had “got” him, too.

“Then we will go in, and you will establish yourselves for the night at once.”

===Chinese===
“我这儿，顺带说一句，是把情形往最坏处描。多半什么事都不会有。你们只会熬一个无眠的、乏味的通宵，脖颈落枕，嘴里咒骂当初自己哪根筋搭错、偏要来当警察。那么，还有一桩事，涉及一位托马斯·霍兰德先生（Mr. Thomas Hollander）。沃思医生认为，恩迪科特身边最好有一位至交在场，并成为他恢复知觉时睁眼所见的第一个人。霍兰德先生便是这位朋友。我打算这就设法联系上他，向他说明原委，请他过来一趟。对于我刚才那番吩咐，霍兰德先生自然是例外。别去招惹他，不要干预他，可是——”瓦尔考中尉这一停顿意味深长，“——盯着他。盯着他，我这两位好小伙子，就像两只相安无事的猫，盯着一只出来散步又近视得厉害的老鼠。要我再重复一遍吗？”

“我懂您的意思了，中尉。”卡西迪说。至于汉森，他大可放心，也同样“懂”了。

“那我们就进去，你们就此各就各位，守上一夜。”

===Original===
He opened the door, and they went inside. Dr. Worth’s arrangements were complete, and he was ready to turn in. Nurse Murrow had received her instructions and was to call Dr. Worth should Endicott show any symptoms of returning consciousness.

Dr. Worth joined Lieutenant Valcour at the door.

“There is nothing further we can do for the present, Lieutenant, except wait,” he said.

“All right, Doctor. I’ve told my men how things stand.” He nodded toward Cassidy and Hansen, who, on tiptoe, were vanishing into the bathroom with two chairs. “I’ve told them you’re in charge here, and that there’s not to be an unnecessary sound or move out of them.”

===Chinese===
他推开门，几人走了进去。沃思医生的安排已一切就绪，他自己也准备歇下了。默罗护士（Nurse Murrow）已领过吩咐，一旦恩迪科特有任何恢复知觉的征兆，便去叫沃思医生。

沃思医生走到门口，与瓦尔考中尉会合。

“眼下我们所能做的，中尉，除了等候，再没有别的了。”他说。

“好的，大夫。我已经把手下人交代清楚了。”他朝卡西迪和汉森点了点头——两人正踮着脚，搬着两把椅子消失在浴室里。“我已告诉他们，这里由您主事，他们不得发出一丝多余的声响，也不得有一丝多余的动作。”

===Original===
Dr. Worth continued to remain politely incredulous. “Well, I dare say you know what you are doing, but it still seems an extraordinary precaution to me.”

“And it probably is. I spoke to one of the maids about your staying here, Doctor.”

“Yes—thank you. They’ve told me where my room is. It’s the one directly above this one.”

“I’ve also lined up one of Endicott’s friends. I’m getting in touch with him directly, and when he comes I’ll have him sent up to you. You can tell him just what you want him to do, and then see that he gets in here all right, if you will, please.”

“By all means. Who is he, Lieutenant?”

“A Mr. Thomas Hollander—lives on East Fifty-second Street.”

“Never heard of him; but there’s no reason why I should have.” He sped a parting look toward Endicott, faintly breathing on the bed. “The most reticent man, Lieutenant, whom I have ever met.”

===Chinese===
沃思医生仍是一副礼貌地将信将疑的神气。“好吧，我敢说您自有分寸，可这番布置在我看来，仍属非同寻常。”

“多半如此。关于您今夜留宿这儿的事，大夫，我已吩咐过一名女佣了。”

“是——多谢。她们已告诉我我的房间在哪里。就在这间房的正上方。”

“我还约好了恩迪科特的一位朋友。我这就去联系他，待他到了，我会让人领他上去见您。您尽可把他该做的事交代给他，然后劳驾看着他顺顺当当进这间房，可好？”

“义不容辞。他是谁，中尉？”

“一位托马斯·霍兰德先生——住在东五十二街（East Fifty-second Street）。”

“从未听说过；不过也没有什么道理我就该认识他。”他朝床上气息微弱的恩迪科特投去临别的一眼。“中尉，他是我平生所见最沉默寡言的人。”

===Original===
They went outside and closed the door.

Nurse Murrow went over and locked it. She felt, to put it mildly, not a little atwitter. Her life had not conformed to the popular version of a trained nurse’s. There had been no romantic patients in it whose pallid, interesting brows she had smoothly divorced from fever by a gentle pass or two with magnetic fingers. No grateful millionaire had offered her his heart and name; nor had any motherly eyed old dowager died and willed her a fortune. No. There had been, on the other hand, a good many years of sloppy, disillusioning, grilling work, long hours spent in pampering peevish patients, patients who were ugly with that special ugliness which is inherent in the sick, snappish doctors, and a perfect desert of romance.

The present case loomed as a heaven-sent oasis. Who knew what might not develop out of it? It awakened all the atrophied hunger of her starved sentimentalism. And even if nothing *did* result from it—nothing practical, like marriage, or a good bonus—it would at least leave her something to think about during those endless, tiresome, tiring hours of the future.…

===Chinese===
他们走到外面，关上了门。

默罗护士走过去，把它锁上。说轻一点，她心里颇有些忐忑不安。她这一生，并不符合世人心目中训练有素的护士那一套版本。她不曾邂逅过什么风流多情的病人，能用她那带着磁性的纤指轻轻一两抚，便把那苍白而有趣的前额从高烧中顺顺当当分离出来。没有哪位感恩戴德的百万富翁向她献上过真心与姓氏；也没有哪位目光慈祥的老太夫人临终把一笔财产遗赠给她。都没有。反之，她度过了漫长的岁月，尽是些拖沓、令人幻灭、煎熬难捱的活计，漫长时间都花在伺候性情乖戾的病人身上——那些病人带着病人与生俱来的那种特有的丑陋——还有动辄发火的医生，以及一片堪称浪漫的荒漠。

眼下的这桩案子，俨然是上天赐下的一片绿洲。谁说得上它能演变出什么名堂来？它唤醒了她那饥渴已久的感伤情怀中所有萎缩了的食欲。退一步说，就算它当真毫无结果——没有什么实在的结果，譬如婚姻，或一笔可观的奖金——至少也能给她留下点念想，供她在往后那些无尽、乏味、令人疲倦的时辰里咀嚼。……

===Original===
She crossed to the bed and looked down at Endicott. She felt his pulse and made a notation on her night chart. She lingered near the bathroom doorway.

“The strangest case,” she whispered, “that I’ve ever been on.”

Cassidy looked up at her bleakly.

Hansen said, “Yes, ma’am.”

“I dare say,” she whispered on, “that it’s quite in the ordinary run of things for you gentlemen.”

“Yes, ma’am.”

“There’s an atmosphere—a something sinister—”

“Yes, ma’am.”

Nurse Murrow’s broad shoulders jerked impatiently. There was a talk-chilling quality in being so determinedly ma’am’d. She gave it up, and settled herself starchly in an armchair. She adjusted a lamp so that it shaded more efficiently her eyes.

===Chinese===
她走到床边，俯身看着恩迪科特。她试了试他的脉搏，在夜间记录单上记下一笔。她在浴室门口附近流连不去。

“我护理过的病人里，”她悄声道，“这可算最离奇的一桩了。”

卡西迪神色淡然地抬头看了她一眼。

汉森说：“是，夫人。”

“我敢说，”她接着低语，“对你们两位先生而言，这不过是稀松平常的事。”

“是，夫人。”

“这儿有一种气氛——一股阴森之气——”

“是，夫人。”

默罗护士宽阔的肩膀不耐烦地耸动了一下。被人这般斩钉截铁地一声声“夫人”着，颇有一种叫人噤若寒蝉的意味。她死了这条心，浆洗得笔挺地在一张扶手椅里坐定。她调了调台灯，好让灯罩更有效地遮住她的眼睛。

===Original===
A floor board creaked upstairs—once.

That would be Dr. Worth, she decided, going to bed. What a man! What a shining light in his profession! A little bigoted, perhaps, in some things, but so distinguished—admirable—a bachelor, too—But what nonsense!

A complete stillness settled gently on the house. The stillness of a grave.

Yes, she thought, just exactly that—the stillness of a grave.…

## XI 12:15 a.m.—To Watch by Night / 十一 凌晨十二时十五分——守夜

===Original===
Lieutenant Valcour refreshed his memory from the leather reference book and then dialled the number.

“Mr. Thomas Hollander?” he said, when a man’s voice answered him. It was a smooth, soft voice, and he suspected that further words beyond the initial “hello” would reveal a Southern accent.
